★AAA — priority and how to decode a twisted question

Decoding a long case (app-written method)

  1. Read the LAST line first. Underline the ask verb: comment / advise / state the responsibility / what should the auditor report / which clause / is it misconduct. The story is often about something else.
  2. Name the actor. Whose duty is asked — statutory auditor, component auditor, internal auditor, management, TCWG, ICAI member in practice / in service? The same fact has different answers for each.
  3. Find the trigger word → standard. e.g. "subsequent to the year end" → SA 560; "uncertainty about continuing" → SA 570; "matter of most significance" → SA 701; "fundamental to understanding, already disclosed" → SA 706 EOM; "not disclosed, relevant to users' understanding of the audit" → OMP; "fee based on outcome" → Clause 10 Part I First Schedule; "client money" → Clause 10 Part I Second Schedule.
  4. Look for the twist fact — date (before / after report), listed vs unlisted, amount vs threshold, member in practice vs service, written vs oral, "disclosed adequately" vs "not disclosed".
  5. Write in 3 blocks: Provision (SA / section / clause with para) → Facts applied → Conclusion (one line, decisive). Marks sit in the provision keywords and the conclusion.

MCQ technique (app-written)

  • Read the question stem and the four options BEFORE the case; then read the case looking only for the facts the options need.
  • "NOT / EXCEPT / least" — circle it. Combination options: eliminate one wrong statement first; it usually removes two options.
  • Numerical MCQs (thresholds, days, %) — the option that differs only in a number is usually the trap: check the exact SM limit.
  • Budget: about 2.5 min per MCQ, 40 min for 15. If a case runs long, answer the short standalone MCQs first.
Descriptive answer frame: Provision (exact SA para / section / clause) → Facts → Analysis (apply each fact to each condition) → Conclusion ("Hence the auditor should…" / "Hence CA X is guilty of professional misconduct under Clause… of Part… of the … Schedule").
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Code of Ethics

19Ch 19 — Professional Ethics & Liabilities of Auditors

Asked: Sep 2025 (MCQ CS-I Q4, Q14, Q15; Q1(b) 5m; Q2(b) 5m; Q5(c) 4m; Q6(c) 4m) · Jan 2026 (MCQ CS-I Q5, CS-II Q9, Q15; Q2(a) 5m; Q3(b) 5m; Q4(b) 5m; Q5(c) 4m) · May 2026 (MCQ CS-II Q9, CS-III Q11; Q2(b) 5m; Q3(b) 5m; Q5(c) 4m; Q6(c) 4m). About 20% of the paper. Every attempt has had 4–5 descriptive parts plus 2–3 MCQs from here.

Answer frame for every ethics question (ICAI style): (1) quote the clause word for word in short form: "As per Clause (x) of Part y of the z Schedule to the CA Act, 1949, a CA in practice shall be deemed guilty of professional misconduct if he…"; (2) give the Council decision, guideline or regulation that applies; (3) apply it to the facts in 2–3 lines; (4) Conclusion: "guilty / not guilty under Clause …". A correct conclusion with the wrong clause loses most of the marks.

2. Code of Ethics: structure

PartCoversApplies to
Part 1Complying with the Code, fundamental principles, conceptual frameworkAll professional accountants
Part 2Professional accountants in service (commerce, industry, service, public sector, education, not-for-profit, regulatory or professional bodies)PAs in service. Also applies to PAs in public practice for work they do as employees of the firm.
Part 3Professional accountants in public practicePAs in public practice providing professional services
Part 4AIndependence: audit and review engagementsWhen doing audit or review. "Audit engagement" in 4A covers review too (Glossary).
Part 4BIndependence: assurance engagements other than audit and reviewOther assurance work
  • Each section has three layers: Introduction, Requirements (the obligations) and Application material (context, explanations, examples).
  • Where law stops an accountant from complying with part of the Code, the law prevails and he must comply with all other parts.

2.1 Fundamental principles (Subsections 111–115)

PrincipleMust-write lineTrigger words in the question
Integrity (111)Straightforward and honest; fair dealing and truthfulness. Must not knowingly be associated with information that is (a) materially false or misleading, (b) provided negligently, or (c) omits or obscures required information. A modified report means no breach. If already associated, take steps to disassociate."signs a report he knows is misleading", "omits information"
Objectivity (112)Judgment not compromised by bias, conflict of interest or undue influence. Must not undertake an activity if a circumstance or relationship unduly influences his judgment."relative", "pressure", "conflict of interest"
Professional competence and due care (113)(a) Attain and maintain knowledge and skill; (b) act diligently, meaning carefully, thoroughly and on time. Staff under him need training and supervision. Make users aware of the limitations of the service."no experience in the area", "delayed", "untrained staff"
Confidentiality (114)Be alert to inadvertent disclosure (including socially, to family or a close business associate); keep information confidential within the firm and for prospective clients; do not use it for personal or third-party advantage; the duty continues after the relationship ends; staff and advisers must respect it."seminar", "presentation", "new employer", "bank asks auditor"
Professional behaviour (115)Comply with laws and avoid conduct that might discredit the profession (judged by a reasonable and informed third party). In promotion: no exaggerated claims, no disparaging references or unsubstantiated comparisons, nothing against the Advertisement Guidelines. If in doubt about an advertisement, consult the Ethical Standards Board."advertises", "claims to be the best", "compares"
  • Confidentiality: when disclosure is or may be appropriate. (a) Required by law: producing documents or evidence in legal proceedings; disclosing infringements of law to the appropriate public authorities. (b) Permitted by law and authorised by the client or employer. (c) Professional duty or right, when not prohibited by law: (i) Peer Review or Quality Review of the Institute; (ii) an inquiry or investigation by a professional or regulatory body; (iii) protecting his professional interests in legal proceedings; (iv) complying with technical and professional standards, including ethics requirements.
  • Points to consider before disclosing (4): whether any party, including third parties, could be harmed even if the client consents; whether all relevant information is known and substantiated, as far as practicable; the type of communication and to whom it is addressed; whether the recipients are appropriate.
  • When principles conflict: he may consult others in the firm or employing organisation, those charged with governance, the Institute or legal counsel. Consulting does not remove his duty to resolve the conflict himself or, unless law prohibits it, to disassociate from the matter. He is encouraged to document the issue, the discussions, the decisions and the reasons for them.

Examiner's twist: TYK 26 asks you to "enumerate circumstances of disclosure and points to consider". These are two separate lists of 3 (with 4 sub-points under (c)) and 4. Students mix them up. A further trap: Peer Review is a professional duty or right, not "required by law".

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Threats, NOCLAR

2.2 Threats, evaluation and safeguards (conceptual framework)

  • Approach in three steps: Identify threats, Evaluate them, Address them by eliminating them or reducing them to an acceptable level.
  • Acceptable level: the level at which a professional accountant applying the reasonable and informed third party test would likely conclude that he complies with the fundamental principles. That third party need not be an accountant, but has the knowledge and experience to judge the accountant's conclusions impartially.
  • Addressing threats (3 ways): (i) eliminate the circumstances, interests or relationships causing them; (ii) apply safeguards, where available and capable of being applied; (iii) decline or end the activity. Some threats can only be addressed by (iii).
ThreatMeaning (key words)SM examples: public practiceSM examples: in service
Self-interestA financial or other interest inappropriately influences judgmentDirect financial interest in a client; fee quoted so low the work cannot meet standards; close business relationship; access to confidential information usable for gain; finding a significant error in the firm's earlier workLoan or guarantee from the employer; incentive pay; personal use of corporate assets; gift from a supplier
Self-reviewWill not properly evaluate an earlier judgment or activity (his own or the firm's) that he now relies onAssurance report on financial systems he implemented; prepared the original data that is the subject matterDeciding the accounting for a business combination after doing the feasibility study for it
AdvocacyPromotes the client's or employer's position until objectivity is lostPromoting a client's shares; advocate in litigation; lobbying for legislation for a clientChance to manipulate a prospectus to get favourable financing
FamiliarityLong or close relationship makes him too sympathetic or too acceptingClose or immediate family member is a director or officer of the client; a former engagement partner joins the client; long association of a team memberFamily member at the employer makes decisions affecting reporting; long association with decision makers
IntimidationDeterred by actual or perceived pressure or undue influenceThreat of dismissal over a disagreement; pressure because the client has more expertise; promotion withheld unless he agrees; significant gift and threat to make it publicThreat of dismissal or replacement over an accounting principle or reporting; influence over contract awards
  • Safeguard examples (SM): extra time and qualified staff → self-interest; a reviewer who was not on the team → self-review; separate partners and teams with separate reporting lines for non-assurance services → self-review, advocacy, familiarity; another firm performs or re-performs part of the work → all five threats; separate teams for confidential matters → self-interest.

Examiner's twist: the significant gift example sits under intimidation (threat of making it public), not self-interest. A gift from a supplier to a PA in service is self-interest. "Client has more expertise" is intimidation, not familiarity. The safeguard that covers all five threats is "involving another firm to perform or re-perform part of the engagement".

2.3 NOCLAR Asked: Sep 2025 Q2(b)(i) and (ii)

  • Non-compliance: acts of omission or commission, intentional or unintentional, contrary to prevailing laws or regulations, committed by the client or employer, TCWG, management, or others working for or under their direction.
  • Not covered: personal misconduct unrelated to the business activities; non-compliance by parties outside this list; clearly inconsequential matters.
  • IESBA examples: fraud, corruption and bribery; money laundering, terrorist financing and proceeds of crime; securities markets and trading; banking and financial products; data protection; environmental protection; public health and safety; tax and pension liabilities.
  • The PA is not required to investigate or ensure complete compliance; he is not expected to know law beyond what the engagement needs; whether there is non-compliance is ultimately for a court to decide; disclosure contrary to law is not required (it is precluded).
  • Objective: turning a blind eye is not an appropriate response, with renewed emphasis on the roles of management and TCWG.
Applicability in IndiaWhoKey condition
Section 260 (PAs in service)Senior professional accountants in service: directors, officers or senior employees who exert significant influence over the organisation's resources, i.e. key managerial personnelEmployees of listed entities
Section 360 (PAs in public practice)Audit engagementsEntities listed on a recognised stock exchange in India and with net worth of ₹250 crore or more. "Audit" here means a reasonable assurance engagement giving a true and fair opinion.

Steps in responding: obtain an understanding of the matter → address the matter → seek advice → decide whether further action is needed → decide whether to disclose to an appropriate authority → imminent breach → documentation. The appropriate authority depends on the matter, for example SEBI for fraudulent financial reporting.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · NOCLAR, membership
PointSA 250NOCLAR (Code of Ethics)
Applies toAudit only, not other assurance workPAs in service and in practice
Laws coveredLaws with a direct effect on amounts and disclosures (tax, labour) and other laws fundamental to operationsSame, plus non-compliance causing substantial harm with serious financial or non-financial consequences
StakeholdersNot definedInvestors, creditors, employees and the general public
Imminent breachNo such provisionAfter considering whether to discuss with management or TCWG, he uses judgment on whether to disclose immediately to an appropriate authority. If he does, the disclosure is permitted.
  • Additional NOCLAR documentation (over and above the SAs) Asked: Sep 2025 Q2(b)(ii): (1) how management or TCWG responded; (2) the courses of action considered, the judgments and the decisions, applying the reasonable and informed third party test; (3) how he is satisfied that he has met his responsibility to act in the public interest.
  • For contrast, SA documentation covers: significant matters, conclusions and judgments; discussions of significant matters with management, TCWG and others (what, when, with whom); and identified or suspected non-compliance with the results of discussions.
  • CA Act confidentiality alignment: in practice → Cl (1) Part I Second Schedule; in service → Cl (2) Part II Second Schedule.

Examiner's twist: the story describes a CFO of an unlisted company (or the audit of a listed company with net worth ₹200 crore) and asks "is NOCLAR applicable?". The answer is no for Indian applicability: Section 260 needs a listed employer, and Section 360 needs listed and ₹250 crore net worth. The score line: quote the two sections with their conditions, then apply them. A junior accountant (not KMP) of a listed company does not meet the "senior professional accountant" condition of Section 260.

3. Membership (Sections 19, 8, 5, 20, 24)

  • S.19 Register: full name, date of birth, domicile, residential and professional address; date of entry; qualifications; whether he holds a COP; other prescribed particulars.
  • S.8 disabilities (cannot be entered or borne on the Register): (i) under 21 years at application; (ii) of unsound mind, so adjudged by a court; (iii) undischarged insolvent; (iv) a discharged insolvent who has not obtained a court certificate that the insolvency was caused by misfortune without misconduct; (v) convicted (in or outside India) of an offence involving moral turpitude punishable with imprisonment, or of a non-technical offence in his professional capacity, unless pardoned or the Central Government has removed the disability by written order; (vi) removed from membership after being found guilty of misconduct. Removed for a period → no re-entry until it expires. Failure to disclose a disability is professional misconduct.
  • S.5 Associate and Fellow: ACA on entry. FCA: (i) an associate in continuous practice in India for at least 5 years; or (ii) an associate for a continuous 5 years with qualifications the Council prescribes as equivalent to 5 years' practice.
  • S.20 removal by the Council: dead; on his request; prescribed fee not paid; subject to a S.8 disability (then or later). The Council shall remove a member under a removal order made under the Act. Reg 19 restoration (for non-payment of fee): on application with arrears of annual fee, entrance fee and additional fee.
Restoration of membership: situationEffective from
Application and fees made within the same year as removalDate of removal (no break)
Removed under orders of the Board of Discipline, Disciplinary Committee, Appellate Authority or High CourtAs per those orders
Other casesDate the application and fee are received
  • S.24 penalty for a non-member claiming membership or using the designation CA, or a member without a COP claiming to be in practice: first conviction a fine up to ₹1,000; any subsequent conviction imprisonment up to 6 months or a fine up to ₹5,000, or both (Prem Batra, 1989).
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · CA in practice

4.1–4.2 Certificate of Practice (S.6, Reg 10, Reg 11)

  • S.6: no member may practise in India or elsewhere without a COP. The annual fee is payable on or before 1 April. The Council may cancel a COP in prescribed circumstances.
  • A member not in practice cannot take engagements of types normally done by a CA, even if no special qualification is needed. A suspended member cannot appear before tax authorities "in some other capacity": he can practise only as a member of the Institute (Illus 1: guilty).
  • Reg 10 cancellation: name removed from the Register; COP issued on incorrect, misleading or false information, or by mistake (after a hearing); ceased to practise; annual COP fee not paid by 30 September of the year. A cancelled COP is surrendered to the Secretary.
  • Reg 11 restoration (cancelled for non-payment): restored from the date of cancellation if a complete application with the fee reaches the Secretary before the end of the relevant year.

4.3 Deemed to be in practice (S.2(2)) Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(c)

  • A member is deemed in practice when, individually or in partnership with CAs in practice (or prescribed professionals), for remuneration, he: (i) engages in the practice of accountancy; (ii) offers to perform or performs auditing or verification of financial transactions, books or records, or prepares, verifies or certifies financial statements, or holds himself out to the public as an accountant; (iii) renders services on accounting procedure or on recording, presenting or certifying financial facts; (iv) renders other services that, in the Council's opinion, a CA in practice may render.
  • Explanation: a salaried employee of a CA in practice or a CA firm is deemed in practice only for training articled assistants.
  • Setting up an office that offers accounting services is "practice" even before any client is served. Service with the armed forces also counts as being in practice.
  • Reg 191 read with S.2(2)(iv): deemed in practice when acting in his professional capacity (not personal, not as an employee) as liquidator, trustee, executor, administrator, arbitrator, receiver, adviser or representative for costing, financial or tax matters, on appointment by the Central or a State Government, a court or a legal authority, or as Secretary, unless employed on a salary-cum-full-time basis.
Management Consultancy and other Services (Council resolution under S.2(2)(iv))Excluded or not permitted
Financial management planning; capital structure and raising finance; working capital; project reports (tax implications of these four count as MCS); budgets; inventory; market research; pricing; MIS; personnel recruitment and selection (includes training programmes, job evaluation); incentive plans; management and operational audits; valuation of shares and business, M&A advice, Registered Valuer under CA 2013 (388th meeting); corporate planning; EDP and systems; advisor or consultant to an issue (drafting the prospectus, listing formalities, publicity budget, advising on selection of underwriters and agencies, post-issue work); investment counselling in securities; registrar to an issue; quality, environment and energy audit; recovery consultant in banking; insurance advisory including brokerage under IRDA; Insolvency Professional (362nd meeting); administrative services (clerical tasks such as word processing, statutory forms, monitoring filing dates; for example, the GST practitioner functions under Rule 83(8))MCS does not include statutory or periodical audit, tax representation or advice, or acting as liquidator, trustee, executor, administrator, arbitrator or receiver (these are separate). Activities of broking, underwriting and portfolio management are not permitted.
  • S.144 CA 2013: the company auditor may give only services approved by the Board or audit committee, and never (directly or indirectly, to the company, its holding or its subsidiary): accounting and book-keeping; internal audit; design and implementation of any financial information system; actuarial; investment advisory; investment banking; outsourced financial services; management services; other prescribed services.

Examiner's twist: "advisor to the issue: advising on selection of underwriters" is permitted; "underwriting the issue" or "managing clients' portfolios" is not (Sep 2025 Q5(c); TYK 1 PMS: guilty). For a Registered Valuer under the Companies Act, the SM answer (TYK 31) is that the rules qualify a CA to value securities or financial assets only, not plant and machinery, even if he is an engineer. Doing the course is generally permitted ("attending classes and appearing for any examination").

4.4–4.6 S.25, S.7, S.27

  • S.25: no company (Indian or foreign) shall practise as CAs. "Company" includes an LLP with a company as partner. Each director or officer knowingly party to it: fine up to ₹1,000 on first conviction, ₹5,000 on subsequent. Under S.141(2) CA 2013, only partners who are CAs may sign for a firm or LLP auditor.
  • S.7: a member in practice uses no designation other than "Chartered Accountant". He may add membership of another accountancy institute recognised by the Council, or any other qualification he has. The prefix "CA" is allowed for all members. ACMA or FCMA letters are allowed, but not the designation "Cost Accountant". Not allowed: Director, member of a political party or club positions; "Income-tax Consultant", "Company Secretary", "Management Consultant", "Corporate Lawyer", the initials "CPA". Foreign institutes recognised through an MoU or MRA (for example SAICA, CPA Ireland, ICAEW) may be mentioned. Category IV Merchant Banker or advisor to an issue: name under the caption "Advisor/Consultant to the Issue", not prominently.
  • S.27 branch offices: each office must be in the separate charge of a member. Being in charge means actively associated: residing at, or attending, the office for at least 182 days a year. The member in charge must be a partner or a whole-time paid assistant.
  • One member may hold charge of a second office if it is in the same premises, in the same city, or within 50 km of the municipal limits of the city of the first office. He must declare which one is the main office (his professional address).
  • Hill-area exemption: a temporary office in the plains for up to 3 months a year; inform the Institute before every winter and on closure (by registered post); the temporary office is not shown on stationery; the name board is shown only during that period.
  • A name board at the residence is allowed only for an individual member, not for the firm (Illus 3: guilty; the 80 km distance is irrelevant).

4.7 KYC norms Asked: May 2026 Q3(b)

  • Issued by the Council of ICAI; mandatory; apply to all assignments of attestation functions.
ClientA. General informationB. EngagementC. Regulatory information
Individual or proprietorName; PAN or Aadhaar; business description; copy of last audited FSType of engagementNone
Corporate entityName and address; business description; name of parent company (if a subsidiary); copy of last audited FSType of engagementCompany PAN; CIN; directors' names and addresses; DINs
Non-corporate entityName and address; copy of PAN; business description; partners' names and addresses (with PAN, Aadhaar or DIN); copy of last audited FSType of engagementNone

Examiner's twist: TYK 15 tells a story about money laundering and a consultant who says "I only did consultancy". The SM answer lists the corporate KYC information. Trap in MCQs: "name of the parent company" is general information, not regulatory. Company PAN is regulatory; PAN of an individual is general.

6. Disciplinary procedure (S.21, 21A, 21B, 21C, 22A, 22G)

PointBoard of DisciplineDisciplinary Committee
When the Director (Discipline) refers the matterPrima facie misconduct under the First ScheduleUnder the Second Schedule or both Schedules
Penalty if found guiltyReprimand; removal of name for up to 3 months; fine up to ₹1,00,000Reprimand; removal permanently or for any period it thinks fit; fine up to ₹5,00,000
AppealMember or Director (Discipline) → Appellate Authority within 90 days. It may confirm, modify or set aside the order; impose, set aside, reduce or enhance the penalty; remit the case for reconsideration; or pass any order it thinks fit.
  • If the Director (Discipline) finds the member not guilty, the Board of Discipline may agree and close the matter, or disagree: it may advise further investigation, proceed itself (First Schedule) or refer to the Disciplinary Committee (Second Schedule or both).

7–8. Types of misconduct and the Schedules map

  • Professional misconduct: Parts I, II, III of the First Schedule and Parts I, II of the Second Schedule. Other misconduct: Part IV of the First Schedule and Part III of the Second Schedule. Other misconduct covers acts outside professional work (for example, forging a relative's will).
  • Part I of the First Schedule covers acts that generally compromise the member's position as an independent person.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · First Schedule Part I, Cl 1–5

8.1 First Schedule, Part I: CA in practice

Cl (1) Allows any person to practise in his name

  • Allowed only if that person is also a CA in practice and his partner or employee. It protects the public from unqualified people practising behind a CA (Illus 5: brother-in-law not allowed; employee CA allowed).

Cl (2) Shares fees, commission or brokerage with non-members

  • Sharing is permitted only with: a member of the Institute; a partner; a retired partner; the legal representative of a deceased partner; members of prescribed professional bodies; or persons with prescribed qualifications. "Partner" includes a partner abroad, where the partnership does not breach Cl (4).
  • Reg 53A(1) bodies: ICSI, ICAI (Cost), Bar Council of India, Indian Institute of Architects, Institute of Actuaries of India. Reg 53A(3) qualified persons: CS; Cost Accountant; Actuary; BE; B.Tech; B.Arch; LLB; MBA (from a university or an AICTE-recognised institution).
  • Substance over name: 50% of audit fees paid to a non-CA as "office allowance" for years means profits were shared → guilty (TYK 4). 1% of profits to an articled clerk over and above stipend → guilty (TYK 5).
  • No bar where a State Government deducts a percentage of the audit fee of a co-operative society to recover administrative costs.
  • Goodwill of a proprietary firm may be sold after the proprietor's death to another eligible member. For deaths on or after 30.8.1998, the sale must be completed within one year of death, and the firm name is kept in abeyance for one year. If there is a dispute about legal heirs, it must be reported within a year, and the name is kept in abeyance until one year after the dispute is settled. Instalment payments are allowed if the sale agreement provides for them. The legal heir of a deceased partner receives a share only if the partnership deed provides for it (Illus 6: the widow may sell; the buyer may practise in that name).

Cl (3) Accepts part of the profits of a non-member's professional work

  • The mirror image of Cl (2), on the receiving side. Proviso: profit sharing, commission or brokerage with prescribed professionals (Reg 53A) is allowed. Referral fees between members in practice are not prohibited.

Cl (4) Partnership with persons other than CAs in practice

  • Reg 53B bodies: CS, Cost Accountant, Advocate, Engineer (Institution of Engineers or degree), Architect, Actuary. However, multi-disciplinary partnerships cannot be formed until the other professions' regulators also permit partnership with CAs and the Council issues guidelines.

Cl (5) Secures work through non-employees or means not open to a CA

  • Work must come through reputation, not through agents or canvassing. Letters to Army Canteen officers giving his experience and fee norms → guilty under Cl (5) and (6) (Jethanand Sharda v Deepak Mehta, 1998). The acts of partners and employees in securing work are covered by Cl (6) and (7).
Look-alike: money flowsDirectionClauseTrigger words
CA in practice pays a share of his fees to a non-memberCA → outsiderFirst Sch Pt I Cl (2)"office allowance", "% of fees to the introducer", "share of profit to the articled clerk"
CA in practice receives part of a non-member's professional profitsOutsider → CAFirst Sch Pt I Cl (3)"commission from a non-CA consultant"
CA gets work through a non-employee agentWork in through an agentFirst Sch Pt I Cl (5)"agent brings clients", "relies on a broker"
Member in service pays a share of his salary to anyone (to procure or retain the job)Employee → outsiderFirst Sch Pt II Cl (1)"pays 10% of salary to whoever got him the job"
Member in service accepts a commission from the employer's lawyer, CA, broker, agent or customerOutsider → employeeFirst Sch Pt II Cl (2) (even if he holds a COP; whole-time or part-time)"referral fee from the lawyer" (Illus 25)
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Cl (6) solicitation

Cl (6) Solicits clients or work by circular, advertisement, personal communication, interview or any other means Asked: Sep 2025 Q6(c); Jan 2026 Q4(b)

  • Provisos (not solicitation): (i) applying to, requesting or securing work from another CA in practice (Illus 10: Mr Honest not guilty); (ii) responding to tenders or enquiries and securing work as a result.
  • Tender guideline (No. 1-CA(7)/03/2016, 7 April 2016; also Chapter XIII of the CGG): do not respond to a tender in areas exclusively reserved for CAs (audit, attestation) unless the tender itself prescribes a minimum fee (commensurate with size, value, volume, manpower and nature of work), or the area is open to other professionals too. A maximum fee only does not lift the bar. Reasonable EMD or deposit is fine; for exorbitant EMD the ESB may look into cases one by one. Maintain a cost sheet (persons, hours) that the Institute may call for.
  • Empanelment: if he knows a panel exists, he may write asking to be included and may quote fees on enquiry. Roving enquiries to have his name included are not proper. Writing repeatedly to the Registrar asking for allotment → guilty (TYK 8(b)). GeM portal registration is allowed: it is run by the client itself, so it is neither empanelment nor an aggregator. Follow the tender guidelines (TYK 30).
ActivityPermitted?Condition (SM)
Press announcementYes, limitedChanges in partnership, dissolution, address or telephone: bare facts; consider area of circulation and number of insertions

Examiner's twist: a long story about a firm's anniversary dinner, CSR camp and an ICAI seminar ends with "advise on each". Score lines: an ICAI PoU event with CPE approval → may sponsor as a firm; CSR → "CA [name]" only, no firm name or logo; any other event → firm cannot sponsor. In a tender, the words "maximum fee" or "fee not to exceed" mean the bar applies; only "minimum fee prescribed" or "open to all categories" lifts it (TYK 21: e-TDS tender open to all → can respond).

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Cl (7) advertising and designations

Cl (7) Advertises professional attainments or uses another designation Asked: May 2026 Q2(b)

  • Prohibits (a) advertising professional attainments or services, and (b) using any designation other than CA on professional documents, visiting cards, letterheads or sign boards, unless it is a university degree (established by law in India or recognised by the Central Government) or a title showing membership of ICAI or another institution recognised by the Central Government or the Council. Proviso: may advertise through a write-up under Council guidelines.
  • Allowed: "Insolvency Professional" and "Registered Valuer" if empanelled (Illus 14); names of all firms in which he is a partner on his personal letterhead (Illus 12); "CA" prefix; ACMA or FCMA letters; recognised foreign institutes.
  • Not allowed: Tax Consultant (Illus 11), Cost Accountant (TYK 9), Management Expert (TYK 10), Corporate Lawyer, Member of Parliament or Municipal Councillor with CA, the date of setting up practice or establishment of the firm on letterheads.
  • Practice as an advocate (with Bar Council permission): never use "CA" and "Advocate" together. Practice as CS or Cost Accountant: never use those designations with "CA". A member holding a COP of a sister institute is treated as in full-time practice.
  • Firm name with "Chartered Accountants" only on professional documents, visiting cards, letterheads and sign boards. Reports and certificates on the firm's letterhead.
  • Media: may appear on TV, films, internet and radio as a CA, with relevant special qualifications; the firm name may be mentioned; no exaggerated claims or comparisons; must not be promotional. Talks and lectures: describe himself as a CA, with no reference to his firm.
  • Sign board: size at his discretion; no glow signs or lights on large boards like traders.
  • CA director in a company's prospectus or announcements: "Chartered Accountant" allowed; no descriptions of expertise or specialisation and no adjectives; other directorships may be given; not the name of his CA firm. Ask management to get the material approved by him (Illus 13: guilty under Cl (6) and (7)).
  • Logo: logos and monograms of firms are prohibited on all media. Only the common CA India logo (2023: blue "CA", tricolour upside-down tick, white background, "India"; do not alter or rotate; one-year transition for existing stationery).
  • Networks: may advertise within the Advertisement Guidelines; constituent firms may print "Network Firms" on stationery.
Look-alikeCl (6) Part I First SchCl (7) Part I First Sch
Core actSoliciting clients or work: asking for workAdvertising attainments or services, or using an unauthorised designation: projecting himself
Medium in the wordsCircular, advertisement, personal communication, interview, any other meansProfessional documents, visiting cards, letterheads, sign boards

Trigger words → which one"requested the audience to approach his firm", "distributed cards", "letters to Registrar", "tagged 30 traders""Tax Consultant on visiting card", "AI Professional tagline", "award mentioned", "client turnover listed"
  • Both clauses often apply together (Illus 13, TYK 10). False information while doing either → also First Sch Pt III Cl (3).
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Cl (8), (9)

Cl (8) Accepts a position as auditor previously held by another CA (or a restricted-certificate auditor) without first communicating with him in writing Asked: Jan 2026 Q2(a)

  • Purpose: not just courtesy. It lets the incoming auditor know the reasons for the change, to protect his own interest, the public interest and the independence of the existing accountant. It is not meant to obstruct the change. The client may change auditors whenever it chooses.
  • Professional reasons for not accepting: (a) non-compliance with S.139 and 140 CA 2013 (Cl 9); (b) non-payment of undisputed audit fees for statutory audit under CA 2013 or other statutes (except sick units); (c) issue of a qualified report. Accepting in (a) or (b) = misconduct. In (c) he may accept if he is satisfied the retiring auditor's attitude was not proper and justified; if the qualification was for good reasons, he should refuse. Ascertain the full facts first.
  • A dispute about fees is not a valid professional reason to decline. For other (non-audit) dues, use your influence to get them settled.
  • Undisputed audit fee = the provision for audit fee in accounts signed by both the auditee and the auditor, plus other audit-related expenses. Sick unit = registered for at least 5 years, with accumulated losses at the end of any financial year equal to or exceeding its entire net worth.
  • Previous auditor unavailable to accept the fee and no electronic transfer possible → the client may buy a demand draft; the incoming auditor may accept after verifying it and must ensure payment at the earliest.
  • Procedure: ask whether the retiring auditor has been informed. If not and the client will not move first, ask the reason; if there is no valid reason, it is healthy practice not to accept.
  • Positive evidence of delivery is needed. A "certificate of posting" is not enough (J.S. Bhati, Rajasthan HC; Illus 16: guilty). Acceptable: (a) Registered Acknowledgement Due; (b) by hand against written acknowledgement; (c) acknowledgement from the retiring auditor's e-mail registered with ICAI or last known official e-mail; (d) UDIN generated on the portal (subject to guidelines).
  • Returned with "office found locked" → deemed delivered. Returned with "no such office exists" at the address registered with ICAI on the date of dispatch → deemed delivered unless the retiring auditor proves otherwise. No reply from the retiring auditor → act after waiting a reasonable time.
  • Applies to all audits: statutory, tax, GST, internal, concurrent and others. Where the previous auditor was not a CA, communicating is healthy practice. Special audit under the IT Act: healthy to communicate with the statutory auditor.
  • Government companies, banks or branches with no time to wait: conditional acceptance after sending the communication, stating it is subject to professional objections from the previous auditor; final decision after considering the reply.
  • Communication sent only after starting the work (Illus 15: tax audit, registered post sent before signing) → still guilty. "First" means before acceptance.

Cl (9) Accepts appointment as auditor of a company without first ascertaining compliance with S.225 of the 1956 Act (now S.139 and 140, read with S.141, CA 2013)

  • "Ascertain" means find out for certain. A management certificate is not enough; verify the records. If the company refuses access, do not accept.
  • What to verify: first auditor → Board resolution within 30 days of registration (or, if made at a general meeting, a proper notice and valid resolution); casual vacancy → Board's power and resolution; vacancy from resignation → resolution at the general meeting; removal before the term ends → special resolution at the GM and prior approval of the Central Government.
  • Replacement of the retiring auditor (S.140(4)): special notice by a member not earlier than 3 months but at least 14 days before the meeting (clear days); sent to all members at least 7 days before the meeting (S.115); a copy to the retiring auditor forthwith; his representation circulated and considered at the GM; resolution passed properly. Obtain a true copy of the notice and a copy of the minutes verified by the Chairman.
  • Service on the retiring auditor: hand delivery or Registered A/D. The company's certificate of posting is not adequate; the outgoing auditor's acknowledgement is good evidence (DCA circular of 17.10.1981).
  • Adjourned AGM: the retiring auditor continues until the adjourned meeting concludes. A special notice received after adjournment cannot be acted on (14 clear days before the original meeting). No auditor appointed at the AGM → the existing auditor continues (S.139(10)).
  • Illus 17: the appointed auditor declined, and the Board appointed another. This is not a casual vacancy, so the appointment is defective and the auditor who accepted is guilty under Cl (9).
  • Order of steps: satisfy yourself on S.139 and 140, then communicate under Cl (8), then accept.

Unjustified removal of auditors (ESB guidelines; CGG Chapter XI)

  • (A) An auditor who resigns or does not offer himself for reappointment for professional reasons should write to the Board of Directors with the reasons and send a copy to the Institute. The incoming auditor must obtain a copy from the Board and consider it.
  • (B) An auditor willing but not reappointed files with the Institute a copy of the statement sent for circulation to shareholders. The incoming auditor must obtain it from the company and consider it.
  • (C) The ESB may call for information and report to the Council. (D) The same applies to removals by the government or statutory authorities.
  • Chapter XI CGG: the incoming auditor must follow the Council's direction not to accept the appointment where the removal is unjustified.

Cl (10) Fees as a percentage of profits or contingent on findings or results Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ Q14; Jan 2026 Q5(c)

  • Prohibited except as permitted by regulations. The reason: payment in proportion to the client's benefit tempts exaggeration and impairs independence (for example, a fee only if an appeal succeeds). Fees fixed by a court or public authority are not contingent.
  • Regulation 192 exceptions: (a) receiver or liquidator: % of realisation or disbursement of assets (Illus 19); (b) auditor of a co-operative society: % of paid-up capital, working capital, gross or net income or profits; (c) valuer for direct taxes and duties: % of the value of the property (TYK 18); (d) certain management consultancy services decided by Council resolution; (e) fund raising: % of funds raised; (f) debt recovery: % of debt recovered; (g) cost optimisation: % of benefit derived; (h) others decided by the Council: (i) acting as Insolvency Professional; (ii) non-assurance services to non-audit clients (TYK 29: 10% of tax demand reduced for a non-audit client → not guilty).
  • ESB decisions: fees as a % of the utilisation amount for certifying the utilisation of a grant by an educational institute are permissible; Mystery Audit is permissible.
Look-alikeCl (10) First Sch Pt ICl (10) Second Sch Pt I
SubjectHow he charges his fee: % of profits or contingentHow he handles client's money: separate bank account and use for the intended purpose within a reasonable time
ReliefRegulation 192Council suggestions (i)–(iv) below

Trigger words → which one"fee of 10% of tax saved", "success fee", "% of profit""₹10 lakh to invest", "kept in the firm's savings account", "advance received", "stamp paper"
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Cl (11), (12)

Cl (11) Engages in another business or occupation without Council permission Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ Q15; May 2026 MCQ CS-II Q9

  • Proviso: he may be a director of a company (not MD or WTD) unless he or any partner is its auditor. The aim is to keep other businesses out of the profession and stop indirect advertising through them.
  • Reg 190A: no other business or occupation except with permission under a Council resolution. Reg 191: may act as liquidator, trustee, executor, administrator, arbitrator, receiver, adviser or representative for costing, financial or tax matters, take a Central or State Government, court or legal-authority appointment, or act as Secretary in his professional capacity, if not on a salary-cum-full-time basis (TYK 27: Secretary to a metro project, not full-time → not guilty; ESB: a mediator is covered as an "arbitrator").
General permission (no application needed)Specific and prior approval of the Council in each case
(1) Employment under a CA in practice or a CA firm; (2) private tutorship; (3) authorship of books and articles; (4) LIC agency licence only for renewal commission; (5) attending classes and appearing for any examination; (6) elected public office: MP, MLA, MLC; (7) honorary leadership of charitable, educational or non-commercial organisations; (8) Notary Public, Justice of the Peace, Special Executive Magistrate; (9) part-time tutorship under the Institute's coaching organisation; (10) valuing papers, paper-setter, head examiner, moderator; (11) editorship of professional journals; (12) Surveyor and Loss Assessor under the Insurance Act, 1938; (13) recovery consultant in banking; (14) owning agricultural land and agriculture (from 9 Aug 2008)(1) Full-time or part-time employment in a business concern (no substantial interest of him or relatives); (2) full-time or part-time employment in a non-business concern; (3) MD or WTD of a body corporate (no substantial interest); (4) interest in a family business (by inheritance, succession or partition, no active part); (5) interest in an educational institution; (6) lectureship for courses other than the Institute's; (7) tutorship under any educational institution other than the Institute's coaching organisation; (8) editorship of non-professional journals (Illus 22); (9) anything else the Executive Committee considers fit
  • Relative: husband, wife, brother, sister, or any lineal ascendant or descendant. Substantial interest: for a company, shares (other than fixed-dividend shares) carrying at least 20% of voting power owned beneficially by him, relatives or their concerns; for other concerns, entitlement to at least 20% of profits.
  • Teaching under general or specific permission: direct teaching hours in total must not exceed 25 hours a week if he wants to do attest functions.
  • Director simplicitor (not MD or WTD; attends only Board meetings; paid only sitting fees): generally permitted, even with substantial interest, unless he or a partner is the auditor. ESB: the auditor of a subsidiary cannot be a director of its holding company. Promoter or signatory to the MoA and AoA: no bar. Being deemed an MD or WTD under S.2(54) CA 2013 if entrusted with substantially the whole of management also counts.
  • HUF or family business: show that the interest devolved by inheritance, succession or partition, that he did not create it and that he is not actively engaged; file a declaration with the decentralised office. As Karta, he is within limits if he invests HUF funds only and is not active in management.
  • Illus 20: full-time lecturer in a college plus partner in his brother's business → both need specific permission → guilty. Illus 21: took office as executive chairman on 1-4-2024 and applied on 10-4-2024 → guilty (permission must come before).
  • Corporate form (CGG Chapter XVII): may be MD, WTD or Manager of a company engaged exclusively in Management Consultancy and other Services, which must be approved and registered with the Institute; no limit on equity; he keeps a full-time COP, can do attest work and train articles. No audit practice in corporate form. The company must not take internal audit or book-keeping of the practitioner's statutory audit clients; the non-audit fee ceiling (Notification of 8 March 2002) applies; it must comply with Cl (6) and (7) (TYK 22: CA Shubh's action valid).

Cl (12) Allows a non-member in practice, or a member who is not his partner, to sign financial statements or reports for him or his firm Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-II Q9

  • Read with S.26: no person other than a member may sign any document for a CA in practice or a firm in its professional capacity. "Report" means a report expressing a professional opinion (financial or not), as finally submitted.
  • May be delegated (no opinion or authentication): (i) audit queries; (ii) asking for information or questionnaires; (iii) letters forwarding draft observations or FS; (iv) initialling and stamping vouchers and schedules; (v) routine correspondence; (vi) memorandum of cash or physical verification; (vii) acknowledgements for records produced; (viii) bills and money receipts; (ix) routine tax matters, subject to S.288 IT Act; (x) other office administration. Delegation is no defence in an enquiry.
  • Not delegable: stock certificate (Illus 24), GST production certificate (TYK 11), FS (TYK 16), power of attorney to an employee CA to sign reports (Illus 23 → Cl (12) plus Cl (1) Pt II Second Sch for breaching S.26).
  • Disclose his name when signing where a statute requires it; SA 700 requires the membership number and FRN; UDIN.

Examiner's twist: an employee CA can practise "in the name" of his employer (Cl 1 allows it) and can do the examination (Cl 2 of the Second Schedule allows it), but cannot sign the report (Cl 12). Three clauses give three different answers for the same employee.

First Sch Part III: members generally

  • Cl (1) Not being a fellow, acts as a fellow.
  • Cl (2) Does not supply information called for, or comply with requirements, of the Institute, Council or its Committees, Director (Discipline), Board of Discipline, Disciplinary Committee, Quality Review Board or Appellate Authority (Illus 26: left the "other business" columns blank in the COP application; Illus 27 and TYK 35: did not reply about the date of leaving or joining).
  • Cl (3) Gives information knowing it to be false while inviting work from another CA, responding to tenders or enquiries, or advertising through a write-up (that is, in Cl (6) and (7) situations).

8.2 Second Schedule, Part I: CA in practice

Cl (1) Discloses client information without consent or legal requirement

  • The duty continues after the assignment. Disclosure that is part of the professional duty the client requested (for example, to exchange control authorities) counts as consent.
  • Who can consent: proprietor or his constituted attorney; in a firm, any partner; in a company, the Board (S.179), or the MD if the Board's powers are delegated to him widely enough, otherwise a Board resolution.
  • Working papers: no duty to give the client or other auditors (main auditor, parent's or subsidiary's auditor) access. The main auditor has no right to the branch auditor's papers; he may seek clarifications or visit the branch. "Auditor" includes internal auditor. He may, at his discretion, give extracts to the client. Information (as opposed to papers) can go to the client or a regulator with the client's consent.
  • Under legal compulsion where privilege (S.126 Evidence Act) cannot be claimed → not misconduct (take legal advice). S.143(12): fraud by officers or employees against the company → report to the Central Government within 60 days of knowledge, following the prescribed procedure.
  • Illus 29 and TYK 19: sharing client information at a symposium or seminar "in national interest" or "to help understanding" → guilty. TYK 12: a bank asks the auditor for working paper details → refuse without the client's specific permission.

Cl (2) Certifies or submits a report on FS not examined by him, a partner, an employee or another CA in practice

  • Allows joint assignments. Joint auditors: divide the work and document and communicate the division; each is responsible for his allotted work; matters needing joint judgement are communicated before reporting; obtaining management information is a joint responsibility unless divided (SA 299).

Cl (3) Permits his name on an estimate of earnings contingent on future transactions so that he seems to vouch for its accuracy

  • He may prepare or review forecasts and projections if his report states the sources of information, the basis and the major assumptions and he does not vouch for accuracy (Council, 100th meeting, July 1982; GN on Reports in Company Prospectuses (Revised 2019)). 97 projection statements issued without verifying basic documents, used for bank loans → guilty under Cl (3), (7) and (8).

Cl (4) Expresses an opinion on FS of an entity in which he, his firm or a partner has a substantial interest

  • "Substantial interest" as in Appendix 9 (20%). "Financial statements" covers reports and certificates under any statute or for income tax. It applies to all attest functions: tax audit, GST audit, concurrent audit, stock exchange and broker audits. "Public conscience is expected to be ahead of the law."
  • Council decisions: a liquidator must not audit his own S.348(1) statement; a CA in employment cannot certify the FS of his employer or of a concern under the same management, even with a COP (unless a law permits it); no audit of a college where he is a part-time lecturer; no audit of a trust where his partner is an employee or trustee.

  • Also under Cl (4): no writing books of account for audit clients; the statutory auditor (under CA 2013 or any statute) cannot be the internal auditor; the internal auditor (employee or practising CA or firm) cannot be the tax auditor or the GST auditor; satisfy yourself that the appointment is valid under the governing statute, trust deed, or regulator's authorisation, and get a resolution of the managing committee or board of trustees, or an engagement letter from a firm or proprietor; S.288 IT Act covers tax audit disqualifications.
  • Cooling-off after directorship (Cl (4) guideline (xv)): no audit of the company for 2 years from the end of his tenure as director or his resignation (TYK 14: guilty).

Cl (5) Fails to disclose a material fact known to him that is not disclosed in the FS but is needed to make them not misleading, where he is concerned with them professionally

  • Materiality must be established (SA 320), judged against both the balance sheet and P&L. It does not cover statements prepared by employees only for their employer. Illus 30: an unrecorded ₹10 lakh loan from the EPF was ignored in the report → guilty.

Cl (6) Fails to report a material misstatement known to him in FS with which he is concerned professionally

  • Same materiality test. Illus 31: as authorised representative he only submitted client data on instructions → not liable under Cl (5) or (6).

Cl (7) Does not exercise due diligence, or is grossly negligent Asked: May 2026 Q5(c)

  • Skill, care and caution of a reasonably competent, careful and cautious auditor; "a watchdog but not a bloodhound" (Karnataka HC, 1977). Minor errors are not gross negligence; the Council only alerts members about them. Cases: circulation figures certified from an MIS report without the books (Illus 32); audit report not given in time for statutory compliance (Illus 33); digitally signing an ROC form with a copied signature without checking (TYK 23).

Cl (8) Fails to obtain sufficient information needed to express an opinion, or its exceptions are material enough to negate an opinion

  • Do not opine before getting the data. If limitations negate an opinion, disclaim clearly; do not write "subject to verification of existence and value of investments… true and fair" (SA 705). Cases: circulation certificate without financial records (Registrar of Newspapers v K. Rajinder Singh, 1971); 12 certificates without stating limitations, assumptions or basis → Cl (7) and (8). "A certificate is a written confirmation of the accuracy of facts and does not involve any estimate or opinion."

Cl (9) Fails to invite attention to any material departure from generally accepted audit procedure

  • Guidance: Engagement and Quality Control Standards, Statements, Guidance Notes and other pronouncements. Failing to perform a statutory duty is not excused by merely qualifying the report (example: not verifying cash when it was necessary, feasible and material). Special procedures: ABC circulation certification (report even insignificant lapses) and bank verification. Peer-reviewed auditors only for listed companies (SEBI). FRN and membership number on all attest reports. UDIN mandatory from 1 July 2019 for all attest functions.
Look-alikeCl (5)Cl (6)Cl (7)Cl (8)Cl (9)

Trigger words"loan not disclosed", "contingent liability omitted""inflated investments he knew of", "wrong figure he knew of""did not verify", "relied on MIS", "late report", "copied signature""no evidence of existence", "only asset unverified", "insufficient documents for the utilisation certificate""did not attend stock count and did not say so", "ABC rules not followed"

Examiner's twist (May 2026 Q5(c) pattern): a utilisation certificate issued on half the documents. The SM pattern is to cite Cl (7) and (8) together. If the facts also say he "knew" a figure was wrong → add Cl (6). If he signed without anyone in the firm examining → add Cl (2) (Illus 34: Cl (2), (7) and (8)). NFRA case scenario (listed SME, FY 2016-17): no stock count or alternative procedures (SA 501), 100% sales to an unidentified related party (SA 550), no confirmations or alternatives (SA 505) → guilty under Cl (7), (8) and (9), penalised and debarred for 2 years under S.132(4).

Cl (10) Fails to keep client money (other than fees or money meant to be spent) in a separate bank account, or to use it for the intended purpose within a reasonable time Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-I Q4; Jan 2026 Q3(b)

Council suggestionSeparate bank account?
(i) Advance against services to be renderedNot covered by Cl (10)
(ii) Money for expenses to be spent within a reasonably short time (statutory fees, stamp paper)Not needed; "reasonably short time" depends on the facts
(iii) Money for expenses not to be spent within a reasonably short timeYes, immediately
(iv) Money received as trustee, executor, liquidator and similarYes, immediately
  • Illus 35: ₹10 lakh from a charity to invest in securities, kept in the firm's own savings account for 3 months → guilty.

Second Sch Part II: members generally

  • Cl (1) Contravenes the Act, the Regulations or any Council guideline. It is the gateway for the CGG 2008. Regulations commonly breached: 43, 46, 47, 48 (articled assistants: engagement, registration, premium, stipend), 56, 65, 67, 68–80 (audit assistants), 190 (register of offices and firms), 190A, 191, 192. TYK 33: issued a certificate without a COP (breach of S.6) → guilty under Cl (1).
  • Cl (2) As an employee, discloses confidential information except as required by law or permitted by the employer (whole-time or part-time; even if he also practises).
  • Cl (3) Includes particulars known to be false in information submitted to the Institute or its bodies.
  • Cl (4) Defalcates or embezzles money received in a professional capacity (fraud, SA 240).
  • Part III: held guilty by a civil or criminal court of an offence punishable with imprisonment of more than six months.

ChapterRule (exact numbers)

IIA member who is an employee must exercise due diligence and must not be grossly negligent
III, IVOmitted (cost auditor; opinion where there is substantial interest)
VA member or firm in practice must keep proper books, including a Cash Book and a Ledger, regardless of S.44AA IT Act limits (Illus 36). Notes on a mobile phone are not books (TYK 32).
VINot more than 60 tax audits (S.44AB) in a financial year: per member or proprietary firm, or per partner of a firm, corporate or non-corporate. Total for a member across all firms and his individual capacity ≤ 60. Audits under S.44AD, 44ADA, 44AE are excluded. Each year's audit is a separate assignment. HO and branches = one; one or more branches of the same concern = one. A part-time practising partner is not counted. Keep a record in the prescribed format. The firm's limit (60 × partners) may be distributed among partners in any manner (10 partners = 600; one partner may sign all of them) in line with SQC 1. Illus 37: 6 partners = 360.
VIIDo not accept an audit where another CA's undisputed audit fee for a statutory audit is unpaid, except for a sick unit (definitions as under Cl (8))
VIIINot more than 30 company audits under S.141 at any time, per member or per partner, private or other companies, excluding OPCs and dormant companies (388th meeting). Aggregate across firms and individual ≤ 30. HO and branches = one; branches of the same company = one. Partners are counted on the date of acceptance. A CA in full-time or part-time employment elsewhere, or a part-time practising partner, is not counted.
IX Asked: May 2026 Q6(c)Statutory auditor of PSUs, Government companies, listed companies and other public companies with turnover of ₹50 crore or more: fees for other work for the same entity, for the auditor plus associate concerns together, must not exceed the statutory audit fee. "Other work" = MCS and all other permitted services, but excludes (a) audit under any other statute, (b) certification required of the statutory auditor, (c) representation before an authority. A stricter regulator condition prevails. Associate concern = a corporate body or firm rendering MCS in which the auditor's proprietor or partners or their relatives are directors or partners or hold substantial interest. Illus 39: audit fee ₹50 lakh, MCS ₹1 crore → misconduct. TYK 20: ITAT representation ₹1.75 crore against an audit fee of ₹80 lakh → no misconduct.
XThe member, his partner, the firm or a relative must not accept an audit while indebted to the concern or having given a guarantee or security for a third person's debt to it: above the statutory limits, and in other cases above ₹1,00,000. Fees recovered progressively under the engagement terms → not indebted (Illus 40). ESB: holding the audited bank's credit card is allowed if the balance outstanding beyond the credit period is within ₹1,00,000. A loan against his FD from the bank he audits → not permitted.
XIFollow the Council's direction not to accept appointment after an unjustified removal
XIIITenders: as under Cl (6) (minimum fee prescribed, or area open to others)
XIVUDIN: certificates from 1 Feb 2019; GST and tax audit reports from 1 Apr 2019; all other audit, assurance and attestation from 1 Jul 2019
XVNetworking (next page)
XVILogo guidelines: CA India logo (2023)
XVIICorporate form of practice: MCS company (see Cl (11))
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Networking, numbers

Examiner's twist (Chapter IX): the question gives a private company or a public company with turnover below ₹50 crore → Chapter IX does not apply. It gives a tax audit fee or certification fee and asks "does it breach the ceiling?" → those are excluded. It splits the consultancy between the firm and a company run by a partner's spouse → that company is an associate concern and the fees are added. Count only MCS and other permitted services, and compare with the statutory audit fee (not the total audit fee).

Chapter XV: Guidelines for Networking Asked: Sep 2025 Q1(b)

  • A network is judged by whether a reasonable and informed third party would conclude that the entities are associated so that a network exists, whether or not they are legally separate. Referral of work alone is not a network.
  • Deemed a network where the structure aims at co-operation and shares any of: profit or cost sharing (not immaterial costs, and not costs only of audit methodology, manuals or training); common ownership, control or management; common quality control policies (designed, implemented and monitored across the structure); common business strategy (not merely a joint response to a request for proposal); common brand name (including common initials; used with the firm name when signing an audit report); a significant part of professional resources (common client, billing or time systems; partners and staff; technical departments; methodology; training). A shared methodology or training only, with no exchange of people or information → unlikely to be significant. Describing membership of an association carelessly may create the perception of a network.
  • Forms: mutual entity (facilitator; does not practise); partnership (maximum 20 partners); LLP; company (under the corporate-form guidelines). Constituents: sole practitioners, partnerships or other permitted entities. A firm may join only one network; firms with common partners join only one network.
StepFormTime limit
Approval of network name (use "& Affiliates", never "& Co." or "& Associates"; Reg 190 applies; name can be withdrawn later)AInstitute approves or rejects within 30 days of receiving Form A. Approval alone does not allow practice in the network's name.
Name reserved after approvalnone3 months from approval
Registration of the network with the Institute (mandatory)BWithin those 3 months, otherwise the name stands cancelled
Change in constitution (entry or exit)CWithin 30 days of the change
Listing of a network with entities outside IndiaDDeclaration within 30 days of entering the arrangement
  • Indian firms networked with a common multinational accounting firm are part of a network. Name examples: "AB & Affiliates" (mutual entity or partnership), "AB Affiliates LLP", "AB Affiliates P. Ltd/Limited".
  • Ethical compliance: (1) if one network firm is the statutory auditor, no network firm or associate may take internal audit, book-keeping or other work prohibited to the auditor; (2) non-audit fee ceiling: for the auditing firm (with associate concerns and firms with common partners) as in the notification; for other firms of the network collectively, 3 times the statutory audit fee; (3) where a regulator prescribes rotation, no network firm may replace a retiring network firm; (4) may advertise within the guidelines; "Network Firms" may be printed on stationery; (5) all ethical standards apply.
  • Registration with the Institute is a public notice, so the client's consent is deemed obtained. Bye-laws (illustrative): managing committee, administration, fees, engagement partner, arbitration, training, newsletters, software, databases, library, technical director, drawing resources, compensation, peer review.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Advertisement Guidelines 2008

9.1 Council Guidelines for Advertisement, 2008 Asked: May 2026 Q2(b)

  • Write-up conditions: (A) honest and truthful; (B) no exaggerated claims about services, qualifications or experience; (C) no disparaging references or unsubstantiated comparisons; (D) nothing that brings the profession into disrepute; (E) no testimonials or endorsements, no names of clients (past or present), no fees charged; (F) no achievements or awards (except those given by the Central or State Government or regulatory bodies), positions held or accreditations; (G) no monogram, no catch words; (H) Membership No. or FRN is mandatory; (I) font size not above 14; (J) must not breach the Act, the Regulations, the Code of Ethics 2020 or any guideline. ICAI does not approve write-ups and may issue a reasoned directive to remove them.
Write-up may contain: memberWrite-up may contain: firm
CA name; membership number; age; dates of becoming ACA and FCA; date from which the COP has been held; recognised qualifications; languages; phone, mobile, fax; professional address; website; e-mail; CA logo; passport-style photograph; employee numbers (CAs, other professionals, articles or audit assistants, others); employees' names and particulars; services provided; position as director or MD of an MCS company registered with ICAIFirm name with "Chartered Accountants"; FRN; year of establishment; professional addresses (HO and branches) registered with ICAI; working hours; phone, mobile, fax; e-mail; number of partners; partners' particulars with photograph; CA logo; employee numbers and particulars; services provided; affiliation with a network registered with ICAI. It may carry a signature, name, place and date.

Examiner's twist: "year of establishment" is allowed in a write-up and on a website, but the date of setting up practice on letterheads is not (Cl (7)). An award from a private chamber of commerce → not allowed; an award from a State Government → allowed. A tagline such as "Your Growth Partner" or "AI Professional" is a catch word or designation → not allowed. "Big clients with turnover above ₹500 crore" breaches E (client names) or B (exaggeration), and listing client details also raises confidentiality under Cl (1) Part I Second Sch (May 2026 Q2(b) pattern).

Website of CA firms

  • Format is free. It must run on a "pull" model, never "push": nothing is circulated by e-mail except on a specific pull request; no circular inviting people to visit; the website address may be shown on stationery and e-mail.
  • May show: member, trade or firm name; year of establishment; addresses, phone, fax, e-mail; partners and employees (name, qualification, contact); job vacancies, including articleship. Only on a specific pull request: nature of services; partners' and employees' areas of experience; number of articled assistants; nature of assignments handled.
  • Client names and fees: not allowed, unless a regulator (in or outside India) requires them, to the extent and for the period required, with an italic note below naming the regulator, jurisdiction and rule.
  • Allowed: passport-style photograph; articles, bulletin boards, professional updates; educational videos; chat rooms between members and between the firm and clients (keep confidentiality); a document management log-in for clients; a link to its social networking page (but do not solicit visits or likes); online advice to clients who specifically ask, free or paid; links to ICAI and government sites. Colours are not restricted (TYK 6).
  • Not allowed: banner or any advertisement; content unbecoming of a CA; information at material variance from ICAI records. The address should be close to the member's or firm's name; the ESB decides difficulties.

Other media

  • Online third-party platforms (banks, newspapers, corporate sites): consultancy and advice only; no contact address; no achievements; only "Chartered Accountant"; the firm name with the suffix "Chartered Accountants" is not permitted.
  • Telephone and trade directories: only under the "Chartered Accountants" category; member belongs to that town; alphabetical order; no prominent entry; open to all CAs of the city; trade and social directories allowed.
  • App-based service aggregators that also list businessmen, technicians and event organisers: not permitted.
  • Specialised directories such as "Who's Who": name, description, address, directorships, personal details and outside interests; no client names.
  • Exemptions (name with "CA" in press advertisements, not more prominent than usual and not in bolder type): recruiting staff for his own office; on behalf of clients seeking staff or buying or selling a business or property; selling a business or property as trustee, liquidator or receiver. Avoid "a well-known firm" in staff advertisements.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Professional EthicsSM Ch 19 · Self-regulation, ESB decisions

10. Recommended self-regulatory measures (recommendatory)

  • Branch audits: not by statutory auditors with 10 or more members; give them to local firms with fewer than 10. Exceptions: branch records kept at HO; significant operations carried out at the branch. The statutory auditor's right of access to branch accounts is not affected.
  • Joint audit: associate a firm with fewer than 5 members in large companies; the senior firm should not object.
  • Staff ratio: at least 1 member for every 5 non-qualified staff (excluding articled and audit assistants, typists, peons and others not doing professional work).
  • Disclosure of payments received for other services through a different firm in which the auditor is a partner or proprietor.
  • Minimum scale of fees: recommended separately for Class A, B and C cities.
  • Fees, relative size: disclose where, for two consecutive years, gross fees from one audit client are more than 40% (non-PIE) or 20% (PIE) of the firm's total fees. Exempt: firm's total fees not above ₹20 lakh; audits of government companies, public undertakings, nationalised banks, PFIs, regulators, or where the government appoints the auditor.

Recent decisions of the Ethical Standards Board Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ Q15; Jan 2026 Q5(c); May 2026 MCQ CS-III Q11

PermissibleNot permissible
  • Statutory auditor giving advisory services on BRSR, or acting as assurance provider of BRSR Core for the same client (SEBI circular 12.07.2023)
  • Assessment or performance audit of skill-development centres (MCS)
  • Mystery Audit
  • Mentioning the position of promoter or director on a company portal (no attainments, no firm name)
  • Professional director on the Board of Management of a co-operative bank; member of the Board of Management of a primary (urban) co-operative bank (like a director simplicitor)
  • Practice in IFSC or GIFT City; serving IFSC units from offices outside
  • Fees as % of utilisation for certifying an educational institute's grant
  • A member of a Trust auditing that Trust (unless a statute bars it or there is a conflict)
  • Professional advice on IPR
  • Internal auditor buying shares of the company
  • Registering on Udyog Aadhaar; services through a kiosk (if permitted professional work); joint GST training for clients with shared fees
  • Non-executive or independent director of a co-operative bank (not day-to-day; not auditor)
  • Authorised representative of a foreign company (if not its auditor)
  • CA in service: e-return registration (cannot certify); tax representation for his employer
  • Equity research adviser (no retail reports)
  • Registration Authority for digital signatures; mediator in court (as arbitrator); settlor of a trust

  • Statutory or tax auditor as valuer where no law prohibits it (subject to the Code)

  • Statutory auditor preparing the BRSR study for an audit client

  • Vision or mission statement on letterhead, visiting card or stationery (allowed in the firm profile on request); vision and values on the back of visiting cards (Cl (6))
  • Statutory auditor of a bank and ASM of a customer of the same bank simultaneously

  • Messaging apps to tell people about his practice and services

  • Bank's statutory auditor doing stock or inspection audit of any branch of the same bank or a sister concern in the same year
  • Internal auditor of a PF Trust (appointed by a Government company) becoming its statutory auditor
  • Concurrent auditor of bank X becoming statutory auditor of bank Y sponsored by X
  • Audit of a bank where he has a loan against his FD
  • Commodity derivatives (equity and currency derivatives in personal capacity are allowed; TYK 25)
  • Representing other employees of his employer (CA in service)

Examiner's twist: BRSR: prepare = no; advise = yes; BRSR Core assurance = yes. Valuer: the 2013 ESB view was "never"; the January 2017 review says it is permitted unless a law (such as the IT Rules) prohibits it. Cooling-off: the SM states the 2-year rule under Cl (4) and uses it in TYK 14, while ESB item 33 (2017) leaves it to CA 2013 and the Code. In an exam, quote the 2-year rule and add the 2017 ESB view in one line.

Beyond the SM (practical): ICAI updates ESB decisions often. The paper uses only the SM list, so learn these items as listed.

AAA · Twist notes · Completion and ReviewSM Ch 6

06Ch 06 — Completion and Review (SA 560, 570, 580)

Asked: May 2026 MCQ CS-I Q4 (SA 560 — fire after report, refusal to amend, 2m); May 2026 Q1(b) (SA 560 non-adjusting event + EOM draft, 5m); May 2026 Q3(c) (SA 570 MUGC, 4m). RTP May 2026 Q10, MTP Jan 2026 S-II 1(c) (going concern); RTP May 2025 Q11 (SA 580); MTP May 2024 S-I 4(a) (SA 560 + EOM not a substitute).

SA 560 — Subsequent Events Asked: May 2026 MCQ CS-I Q4; May 2026 Q1(b)

  • Definition: events occurring between the date of the FS and the date of the auditor's report, and facts that become known to the auditor after the date of the auditor's report.
  • Two kinds: events giving evidence of conditions that existed at the FS date (adjust) and events giving evidence of conditions that arose after the FS date (disclose).
  • Objectives (2): (a) obtain SAAE that events between FS date and report date that require adjustment/disclosure are appropriately reflected; (b) respond appropriately to facts known after the report date that, had they been known at that date, may have caused the auditor to amend the report.
  • After the report date the auditor has no obligation to perform any audit procedures — but must respond if a fact becomes known.
Time periodAuditor's duty (SM)If management amendsIf management does NOT amendTrigger words → this period
1. FS date → report datePerform procedures to identify all such events (consider risk assessment for nature/extent); determine whether appropriately reflected; obtain written representation (SA 580) that all such events adjusted or disclosed.—Treat as misstatement → modify opinion (SA 705)."audit nearing completion", "before signing", "substantive procedures finished"
2. Report date → FS issuedDiscuss with management/TCWG; determine whether FS need amendment; inquire how management intends to address it.Extend procedures; new report (not dated earlier than approval of amended FS) or amend report with an additional date restricted to the amendment; EOM/OM para in new report.Report not yet given to management → modify opinion. Report already given → notify TCWG not to issue FS to third parties; if still issued → take appropriate action to prevent reliance on the report."report signed on…", "AGM due", "not yet filed / not made available to third parties"
3. After FS issuedDiscuss; determine whether amendment needed; inquire how management intends to address.Review steps taken to inform everyone who received the previously issued FS + report; new/amended report with EOM/OM para.If steps not taken → take appropriate steps to prevent reliance on the report."FS circulated / filed with regulator / AGM held"
SM caseFactsAnswer as per SM
TYK 1 (adjusting)Claim receivable 1 crore at 31.3.2024; surveyor settles 45 lacs in April; received 15 May; report not yet issued.Condition existed at FS date → adjust FS.
