Examiner's twist: the story spends a page on the fire/flood/lawsuit and then asks "what is the date of the new report?" or "what does the auditor do after management refuses?". Fix the timeline first (FS date / report date / issue date). One line that scores: "New auditor's report shall not be dated earlier than the date of approval of the amended financial statements." The refusal-after-report-given answer is "notify TCWG not to issue to third parties … prevent reliance" — not "withdraw" and not "modify" (report already out).

SA 570 — Going Concern Asked: May 2026 Q3(c); RTP May 2026 Q10; MTP Jan 2026 S-II 1(c)

  • Going concern: entity viewed as continuing in operation for the foreseeable future, with neither the intention nor the necessity of liquidation or of curtailing materially the scale of operations.
  • Risk assessment (SA 315 stage): consider whether events/conditions exist; consider whether management has made a preliminary assessment; discuss it and management's plans.
  • Auditor cannot be expected to guarantee the entity's ability to continue; conclusions are based on evidence up to the report date — future events may still cause the entity to cease.
  • Communicate with TCWG the events/conditions identified (whether they are a material uncertainty, adequacy of disclosures, implications for report). Delay in approval of FS linked to going concern → additional procedures + consider effect.
Look-alikeManagementAuditor (responsibility)Auditor (additional procedures when events/conditions identified)
Items1. Make a specific assessment of ability to continue as GC. 2. Make appropriate disclosures.1. Obtain SAAE and conclude on appropriateness of use of GC basis. 2. Conclude whether a material uncertainty exists. (Not: guarantee.)(a) Request management to make its assessment (if not done); (b) evaluate management's plans — likely to improve? feasible?; (c) cash flow forecast: reliability of underlying data + adequate support for assumptions; (d) consider additional facts since management's assessment; (e) written representations on plans and their feasibility.
Trigger words"make assessment", "make disclosures""conclude", "assess whether MU exists""evaluate", "analyse forecast", "request", "consider additional facts"
  • Other SM procedures: analyse interim FS; read loan agreements for breach; read minutes for financing difficulties; inquire of legal counsel on litigation; confirm financial support arrangements and ability of parties; subsequent-events procedures for mitigating factors; confirm existence/terms/adequacy of borrowing facilities.
  • Management plans evaluated may include: liquidate assets, borrow money or restructure debt, reduce or delay expenditures, increase capital.
  • Indicators used in SM cases: adverse key ratios (current ratio, DSCR, inventory and receivables turnover); delay in paying creditors/salaries; bankers refusing credit; change from credit to cash on delivery with suppliers; arrears or discontinuance of dividends; loss of a key customer/major market (operating indicator); KMP leaving; no action plan. A new competitor opening nearby is not a financial indicator (SM MCQ).
ConclusionOpinionWhere it goes in the report

Appropriate + MU + inadequate disclosureQualified or adverse (material vs pervasive)Basis for Qualified/Adverse: MU exists and FS do not adequately disclose it
Management unwilling to make/extend assessmentConsider implications for the report—
Appropriate, no MUUnmodifiedNo special paragraph

Examiner's twist: long sad story (bird flu, floods, pandemic) and the ask is only "with specific reference to cash flow forecast" — answer the two forecast tests (reliability of data; support for assumptions) and apply each to the facts (no fresh bank credit, repayments start next year, longer supplier credit in a downturn, extra vet/vaccination costs, pollution claims). Second trap: "management itself discloses that GC is inappropriate" → still adverse. Third trap: MU adequately disclosed → write "unmodified opinion + MUGC section", never "EOM" or "qualified".

AAA · Twist notes · Completion and ReviewSM Ch 6

SA 580 — Written Representations Asked: RTP May 2025 Q11

  • Written representation: written statement by management to confirm certain matters or support other evidence; does not include FS, assertions therein, or supporting books and records.
  • Necessary audit evidence but not sufficient appropriate evidence on their own; reliable WR does not change nature/extent of other evidence.
  • Objectives (3): (1) obtain WR that management/TCWG believe they have fulfilled responsibility for preparation of FS and completeness of information provided; (2) support other evidence on FS or specific assertions; (3) respond appropriately to WR provided (or not provided).
  • Date: as near as practicable to, but not after, the date of the auditor's report. Opinion cannot be expressed and report cannot be dated before the date of WR. Interim WR on a specific assertion → may need an updated WR.
  • Period: all FS and periods referred to in the report. Current management not in place earlier → still must cover the whole period (does not diminish their responsibility). May be a form that updates prior-period WRs.
  • Doubt on reliability (inconsistent with other evidence) → procedures to resolve; if unreliable → action under SA 705. Not provided → discuss, re-evaluate integrity of management, consider effect on report (SA 705).
  • Mandatorily from management — SM MCQ answer: CFO (not audit committee, CS or relationship managers). Not a "pillar": "WR provides sufficient appropriate evidence".
Specimen letter item (SM TYK 5)Linked SA
Related party relationships/transactions accounted and disclosed; identity of related parties disclosedSA 550
All subsequent events requiring adjustment/disclosure adjusted or disclosedSA 560
Uncorrected misstatements immaterial; list attachedSA 450
Results of fraud risk assessment; fraud/suspected fraud involving management, key-control employees, others (material); allegations from employees, analysts, regulatorsFraud
Known/suspected non-compliance with lawsLaws and regulations
Access to all information; additional information requested; unrestricted access to persons; all transactions recordedCompleteness of information (SA 580 core)
Which date? (look-alike)Rule as per SMTrigger words
Auditor's report (SA 700)No earlier than the date of SAAE, including evidence that all statements and notes are prepared and those with authority have asserted responsibility"date of report", "Board approval"
New report on amended FS (SA 560)Not earlier than date of approval of the amended FS"fresh/new report", "amended accounts"
Written representations (SA 580)As near as practicable to, but not after, report date"representation letter dated…"

Examiner's twist: management says "verbal confirmations were documented by your team" → WR is mandatory in writing. Letter dated 15 April, report dated 31 July → anomaly: subsequent events after 15 April not covered. Restated FS for 3 years, new management gives WR only for latest year → must cover all periods; refusal → re-evaluate integrity, SA 705.

SA 700 series — which SA does what

SA 700 — Objectives and elements

  • Objectives: (a) form an opinion based on evaluation of conclusions from evidence; (b) express clearly that opinion through a written report. Applies to complete set of general purpose FS.
  • Opinion: whether FS prepared, in all material respects, per applicable FRF; obtain reasonable assurance. Fair presentation framework → also evaluate overall presentation, structure, content and whether FS represent underlying transactions in a manner that achieves fair presentation.
  • Elements (14, SM order): Title; Addressee; Opinion; Basis for Opinion; Going Concern; KAM; Other Information; Responsibilities for FS; Auditor's Responsibilities; Location of description of auditor's responsibilities; Other Reporting Responsibilities; Signature; Place; Date.
  • Title: "Independent Auditor's Report". Addressee: Members (general purpose statutory FS); Board (special purpose FS). Opinion section is the first section, heading "Opinion".
  • Fair presentation phrases (equivalent): "present fairly, in all material respects" or "give a true and fair view". Compliance framework: "prepared, in all material respects, in accordance with…".
  • Signature: engagement partner in personal name + firm name; membership number; FRN; UDIN mandatory from 1 July 2019 (manual and digital, incl. certificates uploaded online). S.145 r/w s.141(2): only the person appointed / partner who is a CA may sign. Clause (12) Part I First Schedule CA Act: allowing a non-member or a member not being partner to sign = misconduct.
  • Place: ordinarily the city where signed.
  • Location of auditor's responsibilities: (a) within body; (b) appendix (report refers to it); (c) website of appropriate authority where law/regulation/standards expressly permit.
  • Other reporting responsibilities: separate section "Report on Other Legal and Regulatory Requirements", following "Report on the Audit of the Financial Statements".
Opinion section must…Basis for Opinion must…Auditor's Responsibilities section must…
(a) Identify entity; (b) state FS audited; (c) identify title of each statement; (d) refer to notes incl. significant accounting policies; (e) specify date/period of each statement(a) Audit per SAs; (b) refer to auditor's responsibilities section; (c) independence statement referring to ICAI Code of Ethics; (d) whether evidence is sufficient and appropriateObjectives (reasonable assurance; issue report); reasonable assurance = high level, not a guarantee; materiality description; professional judgment and scepticism; identify/assess RMM (fraud risk higher — collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, override); understand IC not to express opinion on IC (omit phrase if IC opinion also given); evaluate policies/estimates/disclosures; conclude on GC; evaluate overall presentation; group audit division of responsibility (SA 600); communication with TCWG; independence statement to TCWG; KAM determination statement
  • Report prescribed by law — may refer to SAs only if it has at minimum 13 items (title; addressee; opinion + FRF; identification of FS; independence statement; SA 570 section; Basis for Qualified/Adverse per SA 570; SA 701 info; management responsibilities; reference to SAs and auditor's responsibilities; signature; place; date).
  • Both SAs and ISAs: refer to both only if (a) no conflict leading to a different opinion or omission of a required EOM/OM, and (b) minimum elements included; identify jurisdiction of origin.
  • Supplementary information: integral part → covered by opinion (e.g. reconciliation to another framework in notes). Not integral → must be clearly differentiated; if management refuses to change presentation → identify it and state it is unaudited (e.g. additional P&L schedule as appendix).

Examiner's twist: "significant deficiencies in internal control communicated to TCWG — where in the report?" → Auditor's Responsibilities section (SM TYK 1), not KAM, not Basis for Opinion. "Who signs?" → partner in personal name with firm name, M.No., FRN, UDIN.

AAA · Twist notes · ReportingSM Ch 7

SA 701 — Key Audit Matters Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-II Q8; MTP Jan 2026 S-I 4(b)

  • KAM: matters that, in auditor's professional judgment, were of most significance in the audit of FS of the current period; selected from matters communicated with TCWG.
  • Applies to: complete sets of general purpose FS of listed entities; when auditor otherwise decides; when required by law/regulation. SA 705 prohibits KAM when auditor disclaims an opinion, unless law/regulation requires.
  • KAM is NOT: (a) substitute for FS disclosures; (b) substitute for modified opinion (SA 705); (c) substitute for SA 570 MU reporting; (d) a separate opinion on individual matters.
  • Auditor shall not communicate in KAM section a matter for which opinion must be modified (SM answer TQ 16).
StageStep (SM)
Determine (step 1: filter)From matters communicated with TCWG, those that required significant auditor attention, considering: (a) areas of higher assessed RMM or significant risks (SA 315); (b) significant auditor judgments on areas of significant management judgment incl. estimates with high estimation uncertainty; (c) effect on audit of significant events or transactions during the period. Then those of most significance = KAM.
Communicate (step 2: report)Section "Key Audit Matters" with introductory language: (a) KAM are matters of most significance in current-period audit; (b) addressed in context of audit as a whole and forming opinion; no separate opinion. Describe each unless law precludes public disclosure, or in extremely rare cases adverse consequences reasonably expected to outweigh public interest benefits.
No KAMStill carry the section: "[Except for the matter described in the Basis for Qualified (Adverse) Opinion section or MUGC section,] we have determined that there are no [other] key audit matters to communicate."
  • SM examples: impairment; provisions for losses and contingencies; valuation of financial instruments; revenue recognition; taxation (multiple jurisdictions, uncertain tax positions, deferred tax assets).
  • SM TYK 2: revenue recognition with discounts/rebates + procedures (read policy vs Ind AS 115, test controls, cut-off with confirmations) → KAM.

Examiner's twist: Audit Committee asks to drop a KAM to avoid "negative publicity" → not a ground; the only exceptions are law precluding disclosure or extremely rare adverse consequences outweighing public interest. Unlisted company → KAM only if auditor decides or law requires. Disclaimer → no KAM section. A matter needing a qualified opinion → Basis for Qualified Opinion, not KAM.

SA 705 — Modified opinions Asked: Jan 2026 Q1(a); Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q1; MTP May 2025 S-II 1(b)

  • Modify when: (a) FS as a whole not free from material misstatement; or (b) unable to obtain SAAE to conclude they are free.
  • Type depends on (a) nature of matter (misstated vs may be misstated) and (b) pervasiveness.
  • Pervasive: not confined to specific elements/accounts/items; or if confined, represents/could represent a substantial proportion; or for disclosures, fundamental to users' understanding.
  • Disclaimer also when, in extremely rare circumstances with multiple uncertainties, opinion not possible due to potential interaction and cumulative effect, even though SAAE on each.
NatureMaterial, not pervasiveMaterial and pervasive
FS materially misstatedQualified — "except for the effects of…"Adverse — "because of the significance… do not present fairly / do not give a true and fair view"
Inability to obtain SAAEQualified — "except for the possible effects of…"Disclaimer — "We do not express an opinion…"; "We were engaged to audit…"
  • Management-imposed limitation after acceptance: request removal → if refused, communicate with TCWG (unless all TCWG manage) and try alternative procedures → still unable: material not pervasive = qualify; material and pervasive = withdraw where practicable and possible under law, else disclaim. Before withdrawing, communicate to TCWG misstatements that would have caused modification.
  • SEBI circular CIR/CFD/CMD1/114/2019 (18 Oct 2019) — listed: resign within 45 days of quarter-end → issue report for that quarter; after 45 days → that quarter and next; limited review issued for all three quarters → issue annual audit report before resigning. ICAI: auditor of unlisted company shall not cite "professional pre-occupation"; state reasons clearly.
  • Adverse/disclaimer on FS as a whole → no unmodified opinion on a single statement or element in same report.
  • Basis section: heading "Basis for Qualified/Adverse/Disclaimer of Opinion"; specific amounts → describe and quantify (or say not practicable); narrative disclosures → explain how misstated; non-disclosure → discuss with TCWG, describe omitted info, include omitted disclosures if practicable and SAAE obtained (unless law prohibits); inability → reasons. Qualified/adverse → amend sufficiency statement to add "qualified"/"adverse". Also describe other matters that would have required modification, even with adverse/disclaimer.
  • Disclaimer omits: reference to auditor's responsibilities section; statement on sufficiency of evidence. Auditor's responsibilities reduced to: duty to audit per SAs and issue report; not able to obtain SAAE because of the matters; independence/ethics statement. No KAM section (unless law requires).
  • Communicate expected modification and its wording to TCWG.
SM IllustrationFactsOpinion
3Financing about to expire; notes incomplete on impact/refinancingQualified (inadequate MU disclosure, not pervasive)
4Financing expired, considering bankruptcy, no disclosureAdverse
5No access to JV = over 90% of net assets (95 crores)Disclaimer
6No loan agreement for 55,00,000 loanQualified — "possible effects"
7Earthquake, MU not disclosedAdverse
8Debtors 150 crores unconfirmed, provision 25 crores inadequate; inventory 40% of total assets unverifiableDisclaimer (multiple elements)

Examiner's twist: facts say "auditor obtained evidence inconsistent with affirmations" → misstatement → qualified/adverse, not disclaimer (disclaimer needs inability). Facts say "management refused access" → inability → qualified/disclaimer, never adverse. Note: "subject to" and "with the foregoing explanation" are not sufficiently clear — use "except for".

SA 706 — Emphasis of Matter and Other Matter Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-I Q2, CS-III Q11; Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q4; May 2026 Q1(b)

  • EOM: refers to matter appropriately presented or disclosed in FS, of such importance it is fundamental to users' understanding. Conditions: (a) no modification required under SA 705 for the matter; (b) when SA 701 applies, matter not determined to be KAM.
  • EOM mechanics: separate section with heading including "Emphasis of Matter"; clear reference to matter and where disclosed in FS; refer only to information presented/disclosed; state opinion not modified.
  • EOM examples (SM): uncertainty on outcome of exceptional litigation or regulatory action; significant subsequent event between FS date and report date; early application of a new accounting standard with material effect; major catastrophe with significant effect on financial position. Also: FRF prescribed by law that would otherwise be unacceptable; special purpose framework alert; new/amended report under SA 560.
  • OMP: matter not presented/disclosed in FS, relevant to understanding of the audit, auditor's responsibilities or report. Conditions: (a) not prohibited by law/regulation; (b) when SA 701 applies, not a KAM. Heading "Other Matter". SM example: branches audited by branch auditors (5 branches, total assets 90 crores, revenue 40 crores); prior year audited by another auditor.
  • Communicate expectation and wording of EOM/OMP to TCWG.
  • EOM is not a substitute for: modified opinion; required FS disclosures; SA 570 MU reporting.
  • EOM placement: directly before or after KAM section, as per relative significance (A16). Widespread use of EOM diminishes effectiveness; EOM is not a substitute for KAM description.
ItemMatter in FS?Evidence / misstatementOpinionKey testTrigger words → which one
KAM (SA 701)UsuallySAAE; no modification neededUnmodified (can coexist with modified for other matters)Required significant auditor attention; most significance"listed", "complex estimate", "significant risk", "how we addressed"
EOM (SA 706)Yes, appropriatelySAAE; no misstatementNot modified for itFundamental to users; not a KAM"draw attention", "fire / flood / litigation disclosed", "early adoption"
OMP (SA 706)No—Not modified for itRelevant to audit / responsibilities / report"branch auditors", "predecessor", "not disclosed in FS or Board's report"
MUGC (SA 570)Yes, adequatelyMU on GC existsUnmodifiedSeparate MUGC section, not EOM"material uncertainty", "significant doubt", "adequately disclosed"
Qualified (SA 705)Misstated or unsupportedMisstatement / inabilityModifiedMaterial, not pervasive"not provided", "departure from AS", "inadequate disclosure"
Adverse / Disclaimer—Misstatement / inabilityModifiedMaterial and pervasive"over 90% of net assets", "multiple elements", "bankruptcy, no disclosure"
Notes to accountsQualification
Management's stand on a matterAuditor's disagreement, stated from auditor's standpoint
A reference to notes in the report does not automatically become a qualificationAll qualifications must be in the report itself, clear and unambiguous, with reasons; "except for", not "subject to"

Examiner's twist: SM TYK 3 — 500 crore claims receivable, auditor relied only on management representation and plans EOM → wrong: WR alone is not SAAE; procedures may lead to modification, and EOM presupposes SAAE. ICAI pattern: "matter not in FS or Board's report" → OMP (Sep 2025). Flood losses disclosed → EOM; records destroyed, material not pervasive → qualified (SM TQ 19).

Beyond the SM (practical): ICAI's Jan 2026 MCQ key gave EOM where management refused to disclose pending litigation; SA 706 in the SM requires the matter to be appropriately disclosed for EOM — if a descriptive asks this, state the SA 706 condition and the SA 705 route for non-disclosure.

  • SA 260 matters: auditor's responsibilities (opinion on FS prepared by management under TCWG oversight; audit does not relieve them); planned scope and timing incl. significant risks; significant findings — qualitative aspects of accounting practices, significant difficulties, (unless all TCWG manage) significant matters discussed with management and WR requested, circumstances affecting form/content of report, other significant matters. All TCWG involved in managing → need not communicate again in governance role.

SA 710 — Comparative information

PointCorresponding figuresComparative financial statements
Opinion refers toCurrent period onlyEach period presented
When opinion refers to prior figuresOnly if prior report was other than unqualified (matter unresolved) or prior-period material misstatement not addressed earlierAlways (each period)
Opinion on prior period now differs from earlier opinion—Disclose substantive reasons in Other Matter paragraph
Misstatement found in prior FS audited earlierModify (if not addressed)Report to appropriate management level, request predecessor be informed; if amended with new predecessor report → report only on current period
Common: predecessor audited prior yearIf permitted and auditor decides to refer: state prior FS audited by predecessor, type of opinion, date of that report
Common: prior year unauditedState in Other Matter paragraph that figures are unaudited; still need SAAE that opening balances have no material misstatement
  • Procedures: agree comparatives to prior-period amounts/disclosures; consistency of policies; changes properly disclosed; possible misstatement → procedures; prior FS audited by same auditor → SA 560; WR for all periods + specific WR on any prior-period item disclosed.
  • Prior qualification resolved and properly accounted/disclosed → current opinion need not refer to it. Unresolved → modify current opinion; Basis section: refer to both current and corresponding figures if current effect material; otherwise explain modification due to effect on comparability (SM TQ 11: 70 lakh diminution).

SA 720 — Other information

  • Other information = financial or non-financial info (other than FS and report) in the annual report. Not applicable to preliminary announcements and securities offering documents / prospectuses.
  • Objectives: consider material inconsistency with (a) FS, (b) auditor's knowledge; (c) respond appropriately; (d) report.
  • Obtaining: determine documents and timing; arrange to obtain final version, if possible before report date; if available only later → request WR that final version will be provided before issuance.
  • Reading: compare selected amounts/items with FS; consider consistency with knowledge; remain alert for other misstatements. If inconsistency → discuss and conclude whether OI misstated, FS misstated, or understanding needs updating.
OI misstated — management refuses → TCWG still refusesAction
Obtained before report dateConsider implications for report and communicate with TCWG how auditor will address it; withdraw where possible under law
Obtained after report dateIf corrected → necessary procedures; if not → appropriate action considering legal rights and obligations to bring it to users' attention
FS misstated / understanding to updateRespond under other SAs
  • "Other Information" section required when: listed — obtained or expects to obtain OI; unlisted corporate — obtained some or all OI. Contents: management responsible; identify OI obtained before report (and for listed, expected after); opinion does not cover OI, no assurance; auditor's responsibilities; if obtained before report — "nothing to report" or description of uncorrected misstatement. Qualified/adverse → consider implications for this statement.
  • SM TQ 17: market-price trend item in annual report contradicts FS margins → partner wrong; SA 720 applies.

Examiner's twist: SA 720 never changes the opinion on the FS by itself — the OI misstatement is described in the Other Information section. Trap option "qualify the opinion because Directors' report is wrong" is wrong unless the FS themselves are misstated.

Section 143(12) — Fraud reporting (Rule 13) Asked: May 2026 Q5(a)

PointFraud ≥ 1 crore (individually)Fraud < 1 crore
Report toCentral Government (via Board/AC first)Audit Committee (s.177) or Board
TimelineTo Board/AC immediately, not later than 2 days of knowledge, seeking reply in 45 days; forward report + reply + comments to CG within 15 days of receipt; no reply in 45 days → forward report + note of detailsImmediately, not later than 2 days
Content / formTo Secretary, MCA, sealed cover, Regd. Post AD or Speed Post + confirming e-mail; letterhead with postal, e-mail, phone; signed with seal and Membership No.; Form ADT-4Nature with description; approximate amount; parties involved
Board's report—Company discloses: nature, approx. amount, parties (if remedial action not taken), remedial actions
  • Scope: fraud "in the company by its officers or employees" (Rule 13: "against the company"). 143(13): good-faith reporting is no breach of duty. Applies mutatis mutandis to cost auditor (s.148) and secretarial auditor (s.204). Non-compliance penalty: 5 lakh (listed), 1 lakh (other).
  • Fraud under s.447 includes acts with intent to injure interests of company/shareholders/creditors/others, with or without wrongful gain/loss; auditor may not detect what is not reflected in books (vendor pay-offs; stolen KMP password).
  • Also CARO 3(xi): (a) any fraud by or on the company noticed/reported — nature and amount; (b) ADT-4 filed?; (c) whistle-blower complaints considered?

Examiner's twist: 75 lakh fraud — "is he obliged to file ADT-4 on MCA portal?" → No; report to AC/Board within 2 days; company discloses in Board's report; still report under CARO 3(xi)(a). Watch "individually": several frauds each below 1 crore are not added up for the CG test (the rule says "individually").

CARO 2020 — applicability Asked: Jan 2026 Q5(a); Sep 2025 Q1(c)

  • Issued under s.143(11) after consulting NFRA, dated 25 Feb 2020. Applies to every company incl. foreign company (s.2(42)). Paras 3 and 4 in addition to s.143 report.
  • Exempt: (i) banking company (s.5(c) BR Act); (ii) insurance company; (iii) section 8 company; (iv) OPC (s.2(62)) and small company (s.2(85)); (v) private limited company — not a subsidiary or holding company of a public company — with all three: paid-up capital + reserves and surplus not more than 1 crore as on BS date; total borrowings from any bank/FI not exceeding 1 crore at any point of time during FY; total revenue (Sch. III, incl. discontinuing operations) not exceeding 10 crore during FY.
  • CFS: CARO does not apply to report on CFS except clause 3(xxi).
  • Para 4: unfavourable/qualified answer → state basis; unable to express opinion → state fact and reasons.

Examiner's twist: year-end borrowing 60 lakh but 1.2 crore in October → limit breached "at any point of time" → CARO applies. Subsidiary of a public company → exemption (v) unavailable whatever the size. Borrowings from a director or body corporate do not count for (v) — only "bank or financial institution".

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1

14Ch 14 — Special Features of Audit of Banks and NBFCs

Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-II Q5 (revenue leakage, numerical), Q6 (NBFC risk-adjusted assets), Q4(a)(i)–(ii) (restructuring; IRAC norms are minimum) · Jan 2026 MCQ Q7 (ICC NOF by 31.3.2027), Q8 (NBFC NPA provision, secured/unsecured), Q12 (approved liquid assets custody), Q6(b) (bank investments) · May 2026 MCQ Q14 (limit overdue for renewal, AS 4), Q3(a) (NBFC prudential norms procedures).

1 Why banks need special audit consideration

  • Features (SM): custody of large monetary items (cash, negotiable instruments) → embezzlement and fraud risk; huge volume and variety of transactions → complex systems and wide IT use; geographically dispersed branches → decentralised authority; off-balance-sheet commitments with no funds transfer (may carry no accounting entry); transactions initiated at one place, recorded at another, managed at a third; transactions done by the customer directly (internet, mobile, ATM) with no employee; integration with national/international settlement systems → systemic risk; regulators influence accounting and audit practice.
  • Special audit considerations arise from: (1) special nature of risks, (2) scale of operations and significant risks in short periods, (3) extensive dependence on IT, (4) effect of statutory and regulatory requirements, (5) continuing development of new products not matched by accounting/audit practice.
  • SBAs and SCAs must know the products and risks in detail; also understand interface controls between applications (data flows from one application to another).

2 Legal framework, form of accounts, appointment

  • Acts: BR Act 1949, RBI Act 1934 (wide powers to give directions), SBI Act 1955, Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Acts 1970/1980, RRB Act 1976, Companies Act 2013, SARFAESI 2002, PMLA 2002, IT Act 2000, CIC(R) Act 2005, PSS Act 2007, Co-operative Societies Act 1912.
  • Third Schedule BR Act: Form A = Balance Sheet, Form B = P&L. AS under s.133 read with Rule 7 apply so far as applicable. Ind AS for all SCBs deferred by RBI.
  • Nationalised/PSU bank: branch accounts consolidated at HO. Private banks: centralised accounting; no concept of mandatory branch audit under RBI guidelines. Listed PSU and private banks follow SEBI LODR.
  • s.30(1) BR Act: auditor must be qualified to be auditor of companies.
  • SCAs: four or more CA firms jointly (size and Board decision). SBA: a single firm. Appointment letter to SCAs: tenure, other central auditors, previous auditors, procedural requirements (acceptance letter, declaration of fidelity and secrecy, restriction on other work), work division (later), scope incl. special reports/certificates.
  • Appointing authority: banking company → shareholders at AGM; nationalised bank → its Board of Directors. In either case, prior RBI approval.

Examiner's twist: "The private bank's audit committee wants branch auditors for 40 branches; the auditor says RBI requires it." Trap — no mandatory branch audit concept for private banks (centralised accounting). Second trap: "Board of the banking company appointed the auditor with RBI approval" — wrong body (AGM for a banking company); RBI approval alone does not cure it.

3 Conducting the audit — five stages

StageContents (SM)
I Initial considerationsAcceptance and continuance (engagement risk before acceptance); declaration of indebtedness; internal assignments bar; terms of engagement (SA 210); communication with previous auditor (Clause 8, Part I, First Schedule); planning; engagement team.
II UnderstandingBank and environment incl. internal control; accounting process; risk management process (oversight by TCWG, identify/measure/monitor risks, control activities, monitoring activities, reliable information systems).
III Risk assessmentROMM (SA 315); fraud risk incl. money laundering (SA 240; KYC–AML guidelines); specific risks (banking industry, IT); risks of outsourcing.
IV ExecutionEngagement team discussion; responses to assessed risks (SA 330); overall audit strategy (SA 300); audit planning memorandum; audit materiality; going concern.
V ReportingIndependent auditor's report; LFAR; reports on any other matter to bank, regulator or Government.
  • Declaration of indebtedness: written confirmation that auditor/firm/partners/family members are not declared wilful defaulters by any bank/FI — in addition to the s.141 disqualification declaration (indebtedness above the limit).
  • Internal assignments: audit firms should not take statutory audit while associated with internal assignments in the bank during the same year.
  • Audit planning memorandum: describe expected scope/extent; highlight significant issues and risks; evidence that engagement risk, pervasive risk, specific risks were addressed.

Examiner's twist: "List the components of an effective risk management system" (Stage II) vs "steps in risk assessment" (Stage III) vs "what the planning memorandum achieves" (Stage IV). Same case, three different lists. Trigger "TCWG approve written policies" → risk management system; "money laundering, outsourcing" → risk assessment; "scope and extent, significant issues" → planning memorandum.

4 IT environment and internal audit

  • Overall review of IT environment and CBS: at HO level — SCAs' responsibility. Branch auditors usually have no access to IT policy; on SCA guidance they review/analyse data through CBS, test controls and transactions at branch and share results with SCAs.
  • Key security controls: authorised, accurate, complete data; restart without distortion after failure; no unauthorised program changes; access rights match job responsibilities; segregation while giving access + activity log review; parameter/user-level changes authenticated; manually computed charges authorised; exception reports authorised and verified daily; account masters/balances changed only by authorised staff; all HO-authorised GL codes exist; GL balances tally with subsidiary ledgers.
  • Central Audit & Inspection Department (headed by Chief Audit Executive): RBIA per RBI framework; identifies branches for revenue audit; appoints concurrent auditors, fixes scope, reviews their work.
  • RBIA risk assessment: identify inherent business risks (business risk) → assess control effectiveness (control risk) → level and direction of risk → risk matrix for the branch.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — controls, CRR/SLR

5 Internal controls in selected areas

AreaMust-write control (SM)
GeneralFrequent unannounced staff transfers; one person's work always checked by another (officer); arithmetic accuracy proved daily; all bank forms (cheque books, DD/PO books, traveller's cheques, forex cards) with one officer, issue and stock verified by another; mail opened by responsible officer, signatures on advices checked with signature book; signature book and telegraphic code book with responsible officers; insurance; financial powers defined; surprise inspections by internal audit.
CashJoint custody of two officers; daily check + periodic full count by an officer other than cash handlers; cashier no access to customer ledger and day book; counterfoils signed by cash officer besides receiving cashier; payment only after voucher passed and posted; large receipts/payments verified by senior officer/BM; surplus cash to currency chest daily.
Clearing (CTS)Branch must call or e-mail the customer for any inward clearing cheque of ₹5 lakh and above (auditor checks on test basis); drawer's signature verified by staff, else liability is the paying bank's; unpaid outward cheques returned to customer address or customer informed.
Bills for collectionDocuments received and registered by responsible officer; credit only after realisation (or advice from collecting branch/agent); bills sent to another branch of the same bank must not appear twice in the consolidated BS — entries reversed at year end.
Bills purchasedTitle documents properly assigned; adequate margin; immediate action if bill unpaid on due date; irregular accounts reported to HO; discount apportioned between two years.
Loans and advancesSanction only after creditworthiness and proper authority; documents complete before disbursement; adequate margin; securities in joint custody; registration in bank's name; accounts within DP and sanctioned limit — additional temporary limit up to 20% of existing limit for maximum 90 days; over-limit/unapproved security accounts reported to management/HO; each advance account reviewed at least once a year.
Demand draftsSignatures checked with signature book; every DD confirmed by advice to paying branch; unconfirmed DD or no credit → immediate inquiry.
Inter-branchAdjust only on advices from other branches (not on entries in statements); unresponded entries (especially debits) → prompt central action.
Credit cardsScreening with credit appraisal; strict control over storage and issue; merchant confirms unutilised limit with bank if settlement exceeds a specified % of card limit; prompt reporting by merchants; reimburse merchants after validity check; immediate debit to customer; regular statements; follow-up of overdue; bulletins to merchants on defaulting cards; periodic review incl. doubtful amounts and provision.

Examiner's twist (TYK 13 type): small branch, peon opens all e-mails, no signature book, one staff holds all bank forms carefully, "short of staff so no checking". Answer: violations of general controls; mail must be opened by a responsible officer; forms with one officer, issue/stock verified by another; shortage of staff is not a valid excuse — fraud risk; report it. Illustration in SM: same stock statement figures month after month = control lapse (not checked/entered by responsible officer).

6 CRR, SLR and DTL SM TYU 2

  • CRR: specified minimum fraction of total deposits kept as cash/deposit with central bank. Requirement for banking companies in BR Act 1949; for scheduled banks in RBI Act 1934. Rate per RBI Master Circular.
  • SLR: liquid assets (gold, cash, approved securities) to be maintained before lending; s.24 BR Act. SCAs verify SLR compliance on odd dates in different months of the year, not being Fridays. Report goes to top management and RBI.
  • Procedures: branches send weekly trial balance on Fridays → consolidated at HO for each reporting Friday; SCA asks branch auditors to verify trial balances of selected dates and cash; check consolidation, valuation of securities; Form A return (CRR) and Form VIII (SLR) on sample basis; regional consolidation countersigned by Regional Manager; state number of unaudited branches and that returns of unaudited branches were relied upon; Automated Data Flow (ADF) now used.
  • Foreign currency conversion: USD, GBP, JPY, Euro at RBI reference rate (FBIL); other currencies via New York closing rate to USD.
Excluded from DTL (selected)Included in DTL (selected)Trigger words → which
Paid-up capital, reserves, P&L credit balance; loans from RBI; refinance from EXIM Bank, NHB, NABARD, SIDBI; bills rediscounted with eligible FIs; net income tax provision; DICGC claims received pending adjustment; ECGC on invoking guarantee; ad hoc settlements from insurers pending court; court receiver; net unrealised gains on derivatives in trading book; non-refundable fees received in advance; liabilities under Bankers' Acceptance Facility; part recoveries from bad/doubtful debts; amounts in sundry deposits for import bills pending final rates; unadjusted link-branch balances for agency business; margins held for funded facilities.Net credit balance in branch adjustment accounts (credit entries outstanding over 5 years taken gross); interest accrued on deposits each reporting fortnight whether or not booked; cash collateral under collateralised derivatives (outside liabilities); overseas borrowings as liabilities to others, gross; adverse balance in Nostro mirror account = liability to others."owned funds / refinance / part-recovery / margin for funded facility" → exclude. "not yet booked / branch adjustment credit / Nostro mirror adverse" → include.

Exempt categories to check in DTL computation: eligible credit (EC) and outstanding long-term bonds (LB) for infrastructure and affordable housing; eligible incremental FCNR(B) and NRE deposits of maturity 3 years and above.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — verification of assets

7 Cash, balances with RBI/other banks, call money

  • Objective: existence and completeness at BS date. Cash physically verified close to BS date incl. foreign currency, cash in ATMs and cash deposit machines; tally with cash register/CBS.
  • Balance with RBI: confirmation + reconciliation; watch unresponded cash transactions, revenue items needing adjustment/write-off, other entries in RBI statement unresponded for over 15 days.
  • Other banks: no debit for charges or credit for interest outstanding; no cheques outstanding in clearing; bills/outstation cheques realised later; large inter-bank transactions near year end for window dressing. Foreign balances at closing rate.
  • Call money: proper authority, HO limits incl. inter-bank call money limits; borrower certificates/call loan receipts; tally with GL; subsequent repayment. Call loans made cannot be netted against call loans received.

8 Investments Asked: Jan 2026 Q6(b)

  • Objective: existence, ownership, valuation + compliance with statutory/regulatory parameters (non-compliance has direct and material effect). Investment policy read with RBI Master Circular; must separately state objectives for own account and on behalf of customers.
  • Segregation: dealing (front office), settlement/monitoring (mid office), accounting (back office).
  • Reconciliation: physical verification; confirmations of BRs issued by counterparty banks; SGL balance with Public Debt Office; control of BRs issued by the bank. BRs outstanding unduly long → written explanation from management. No BR for G-sec transactions where SGL facility exists. Independent confirmations under SA 505; if not received, alternative procedure e.g. bank staff download holdings from E-Kuber (RBI's CBS) in auditor's presence.
  • Securities in nominee names: transfer deeds signed + declaration that held on behalf of bank.
  • Classification: entire portfolio in HTM, HFT, AFS; category decided at acquisition by competent authority (Board, ALCO, Investment Committee); shifting AFS → HTM duly approved by the Board.
  • NPI: interest/principal due and unpaid for more than 90 days → do not reckon income + provide for depreciation.
  • Income: check where income is received electronically; TDS certificates system; profit/loss on sale; disclose change in valuation method.
  • Dealings for others: prior approval; income recorded; fiduciary breach liability. Separate accounts for own, PMS clients, others (incl. brokers); PMS investments separately audited by external auditors.
  • Half-yearly review of portfolio (as on 30 September and 31 March): operational aspects, policy amendments, compliance certification. Internal auditors do concurrent audit of treasury; results placed before CMD once a month; internal audit reports not sent to RBI.
  • Dividend (TYK theory Q1): accrual only if declared by the company at its AGM and right to receive established (AS 9); dividend declared after year end → no accrual. Interest on G-secs/bonds with pre-determined rates → accrual if paid regularly and not in arrears; also on securities guaranteed by Central/State Government.

Examiner's twist: a long story about treasury dealers' performance, then "comment on the transfer of ₹X crore securities from AFS to HTM approved by ALCO". Score: classification at acquisition by Board/ALCO/Investment Committee, but AFS → HTM shifting needs Board approval — ALCO approval alone is not enough. Do not mix with the 90-day NPI rule.

9 Advances — approach and procedures

  • Third Schedule classification three ways: nature (CC, OD, term loans, bills purchased/discounted), security (tangible assets, bank/Government guarantees, unsecured), place (in India/outside India); in India also by sector (priority, public sector etc.).
  • Evidence sought: amounts outstanding at BS date; due to the bank; duly documented; no unrecorded advances; valuation basis appropriate and reflects recoverability; disclosure/classification per GAAP and law; adequate provisions per RBI norms, AS and GAAP.
  • IC procedures: credit appraisal; sanction within delegated authority; documentation before disbursement; terms of sanction and end use (term loans); existence/enforceability/valuation of securities, registration; adverse features like unauthorised over-limit drawings; review/renewal system; DP from stock/book-debt statements per sanction letter; RBI prudential norms and loan policy.
  • Substantive: verify CBS master data (instalments, EMI, rate, tenure); every customer tagged under a single customer ID; large advances fully, others on sample; obtain SMA-1 and SMA-2 lists to select problem accounts; accounts with adverse remarks by concurrent auditors/inspection/RBI; restructured accounts (additional provision required); quick/early mortality: advance turning NPA within 12 months of sanction; interest completeness; analytics.
  • Recovery: periodic statements, latest financial statements, security inspection reports, borrowers' auditors' reports where exposure exceeds Board cut-off.

Beyond the SM (practical): stock audit reports are the first place a branch auditor finds DP errors; the SM itself asks the auditor to read them.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — IRAC verification

10 Asset classification, income recognition, provisioning (bank) Asked: Sep 2025 Q4(a)(i)–(ii); May 2026 MCQ Q14

  • SM sends the student to Intermediate Ch 12 for provisioning rates; this chapter gives the audit approach. Auditor downloads RBI Master Circular + circulars issued after it up to audit date.
  • Norms are minimum: where higher provision is needed because of threat to recovery, higher provision must be made.
  • s.15 BR Act (banking companies, nationalised banks, SBI, RRBs): no dividend until adequate provision for bad debts is made to the auditor's satisfaction.
  • Both SBA and SCA are responsible for confirming asset classification, income recognition and provisioning.
SituationSM rule

Examiner's twist (May 2026 MCQ, TYK 12): limit renewal due long ago, 180 days passed by 31 March, account regular and within DP, borrower's audited FS delayed (auditor died), renewal letter issued 10 April before audit report signed. Answer: standard — renewal after BS date reflects BS-date condition → AS 4 adjusting event; substance over form. Trap option: "NPA because not renewed on 31 March". Consortium (TYK 5): lead bank certifies "performing" but the member's own record shows interest not serviced for two quarters → each bank classifies on its own record of recovery; reverse interest; NPA. TYK 1: five regular accounts with poor loan appraisal → no change in classification (accounts performing); report appraisal weakness in LFAR.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — NPA sale, fixed and other assets

11 Sale / purchase of NPAs SM TYU 10, 14

Seller bank — checkBuyer bank — check
Board policy (assets that may be sold, norms, valuation, accounting, delegation); only NPAs that remained NPA at least 2 years in its books; without recourse (buyer takes entire credit risk); no legal/operational risk retained; cash basis, entire consideration upfront; never at contingent price; bank has not bought back an NPA it originally sold; removed from books on transfer. Price below NBV (book value less provisions) → shortfall to P&L of that year. Price above NBV → excess provision not reversed; used for shortfall/loss on sale of other NPAs.Purchased NPAs follow provisioning for their classification status in buyer's books; all recoveries first adjusted against cost; only recovery above cost recognised as profit; 100% risk weight on NPAs purchased from other banks for capital adequacy.

Examiner's twist (TYK 14): table of accounts with "FY from which NPA" and amounts; sale in April 2024 → only those NPA from before April 2022 can go; the 2022-23 account is out. A numerical MCQ will ask "amount eligible for sale" — add only accounts with 2 full years as NPA. Trap: counting by financial-year labels without checking the 2-year span at the sale date.

12 Fixed assets

  • Third Schedule: Premises (wholly/partly owned for business incl. staff residences) and Other fixed assets (furniture, fixtures, vehicles, computers, office equipment). Leased and intangible assets shown separately (AS compliance).
  • s.9 BR Act: no immovable property (however acquired, incl. in satisfaction of claims) held for more than 7 years from acquisition, unless required for own use (incl. staff use).
  • Branch auditor: title deeds at HO → written representation from branch management, mention to SCA in report and in LFAR; written representation that no immovable property other than for own use is included. Premises under construction shown separately. Revaluation basis and surplus/deficit per AS; impairment.
  • Transfers between branches: accumulated depreciation also transferred. Intangibles: RBI circulars + AS 26. Leased assets: AS 19. Impairment: AS 28. Group like items — safe deposit vaults not clubbed with office equipment or theft alarm. HO: consolidated schedule consistent; depreciation on gross block, especially computer hardware/software.

13 Other assets

  • Inter-office adjustments: reconciled centrally; report year-end status, dates up to which reconciled, number and amount of outstanding entries (debits and credits separately).
  • Interest accrued: only interest collectible in normal course (AS 9 uncertainty principle); overdue bills purchased/discounted specially considered.
  • TDS: certificates collected, originals to HO; Form 26AS vs ITR at HO.
  • Stationery and stamps: only exceptional items (bulk security paper written off over a period) at cost; physical verification of security stationery; branch auditor comments on IC in LFAR.
  • Non-banking assets acquired in satisfaction of claims: held for disposal; verify documents (agreement, court order, award); legal title; disputes → AS 29; s.9 holding period; recorded at lower of NBV of the advance or NRV of asset acquired.
  • Others: suspense (old entries, provision/write-off), prepaid expenses, interest-free staff advances, loans to officers and relatives (sanctioned by next higher authority; Board informed of large/senior-officer loans), security deposits, sundry debit balances on Government account (age-wise, reasons for large variations).

14 Capital, CRAR, stress testing, Basel III

  • CRAR = Eligible total capital funds ÷ (Risk-weighted assets + off-balance-sheet items) × 100. RBI: minimum 9% on an ongoing basis. Uniform capital adequacy method for SCBs (except RRBs) from 1992; Master Circular on NCAF.
  • Tier I: mainly share capital and disclosed reserves — highest quality, fully available to cover losses. Tier II: certain reserves and some subordinated debt — lower loss-absorption.
  • Illus 6 risk weights: secured housing loans up to ₹75 lakh 50% (subject to conditions); above ₹75 lakh 75%; commercial real estate 100%. ₹60 lakh housing loan → RWA 30 lakh.
  • Stress testing: all commercial banks except RRBs and LABs to have Board-approved stress-testing framework (adequate capital and buffers in adverse conditions).
  • Basel accord adopted 1988 (Basel Committee of G-10). Basel III objectives: (a) improve ability to absorb shocks; (b) improve risk management and governance; (c) strengthen transparency and disclosures.
  • Capital verification: opening from last audited BS; increase in authorised capital → special resolution and MOA; paid-up → prospectus, registrar's report, bank statements, ROC returns.
  • Reserves: statutory reserve, capital reserve, share premium, revenue and other reserves (incl. investment fluctuation reserve) — opening, additions, deductions separately; check transfer of prescribed % of profit to statutory reserve or exemption documents; foreign branches — foreign laws.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — liabilities, reporting

15 Deposits, borrowings, other liabilities, contingent liabilities

  • Objective for liabilities: all known liabilities recorded and disclosed at proper amounts.
  • CASA: KYC on sample of accounts opened in year; SB accounts only for individuals, HUF, specified institutions (trusts, educational) — not for business; current accounts for all types; banks generally pay no interest on current accounts; debit balances in current accounts shown under advances, not netted; inoperative/dormant = no transaction for over 2 years — revival with proper authority; check withdrawals where balance fell significantly vs last year.
  • Term deposits: receipts serially issued and accounted; bulk deposits (currently ₹2 crore and above for SCBs) at correct rate; premature closure penalty deducted; RD sample.
  • FCNR: opened by NRIs only as term deposits; permissible debits/credits; converted at HO-notified rate; difference to P&L; interest on 360-day year. NRE repatriable; NRO not repatriable except current income (conditions).
  • Window dressing: OD given near BS date, parked as deposits, further advances against them → both inflated, reversed after year end → consider qualification in main report besides other reporting. Interest accrued but not due shown in "Other liabilities and provisions", not added to deposits.
  • Borrowings (in India from RBI, other banks, other institutions; outside India): confirmations, SA 505; "refinance" is borrowing, "rediscount" is not; call borrowings authorised; inter-office transactions are not borrowings; branch borrowings authorised by HO.
  • Other liabilities: bills payable controls (standard printed forms, unused forms with responsible officer, secret code, signatures checked, TT/DD confirmed immediately, debit advice by paying branch); inter-office adjustment credit balance shown here; provisions against advances/investments are deducted there, not shown here.
  • Contingent liabilities heads: claims not acknowledged as debts; partly paid investments; outstanding forward exchange and derivative contracts; guarantees on behalf of constituents (in/outside India); acceptances, endorsements and other obligations; other items. Bills for collection separately. Lump-sum disclosure not acceptable (TYK 8).
  • Management representations (8): all OBS transactions recorded, per procedure, documented; all contingent liabilities disclosed; no crystallised liabilities in contingents; estimates per AS 29; guarantees on behalf of directors — party's own resources, no loan to meet them, no contravention of s.20 BR Act; justification for non-disclosure of remote items.
  • Procedures: non-fund facilities only to regular constituents — else concurrence of existing bankers and inquiry into financial position; LC payments only on documents conforming to LC; comfort letters considered; trade-credit guarantee period matches credit period from shipment date; guarantee register — expired guarantees excluded; counter-guarantees and margins; claims → AS 29 provision. Bills for collection: bills drawn on other branches of the bank excluded; register; subsequent realisations; commission debited on realisation; no income accrued on bills outstanding at BS date.
  • Illus 5: SWIFT messages misused with collusion, never in core system, no security → thousands of crores loss → recording and disclosure of contingent liabilities matter even if they never sit on the main BS.

16 Auditor's report, LFAR, other reports SM TYU 3

  • Nationalised bank — report to Central Government stating: transactions within bank's powers; explanations/information obtained and satisfactory; BS full and fair, exhibits true and fair view; returns from offices/branches adequate; P&L shows true balance of profit/loss; any other matter to be brought to Central Government's notice.
  • Report format: SA 700, 705, 706, 710, 720. Disclose number of unaudited branches and their advances, deposits, interest income and interest expense.
  • Banking company: also s.143 matters. CARO 2020 not applicable to a banking company (s.5(c) BR Act). Rule 11 CAAR 2014 still applies: pending litigations; foreseeable losses on long-term contracts incl. derivatives; delay in IEPF transfer; intermediary/ultimate beneficiary representations (and nothing contrary noticed); dividend compliance with s.123; audit trail (edit log) operated throughout year, not tampered, preserved.
  • LFAR: required by terms of appointment of auditors of public sector, private and foreign banks (and their branches). Branch LFAR = questionnaire (cash, balances with banks, investments, advances, deposits etc.) given by SBAs to SCAs → consolidated at HO → SCAs give bank LFAR to management → placed before ACB with action taken/proposed → copy of LFAR + agenda note with Board views/directions to RBI within 60 days of LFAR submission by statutory auditors.
  • Other reports: adequacy and operating effectiveness of IFC over financial reporting; LFAR; compliance with SLR; treasury operations per RBI directions.
  • Fraud: s.143(12) reporting to Central Government; RBI circular (committee on legal aspects of bank frauds) on professional conduct, non-disclosure of customer information, fraud reporting. Auditor evaluates the system as a whole, not each transaction; instance noticed in normal duties → report to RBI and Chairman/MD/CEO of the bank. SA 250: duty of confidentiality overridden by statute/law/courts.
  • SCA reports and certificates: reconciliation of securities (own + PMS); compliance with prudential guidelines on investment transactions; IRAC compliance; serious irregularities needing immediate attention (s.143(12)); custody of unused BR forms; CRAR and ratios in notes; Ghosh Committee (frauds) and Jilani Committee (internal control/inspection/credit) compliance; adverse credit-deposit ratio in rural areas; ALM; corporate governance (listed); interest subsidy/subvention claims.
Look-alikeKeyTrigger words → which
Main audit reportOpinion on FS; SA 700/705/706; s.143; to Central Govt for nationalised bank"true and fair", "within powers", "unaudited branches"
Rule 11 CAARSpecific statements even when CARO does not apply"audit trail", "IEPF", "pending litigation"

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 1 — concurrent audit, ACB, revenue leakage

17 Concurrent audit SM TYU 3, 7, 9

  • Examination contemporaneous with transactions; not a pre-audit; verifies authenticity in the shortest time after occurrence; like internal audit. Done by bank's own inspection staff (not in operations) or external CA firms; RBI guidelines.
  • Scope fixed uniformly for whole bank by Central Inspection & Audit Department in consultation with ACB; weight to high-risk, large-value transactions; detailed scope approved by ACB. Coverage of business/branches left to Head of Internal Audit with ACB prior approval; risk-based; random sample checks of sufficiently large samples.
  • System = part of bank's early warning system; bank reviews effectiveness once a year.
  • Areas: cash, investments, deposits, advances, foreign exchange, housekeeping, others. Forex: bills negotiated under LCs; FCNR/NR debits-credits; remittances; forward contract extension/cancellation authorised and charges recovered; Nostro balances within limits; overbought/oversold positions reasonable; dealing room per RBI/HO; Nostro/Vostro reconciliation. Housekeeping incl. detect and prevent revenue leakage by scrutiny of income and expense accounts; debits to income accounts authorised; staff accounts; returned cheques/bills.
  • Appointment: own staff (experienced, trained, senior, independent of the branch) or external — bank's discretion. External concurrent auditor: tenure with the bank normally not more than 5 continuous years; with a branch/business unit not more than 3 years. Serious lapses → appointment terminated, inform RBI and ICAI. Remuneration and terms by ACB.
  • Reporting: structured format; major deficiencies by special note to branch/controlling office immediately; quarterly review to ACB; subject-wise reporting and annual appraisal to ACB; discuss with branch manager before report; frauds → I&A Dept (HO), Chief Vigilance Officer, branch manager (if not involved).

18 Audit Committee of the Board (ACB)

  • Set up per RBI; gives directions and oversees total audit function; reviews internal inspection/audit (system, quality, effectiveness, follow-up); reviews appointment and remuneration of concurrent auditors and quality of their work; periodic meetings with concurrent and statutory auditors.

19 Revenue leakage — what to verify in advances Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ Q5 (numerical)

Area (SM TYK 2 answer)Leakage arises when
Interest rateRate in system differs from sanction letter; interest not applied at prescribed intervals.
Processing feeNot charged on new sanctions and renewed limits per bank circular/manual.
Penal interestNot charged for non-submission of stock statements as stipulated in CC sanction.
Export financeOverdue interest on export bills purchased; packing credit for the overdue period.
LCs / guaranteesCharges/commission on LCs (and guarantees) not recovered per circular.

Examiner's twist: a numerical MCQ lists five "shortfalls" and asks the total leakage. Remove items that are not leakage: interest not applied on an NPA (income only on realisation — correct treatment); no interest on current account credit balances (banks generally pay none). Compute only rate difference, unrecovered fees/commission, penal and overdue interest. Read whether the rate is per annum and the period in months/days.

Examiner's twist (TYK 3, stock auditor): stock auditor certified DP ₹12.05 crore; a week later inspector finds mouldy rice worth ₹1.50 crore held for 15 months. Answer: stock auditor not diligent — role is not only counting quantities; should have opened bags on test basis; damaged stock excluded from DP; DP shown is not proper.

RBIACentral Audit & Inspection DeptBusiness risk × control risk → risk matrix"risk matrix", "frequency of audit"
Stock auditStock auditor for large CC accountsQuantity + condition + DP computation"godown", "DP certified"
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 2 — NBFC definition, registration, SBR

20 NBFC — definition and principal business test

  • s.45-I(f) RBI Act: (i) financial institution which is a company; (ii) non-banking institution which is a company whose principal business is receiving deposits under any scheme or lending in any manner; (iii) other institutions notified by RBI with prior Central Government approval.
  • NBFC activities: loans and advances, acquisition of shares/stock/bonds/debentures/securities, leasing, hire-purchase, insurance, chit business. Excludes companies whose principal business is agriculture, industrial activity, purchase/sale of goods (other than securities), services, or sale/purchase/construction of immovable property. Residuary NBC: principal business receiving deposits.
  • Principal business (both tests, from last audited BS): financial assets > 50% of total assets (netted off by intangible assets) AND income from financial assets > 50% of gross income. One test alone → not NBFC (Satyam Pvt Ltd: assets test met, trading income majority → not NBFC). Singh Ltd: financial assets 374 / (620 − 12 = 608) and income 68 / 118.75 → both > 50% → apply for CoR.

21 Registration, NOF Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ Q7

  • s.45-IA: no NBFI business without (a) CoR from RBI and (b) NOF of ₹25 lakh or such other amount not exceeding ₹100 crore as RBI notifies (different amounts for different categories allowed).
  • New applicants: minimum NOF ₹10 crore (earlier ₹2 crore); ₹2 crore continues for NBFC-P2P, NBFC-AA, NBFCs without public funds and without customer interface; NBFC-IFC and IDF-NBFC ₹300 crore.
Existing NBFCCurrent NOFBy 31 March 2025By 31 March 2027
NBFC-ICC₹2 crore₹5 crore₹10 crore
NBFC-MFI₹5 crore (NE region ₹2 crore)₹7 crore (NE ₹5 crore)₹10 crore
NBFC-Factor₹5 crore₹7 crore₹10 crore
  • Post 9 January 1997 NBFCs cannot commence business without CoR → auditor obtains copy of CoR or application form; checks dual requirement (registration + NOF).
  • Exempt from registration (regulated elsewhere): housing finance institutions (NHB), merchant banking cos, stock exchanges, stock-broking/sub-broking cos, venture capital fund cos (SEBI); Nidhi cos (MCA); insurance cos (IRDA); chit cos (Chit Funds Act 1982 s.2(b)); notified microfinance cos; securitisation and reconstruction cos; mutual benefit cos; core investment cos; AIF cos. CICs with assets below ₹100 crore, and those ₹100 crore+ not accessing public funds, exempt.

22 Scale-based regulation (announced 22 Oct 2021, effective 1 Oct 2022)

LayerWho (SM)
Base (NBFC-BL)Non-deposit NBFCs with assets below ₹1,000 crore; always: NBFC-P2P, NBFC-AA, NOFHC, NBFCs without public funds and customer interface.
Middle (NBFC-ML)All deposit-taking NBFCs irrespective of size; non-deposit NBFCs with assets ₹1,000 crore and above; SPDs, IDF-NBFCs, CICs, HFCs, NBFC-IFCs. SPD and IDF-NBFC always Middle. NBFC-D, CIC, IFC, HFC → Middle or Upper, never Base.
Upper (NBFC-UL)Specifically identified by RBI (parameters + scoring); top ten eligible NBFCs by asset size always Upper.
Top (NBFC-TL)Ideally empty; RBI may move an Upper Layer NBFC if systemic risk increases substantially.
  • ICC, MFI, Factor, Mortgage Guarantee Cos → any layer per SBR parameters. Government-owned NBFCs → Base or Middle; not Upper until further notice.
  • From 1 Oct 2022: references to NBFC-ND → NBFC-BL; NBFC-D and NBFC-ND-SI → NBFC-ML or UL. Existing NBFC-ND-SI with assets ₹500 crore to below ₹1,000 crore (other than those in Middle) → NBFC-BL.
  • TYK: P2P platform → Base. Sugam Housing Finance (₹800 crore, not identified Upper) → Middle (asset size irrelevant for HFC); CARO 3(xvi)(b) reporting on housing finance activity without valid CoR.
  • Bank vs NBFC (4): NBFC cannot accept demand deposits (some accept term deposits); not part of payment and settlement system, cannot issue cheques drawn on itself; no DICGC deposit insurance; no minimum priority-sector requirement.

Examiner's twist: case gives a ₹350 crore NBFC taking public deposits and a ₹1,200 crore HFC "not identified by RBI", and asks the layer of the smaller one. Size test (₹1,000 crore) applies only to non-deposit, non-specified NBFCs; deposit-taking → Middle regardless of size; HFC → Middle unless identified Upper.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 2 — prudential norms

23 Capital requirements Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ Q6

  • Scale Based Regulation Directions 2023: minimum capital ratio (Tier 1 + Tier 2) not less than 15% of aggregate risk-weighted assets on BS and risk-adjusted value of off-BS items. Tier 1 not less than 10% at any time (except NBFC-MFI and NBFCs primarily in gold-jewellery loans).
  • Gold-jewellery lenders (such loans 50% or more of financial assets): Tier 1 at least 12%.
  • Tier 1 (non-BL): owned fund less investments in shares of other NBFCs and shares/debentures/bonds/loans/deposits with subsidiaries and group companies in excess of 10% of owned fund; plus perpetual debt instruments issued by non-deposit NBFCs each year up to 15% of total Tier 1 as on 31 March of previous year. NBFC-BL cannot include perpetual debt in Tier 1 or Tier 2.
  • Tier 2: preference shares other than those convertible into equity; revaluation reserves at 55% discount; general provisions (incl. standard asset provision) and loss reserves not attributable to specific assets, up to 1.25% of risk-weighted assets; hybrid debt; subordinated debt; excess perpetual debt — total Tier 2 not exceeding Tier 1.
Risk weight (SM table, clear items)%
Cash and bank balances incl. FDs and CDs with banks; approved securities; TDS, advance tax (net); interest due on G-secs; claims on Central Govt; direct exposure to State Govt securities; Central Govt-guaranteed claims; loans fully secured against deposits held; loans to staff0
Bonds of public sector banks; State Govt-guaranteed claims not in default (or in default up to 90 days)20
Assets covering PPP and post-COD infrastructure projects in commercial operation for over one year50
FDs/CDs/bonds of public financial institutions; shares, debentures, bonds, CPs of all companies and MF units; stock on hire (NBV); ICDs; other secured loans considered good; bills purchased/discounted; leased assets, premises, furniture (net); other assets; State Govt-guaranteed claims in default over 90 days100
Consumer credit (excluding housing, education, vehicle, gold-jewellery and microfinance/SHG loans); credit card receivables125

Value of each asset × risk weight; gross values used for minimum capital ratio.

24 Income recognition, classification, provisioning Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ Q8; May 2026 Q3(a)

  • Income on NPAs (interest, discount, hire charges, lease rentals, other charges) recognised only when actually realised; income recognised before the asset became NPA and remaining unrealised reversed. Classification and provisioning norms below apply to every applicable NBFC except NBFC-MFIs.
  • Classes: standard, sub-standard, doubtful, loss. No upgrade merely on rescheduling unless upgrade conditions met.
  • NPA: interest overdue / term loan instalment / demand or call loan / bill / other current assets / dues from sale of assets or services / lease rental and HP instalment overdue for more than 180 days (adjusted by glide path); all facilities of the same borrower NPA when one is NPA. Lease/HP accounts classified on record of recovery.
NPA glide path (SM)Norm
By 31 March 2024> 150 days overdue
By 31 March 2025> 120 days overdue
By 31 March 2026> 90 days overdue

Glide path does not apply to NBFCs already required to follow 90 days.

ClassNBFC-BLNBFC-ML and aboveProvision (SM)
Sub-standardNPA up to 18 monthsNPA up to 12 months10% of total outstanding (general provision). Also renegotiated/rescheduled/restructured assets until one year of satisfactory performance.
DoubtfulSub-standard for more than 18 monthsSub-standard for more than 12 monthsUnsecured portion (not covered by realisable security with valid recourse) 100% + secured portion: up to 1 year 20%; 1–3 years 30%; above 3 years 50%
LossIdentified by NBFC, its internal/external auditor or RBI inspection, not written off; or non-recoverable owing to erosion of security, no security, fraud/omission of borrowerWrite off; if retained, 100% of outstanding
StandardNo default; no problem; no more than normal business riskNBFC-BL 0.25%; NBFC (not UL) 0.40% of outstanding — not reckoned for net NPA; shown as "Contingent provisions against standard assets", not deducted from advances

Examiner's twist (Jan 2026 MCQ type): outstanding ₹3.8 crore, realisable security ₹2.2 crore, NBFC-ML, NPA since a date that makes it doubtful < 1 year → 1.6 × 100% + 2.2 × 20% = ₹2.04 crore. Traps: using BL's 18-month cut-off for an ML company (would leave it sub-standard at 10% = ₹0.38 crore); applying % to the whole outstanding; using bank rules. May 2026: management "watch-list" or rescheduling cannot override the overdue-based classification; income on NPA only on realisation.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Banks and NBFCsSM Ch 14 Unit 2 — audit procedures, frauds, checklist

25 NBFC audit procedures

  1. Ascertain the business — first step: study MOA/AOA (objects wide), business policy, Board/committee minutes, discuss with top management; corroborate independently with financial results. Principal business decides classification and public deposit limits.
  2. Evaluate internal control — especially effectiveness of recovery system and periodic review of advances (absence → NPAs rise to alarming levels).
  3. Registration with RBI — s.45-IA; CoR copy or application; post 9.1.1997 no business without CoR; NOF. Public-deposit NBFCs invest a prescribed % in liquid assets; quarterly return signed by an officer within prescribed time.
  4. Public Deposit Directions 2016 — correct classification; ceiling on public deposits linked to credit rating by approved CRA; on rating change, deposits adjusted within time and RBI informed in writing. Downgrade below minimum investment grade (ICC or Factor): (a) stop accepting new and renewing deposits immediately; (b) existing deposits run off to maturity; (c) report to RBI Regional Office within 15 working days; no renewal without depositor's express voluntary consent. Check interest not above ceiling; brokerage/commission/reimbursement within limits; written application in company's form with all particulars (depositor category — shareholder, director, promoter, public); deposit register and receipts; repayment on due dates and reasons for defaults; liquid-asset securities kept in safe custody with the designated scheduled commercial bank and certificate; returns filed in time; non-acceptance → Board resolution that it neither accepted nor will accept public deposits; group holding investment cos → Board resolution naming group companies, will not trade in them, no public deposits.
  5. Prudential norms — income recognition, income from investments, AS, accounting for investments, classification, provisioning, capital adequacy, no loans against own shares, concentration norms; Board policy for demand/call loans; NPA income only on realisation (not accrual); last year's NPAs still NPA unless regularised per directions.

26 Frauds in NBFCs (classification for uniform reporting, based on IPC)

  • (a) misappropriation and criminal breach of trust; (b) fraudulent encashment through forged instruments, manipulation of books, fictitious accounts, conversion of property; (c) unauthorised credit facilities for reward or illegal gratification; (d) negligence and cash shortages; (e) cheating and forgery; (f) irregularities in foreign exchange transactions; (g) any other type.
  • (d) and (f) reported as fraud if fraudulent intention suspected/proved. Even without suspected intent, report as fraud: cash shortage more than ₹10,000; cash shortage more than ₹5,000 detected by management/auditor/inspecting officer and not reported on occurrence by the persons handling cash. NBFCs with overseas branches report frauds there too (Master Direction – Monitoring of Frauds in NBFCs 2016).

27 Audit checklist (example: NBFC-ICC)

  • Physically verify shares and securities; certificate where lodged with bank/institution; depository confirmation; confirmation from approved intermediary for securities lent/borrowed; same type securities returned with all corporate benefits; fees on securities lending verified.
  • No loans against security of own shares. Dividend income on shares and MF units on cash basis; accrual option only when declared at AGM and right to receive established. Interest on corporate bonds/debentures with pre-determined rate paid on time and not in arrears → accrual required.
  • Broker's notes vs stock-exchange quotations; Board minutes for purchase/sale; current vs long-term classification by Board resolution or management certificate; valuation per prudential norms, provision for decline in market value; investments in subsidiaries/group cos and basis of price; unquoted debentures/bonds treated as term loans/credit facilities for income recognition and classification; AS 13.
  • Loans: proper sanction and terms; agreements and collateral; net worth of borrower/guarantor; confirmations; bill discounting records; single/group borrower exposure limits; appraisal and follow-up system; recovery trends; classification and provisioning adequacy. (Checklist illustrative, not exhaustive.)

Examiner's twist: "Approved securities of a deposit-taking NBFC are kept in the company's own strong room, certified by its CFO" → wrong: must be in safe custody with the designated scheduled commercial bank (Jan 2026 MCQ). P2P twist: platform must not lend on its own; verify CoR, Board policies on participant eligibility, pricing, grievances, arrangements, RBI reporting.

28 NBFC auditor's reporting — RBI Directions

  • s.45MA RBI Act (from 13.12.1974): auditors of NBFCs or other non-banking cos accepting public deposits inquire whether deposit particulars were furnished to RBI under Chapter IIIB; if not satisfied, report to RBI the aggregate deposits; also include that report in the s.143 report.
  • NBFC Auditor's Report (Reserve Bank) Directions 2016 — apply to every auditor of an NBFC (s.45-I(f)); additional/separate report to the Board of Directors, besides s.143 report (and besides CARO).
GroupApplies toStatements (SM)Trigger words → which
AAll NBFCs(I) engaged in NBFI business and meeting principal business criteria (RBI press release 8 April 1999) — has it obtained CoR (business without CoR is an offence under Chapter V); (II) holder of CoR — entitled to continue as per principal business criteria on 31 March of the year; (III) meets required NOF"CoR", "50% test", "NOF"
BAccepting/holding public depositsDeposits (incl. unsecured NCDs to public, shareholder deposits of public company) within limits; excess regularised; no deposits without minimum investment grade rating; CRAR in return correctly determined and meets minimum; rating effective and deposits within CRA limit; no violation of restrictions; defaults in repaying interest/principal; prudential norms (income, AS, classification, provisioning, concentration); liquid assets (s.45-IB) and designated bank details intimated (NBS 3); return on deposits (DNBS 01) and quarterly prudential return in time; branches/agents per directions"public deposits", "rating", "liquid assets", "DNBS 01"
