Information processing — general IT controlsProgram change controls; access to programs/data; new releases of packaged software; system software/utilities that could change data without audit trailPhysical control "authorisation for access to programs and data files"
Physical controlsPhysical security of assets; authorisation for access to computer programs and data files; periodic counting and comparison with recordsSafeguarding (structure)
Segregation of dutiesDifferent people authorise, record, keep custodyInternal check (wider arrangement of duties)

Entity's risk assessment process — circumstances that create or change risk Asked: MTP May 2026 S-I 5(b)

  • (a) changes in operating environment; (b) new personnel; (c) new or revamped information systems; (d) rapid growth; (e) new technology; (f) new business models, products, activities; (g) corporate restructurings; (h) expanded foreign operations (forex); (i) new accounting pronouncements.

Examiner's twist: a story lists "new ERP, two new foreign markets, new finance staff, smart appliances, rapid growth, policy changes" and asks for circumstances giving risk → map each to the SM label (c, h, b, e/f, d, i) with one line on IC effect. An MCQ on "edit checks and access authorisation" is information processing (Sep 2025 key D), not physical controls.

Internal check Asked: Jan 2026 Q2(b)

  • Arrangement of book-keeping and staff duties so that no one person can carry through a transaction and record every aspect; built-in device; work of each proved independently or complementary.
ListItemsTrigger words
Objectives of internal check (6)Detect errors and frauds with ease; avoid and minimise commission; increase efficiency of staff; locate responsibility area; protect integrity by proper scrutiny; prevent misappropriation of cash and falsification"objectives of internal check"
Effectiveness depends on (4)Clarity of responsibility; division of work (with rotation); standardisation; appraisal (periodic review, audit flow chart)"considerations for efficient system"
General conditions (10)(i) no single person complete control, every action reviewed; (ii) rotation of duties; (iii) leave at least once a year; (iv) custody of assets ≠ access to books; (v) accounting control per class of assets plus periodical inspection; (vi) mechanical devices for cash; (vii) budgetary control, deviations reconciled; (viii) year-end inventory taking: trading suspended if possible, staff from several sections; (ix) powers distributed judiciously and reviewed; (x) periodical verification of accounting records"violations", "principles of adequate internal check", "purchase head sanctions payments", "storekeeper keeps records"
  • Segregation (structure): authorisation; execution; custody; recording. Rotation: fraud not undetected for long; no vested interest; work kept up to date; employee careful.
  • Evaluation SM points: SOPs; enterprise risk management; segregation (buyer not involved in receiving or passing bills; BRS by someone other than bank book keeper); job rotation in sensitive areas; delegation of financial powers document; IT-based controls.
  • Effect on risk (Jan 2026 ask): violations weaken control environment and control activities → control risk high; promoter's relative, long tenure, no records = conditions for fraud and error → inherent risk of misstatement in purchases, payables, inventory rises; combined → RMM high → lower acceptable DR, more substantive work.

Review and evaluation of IC — ICQ, checklist, flow chart

  • Design first, then implementation (exists and used). Improper design may be a material weakness or significant deficiency. Manual elements suit large/unusual/non-recurring transactions, hard-to-predict errors, changing circumstances, monitoring automated controls.
  • Review enables auditor to (i) decide reliance on the system; (ii) locate weak areas to adjust NTE of substantive and compliance procedures (wider debtor confirmation; surprise cash count after routine verification).
  • Continuing client: review changes; comprehensive review at interval of, say, 3 years. ICQ necessary in first year; good practice to re-issue every third year even without change. Preliminary evaluation assumes controls operate as described.
PointInternal control questionnaireCheck listFlow chart
NatureLarge number of detailed questions per area; "No" usually = weaknessInstructions/questions on main control objectives; reminder to auditor; auditor initialsGraphic, horizontal flow of documents and activities across sections; bird's eye view
Answered byCompany executives (in practice filled in meetings, executive initials)Auditor/audit staffDrawn by auditor after review
Weakness signal"No" shows weakness but not its significanceSpecific statement where weakness may be materialGaps visible (unnumbered salesmen advices, no credit sanction, no pricing check)

Reporting IC weaknesses; SA 265 Asked: Sep 2025 Q6(a); Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q6

  • Material weakness (SM): absence of adequate controls on flow of transactions increasing possibility of errors and frauds. Communicate to management/audit committee timely, preferably in writing (letter of weakness / management letter): lists weaknesses and suggestions; states only weaknesses noticed in audit, examination not designed to determine adequacy of IC; reference document; may minimise legal liability (Re S.P. Catterson & Ltd.). Letter does not absolve the duty to qualify.
  • Deficiency (TYU 5 ans): control unable to prevent or detect and correct timely, or necessary control missing. Significance depends on likelihood and potential magnitude, not on actual misstatement; susceptibility of asset to loss is a factor (uninsured assets).
  • SA 265 adds no responsibility beyond SA 315/330 for understanding IC or testing controls.
To whomWhatForm
TCWGSignificant deficiencies, timelyIn writing: description and potential effects; context — (i) purpose was opinion on FS; (ii) IC considered to design procedures, not to opine on IC effectiveness; (iii) limited to deficiencies identified and of sufficient importance
Management (appropriate level)(a) significant deficiencies communicated/to be communicated to TCWG — unless inappropriate to tell management directly; (b) other deficiencies not communicated by others and of sufficient importance(a) in writing; (b) as per SM, of sufficient importance to merit attention

SA 330 — responses

  • Overall responses at FS level; further procedures at assertion level responsive to risk — consider reasons (IR, CR) and obtain more persuasive evidence the higher the risk.
  • Tests of controls when: auditor expects controls to operate effectively (intends to rely); or substantive procedures alone cannot provide sufficient evidence. More reliance → more persuasive evidence.
  • Irrespective of risk: substantive procedures for each material class, balance, disclosure.

Examiner's twist: "Auditor's responsibility on internal control" MCQ (Jan 2026) mixes ICFR reporting, significant deficiencies to TCWG and a CARO clause — the CARO option is the odd one. In descriptive, the story is about a fraud loss; ask is "what should the auditor communicate and how" → SA 265 writing + three context statements; mention letter of weakness does not replace qualification.

Frameworks (one line each)

  • Indian benchmark: Appendix 1 of SA 315 (IFC over FR). COSO 1992 — 5 components, 17 principles, objectives: operations, reporting, compliance. CoCo (CICA) — 20 criteria: purpose, commitment, capability, monitoring and learning. COBIT (ISACA) — 34 processes, 210 control objectives, 4 domains. Turnbull — directors review IC at least annually. SOX 404 — auditor gives two opinions: ICFR and FS.

05Ch 05 — Audit Evidence

Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(b) (SA 505/500 responses received via management) · Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q2 (stock count not observed, immaterial, alternatives done → no modification) · Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q3 (undisclosed related party loan) · May 2026 Q1(a) (SA 580 + SA 501 legal counsel, SA 705) · May 2026 Q4(a) (SA 500 management's expert — CTO). Also RTP May 2025 Q10 (positive/negative), MTP Jan 2026 2(a) (refusal to confirm + KAM), RTP Jan 2026 Q11 (count instructions).

Selective examination vs audit sampling (opening case)

PointSelecting specific itemsAudit sampling (SA 530)
WhatItems above a threshold (₹25 lakh), suspicious items, all RPTs, litigationLess than 100% of items such that all sampling units have a chance of selection
ProjectionResults cannot be projected; no evidence on remainderMisstatements in tests of details are projected to population
RiskNon-sampling risk — inappropriate procedures, misinterpretation, failure to recognise misstatementSampling risk — conclusion differs from what 100% testing would give

SA 500 — Audit Evidence Asked: May 2026 Q4(a)

  • Evidence includes accounting records and other information; obtained by risk assessment procedures and further procedures (tests of controls, substantive procedures).
  • Relevance (logical connection with purpose/assertion; affected by direction of testing) and reliability (source, nature, circumstances, controls). Generalisations have exceptions — even external evidence may be unreliable.
  • Information produced by entity → evaluate reliability, completeness, accuracy. Inconsistency or doubt → modify/add procedures and consider effect on other aspects.
  • Management's expert: to extent necessary, having regard to significance — (a) evaluate competence, capabilities, objectivity; (b) understand the work; (c) evaluate appropriateness as evidence for the assertion — findings (consistency, reflected in FS), significant assumptions and methods, significant source data. Sources of info on expert: prior experience, discussions, qualifications, published papers.
  • Threats: self-interest, advocacy, familiarity, self-review, intimidation. Intimidation threat may be less for an engaged expert than an employed one. Employee-expert cannot ordinarily be regarded as more objective than other employees. Engaged expert: discuss interests (financial, business/personal, other services) and safeguards.
  • MCQ (SM): objectivity likely lesser if expert is employed by the entity. BBNS: actuary assumed 65 retirement age while only a proposal exists → assumption inappropriate → auditor may qualify.

Examiner's twist: CTO with 20 years' service, profit-linked incentives, no market benchmarks, team lacks engineering skill (May 2026). Ask is "rely on CTO?" → SA 500 objectivity threat (employee + incentive) → cannot rely without more; consider auditor's expert (SA 620), SA 540 significant-risk evaluation, range; if unresolved, SA 705.

SA 501 — Inventory, litigation, segment Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q2; May 2026 Q1(a); RTP Jan 2026 Q11

  • Objectives: existence and condition of inventory; completeness of litigation and claims; presentation and disclosure of segment information.
  • Inventory material → attendance at count unless impracticable: (i) evaluate management's instructions and procedures; (ii) observe count procedures; (iii) inspect inventory; (iv) test counts; plus procedures over final inventory records.
  • Count at a date other than FS date → procedures on changes between count date and FS date.
  • Third-party custody (material) → confirmation of quantities and condition, and/or inspection or other procedures. Doubt on third party's integrity (DGGI raid) → other procedures instead of or in addition: attend/arrange another auditor to attend count; another auditor's or service auditor's report on third party's IC; inspect documents (challans, e-way bills).
  • Count instructions to correct (SM TYK 6 / RTP): supervisor from finance, not storage in-charge; no movement during count; counters independent of storage, separate counting and recording, serially numbered count sheets, tags; layout plans; quantities by measurement (gauges), not capacity; WIP stage-wise, not plant capacity; damaged/obsolete segregated; sheets signed.

Unable to attend due to unforeseen circumstancesMake or observe some physical counts on an alternative date and perform procedures on intervening transactions"road blocked for a month", "flight cancelled", "agitation" (TYU 2)

  • Litigation — identify by: inquiry of management and others incl. in-house legal counsel; minutes of TCWG and correspondence with external counsel; legal expense account. Not by inquiry of the opposite party (SM MCQ: inquiry of Mr. X is not a procedure).
  • RMM on litigation or procedures indicate material litigation → seek direct communication with external legal counsel through a letter of inquiry prepared by management and sent by the auditor (TYU 3 — auditor's own letterhead letter is not proper). Letter of specific inquiry: list of litigation; management's assessment and estimate; request to confirm reasonableness and add if list incomplete. Meeting may be sought if significant risk, complex or disagreement.
  • Counsel refuses / management refuses permission and no alternative evidence → modify opinion (SA 705) (SM integrated case MCQ 2 — key (a), not disclaimer by default, not withdrawal).
  • Written representation: all known actual or possible litigation and claims disclosed and accounted for — representation alone is not sufficient (May 2026 Q1(a) with SA 580).
  • Segment: understand methods used by management and perform analytical procedures. Matters: inter-segment sales, transfers, charges and eliminations; comparisons with budgets (operating profit % of sales); allocation of assets and costs; consistency with prior periods and disclosure of inconsistencies.

SA 505 — External Confirmations Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(b); RTP May 2025 Q10; MTP Jan 2026 2(a)

  • Procedure: determine information to confirm; select appropriate confirming party; design requests; send requests incl. follow-ups.
  • Management refuses to allow a request: (1) inquire reasons, seek evidence on validity and reasonableness; (2) evaluate implications on RMM incl. fraud and on NTE of other procedures; (3) alternative procedures. Refusal unreasonable → communicate with TCWG and consider implications on the audit and the auditor's opinion. First response = (1) (SM integrated case MCQ 3, key (d)).
  • "Request may be used as proof of liability" (MTP Jan 2026) — test validity of that reason; unilateral write-back of liabilities to other income is itself a risk area.
PointPositive confirmationNegative confirmationTrigger words
Response soughtConfirming party replies directly to the auditor whether it agrees or disagrees (contrast with SM definition of negative; wording as in RTP May 2025 Q10)Replies only if it disagrees (SM)"respond only if there is a disagreement" → negative
PersuasivenessMore persuasiveLess persuasive; non-response does not show receipt or accuracy; parties more likely to respond when information is not in their favour
Use as sole substantive procedure—Only when all: RMM low and relevant controls effective; large number of small, homogeneous items; very low exception rate expected; no known circumstance making recipients disregard"1,800 small dealer balances", "one old ₹20 lakh balance" (Star Ltd — not suitable for that item)

ResultWhat the auditor shall do
Doubt on reliability (received indirectly, appears not from intended party, GST registrations cancelled)Obtain further audit evidence; if unreliable → evaluate implications on RMM incl. fraud and NTE of other procedures. All responses carry risk of interception, alteration, fraud — paper or electronic.

Examiner's twist: SM MCQ — confirmation "signed by the plant manager" is unreliable (not the appropriate confirming party). Sep 2025: responses collected by the company's staff and handed over → received indirectly → doubt on reliability → further evidence and fraud-risk evaluation; not "accept since signed".

SA 510 — Initial Audit Engagements: Opening Balances

  • Initial engagement: prior period FS not audited, or audited by predecessor.
  • Objectives: whether opening balances contain misstatements materially affecting current FS; whether accounting policies consistently applied and changes properly accounted, presented, disclosed.
  • Procedures: read most recent FS and predecessor's report; prior closing balances correctly brought forward; opening balances reflect appropriate policies; perform procedures on opening balances; misstatement found → communicate with management/TCWG.
  • Inventory: current closing-stock work gives little evidence on opening — observe current count and reconcile back; procedures on valuation of opening items; gross profit and cut-off (TYU 6 — prior year unaudited).
  • SM MCQ: "ascertaining whether predecessor attended the count" is not an SA 510 procedure.
ConclusionOpinion
Unable to obtain evidence on opening balancesQualified / disclaimer
Opening balances misstated, not properly accounted/presented/disclosedQualified / adverse
Policies not consistently applied or change not properly accountedQualified / adverse
Predecessor's modification relevant and material to current periodModify current report

SA 530 — Audit Sampling

  • Objective: reasonable basis to draw conclusions about the population.
  • Design: purpose of procedure and characteristics of population; sample size reduces sampling risk to acceptably low level; each unit has a chance of selection.
  • Procedure not applicable to an item → replacement item. Unable to apply designed procedure → treat as deviation (tests of controls) or misstatement (tests of details).
  • Investigate nature and cause; anomaly → procedures for high degree of certainty it is not representative; project misstatements (tests of details); evaluate results and whether sampling gave reasonable basis. If not: request management to investigate and adjust; or tailor NTE (extend sample, test alternative control, modify substantive procedures).
  • TYU 7: erroneous conclusion that controls are less effective / that misstatement exists → affects efficiency (additional work).

SA 550 — Related Parties Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-I Q3

  • Expands SA 315, SA 330, SA 240 for RPs. Objectives: understand RPTs to recognise fraud risk factors and conclude whether FS achieve true and fair presentation / are not misleading; evidence that RPTs are properly identified, accounted, disclosed.
  • Requirements: risk assessment; understanding relationships and transactions; alertness when reviewing records; share RP information with the team; assess RMM; respond.
  • Team discussion: independent identification (MBP-1, MCA data); skepticism; SPEs, complex structures, off-balance sheet; management override.
  • Written representation: all RPs and RPTs disclosed to auditor; properly accounted and disclosed. TCWG: significant matters on RPs. Documentation: names of RPs and nature of RPTs.
SituationAuditor shallTrigger words
Previously unidentified / undisclosed RP or RPTDetermine whether circumstances confirm existence; promptly communicate to team (reassess RMM); request management to identify all transactions with new RP; inquire why controls failed; substantive procedures; reconsider other undisclosed RPs; intentional non-disclosure → SA 240, re-evaluate reliability of management's responses and representations"agency paid ₹20 crore, promoter infuses ₹5 crore", "director's relative's firm", "loan not disclosed"
Significant RPT outside normal course of businessInspect contracts: business rationale (or lack), terms consistent with management's explanations, properly accounted and disclosed; evidence of authorisation and approval"sold blanket machinery to a floor-covering company" (TYU 8 — no rationale; may be fraud risk factor even at market price)
  • Fraud risk factors (Case Study 2): incentive/pressure (financial difficulty, lifestyle, equity to fund); opportunity (foreign partner's dependence, SRA model, dominant MD → override). SM MCQ: "floating of a new SPV itself" is not a fraud risk factor; most important red flag = undisclosed RP relationships to siphon funds.
  • Likelihood of misappropriation established → reassess reliability of evidence; professional and legal reporting responsibilities; consider withdrawal where legally permitted; discuss reasons with management/TCWG.

Examiner's twist: Jan 2026 MCQ — RP loan not disclosed: first assess whether it causes a material misstatement and ask management to disclose; jumping to "adverse opinion" or "report to ROC" is the trap. In a descriptive, a "market rate" sale between group companies still needs business rationale from the RP's perspective.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Review of Financial InformationSM Ch 10

10Ch 10 — Review of Financial Information

Asked: Sep 2025 Q3(c) SRE 2410 objective (4) · Sep 2025 Q6(b) SRE 2410 written representations (5) · Jan 2026 Q2(c) SRE 2400 analytical procedures (4) · May 2026 Q4(b) SRE 2400 written reps not provided (5) · Nov 2024 Q1(c) SRE 2400 preconditions (4)

1 What is a review · 1.1 Standards on Review Engagements

  • "Review" = limited assurance engagement: engagement risk reduced to a level acceptable in the circumstances but greater than for reasonable assurance; evidence at least sufficient for a meaningful level of assurance (likely to enhance intended users' confidence).
  • Fewer procedures than audit, yet sufficient appropriate evidence is still gathered; limited conclusions drawn. Subject matter = historical financial information, as in an audit.
  • Two SREs: SRE 2400 Engagements to Review Historical Financial Statements; SRE 2410 Review of Interim Financial Information Performed by the Independent Auditor of the Entity. SREs are Engagement Standards → firm is subject to SQC 1.

SRE 2400 vs SRE 2410 — the first filter in every review question

PointSRE 2400SRE 2410Trigger words → which one
Who performsPractitioner who is not the auditor of the entity's FSIndependent auditor of the entity (audit client)"also statutory auditor", "quarterly results of listed co. (SEBI Reg 33)" → 2410; "lender / contract / not the auditor" → 2400
SubjectHistorical FS (general or special purpose framework)Interim financial information: complete or condensed set for a period shorter than the financial year"quarter", "half-year", "interim" + auditor → 2410
Assurance wordingLimited assuranceReduce to a moderate level the risk of an inappropriate conclusion"moderate level" → 2410 objective
Main proceduresPrimarily inquiry and analytical procedures; additional procedures if FS may be materially misstatedInquiries (primarily of persons responsible for financial and accounting matters), analytical and other review procedures"other review procedures" → 2410
UnderstandingObtain understanding of entity, environment and frameworkUpdates understanding from prior audit (reads prior audit/review documentation etc.)"already well-versed, update" → 2410
ReportPractitioner's report; Conclusion paragraph; reference to SRE 2400Review report; statement that review done per SRE 2410; membership no. + FRNBoth: UDIN required

2.1 SRE 2400 objectives

  • (a) Obtain limited assurance, primarily by inquiry and analytical procedures, whether FS as a whole are free from material misstatement → express conclusion whether anything has come to attention that FS are not prepared, in all material respects, per AFRF.
  • (b) Report on FS as a whole and communicate. When limited assurance cannot be obtained and a modified conclusion is insufficient → disclaim or, where appropriate, withdraw (if possible under law).
  • SM TYU 1: FS under a contract's reporting provisions (special purpose framework) can be reviewed under SRE 2400.

2.2 Acceptance — do NOT accept (unless required by law) if Asked: Nov 2024 Q1(c)

  • (a) Not satisfied: (i) rational purpose exists — no rational purpose if significant scope limitation, suspected inappropriate association of name, or law requires an audit; (ii) review is appropriate — may recommend audit, or compilation/other accounting service.
  • (b) Ethics incl. independence will not be satisfied. (c) Information likely unavailable or unreliable. (d) Doubt on management integrity likely to affect review. (e) Management/TCWG impose a scope limitation that will result in a disclaimer.

2.3 Preconditions Asked: Nov 2024 Q1(c)

  • (a) Framework acceptable (for special purpose FS: understand purpose and intended users).
  • (b) Management acknowledges responsibility for: (i) preparation per AFRF incl. fair presentation; (ii) internal control necessary for FS free from material misstatement (fraud or error); (iii) providing access to all information, additional information requested, unrestricted access to persons.
  • Not satisfied → discuss with management/TCWG → if no change, do not accept unless law requires; then engagement does not comply with SRE → no reference to SRE in report.
  • Discovered after acceptance → discuss and determine: (a) can it be resolved; (b) continue?; (c) whether and how to communicate in report.

2.4 Terms; materiality; procedures

  • Terms agreed prior to performing; recorded in engagement letter or other written form. Recurring: evaluate revision, remind. Request to change to a no-assurance engagement → only with reasonable justification; new terms recorded in writing.
  • Materiality: determined for FS as a whole; judgment of materiality is the same regardless of level of assurance; revise if information would have caused a different amount.
  • Design inquiry and AP (a) to address all material items incl. disclosures; (b) focus on areas where misstatements are likely. May read a significant contract — other procedures do not change the limited-assurance objective.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Review of Financial InformationSM Ch 10

Inquiry vs analytical procedures in a review (SRE 2400) Asked: Jan 2026 Q2(c)

Inquiry — importanceAnalytical procedures — assist the practitioner in
Often the principal source of evidence about management intent; if support for intent is limited, look at management's past history of carrying out intentions, stated reasons, and ability to pursue the action. Professional skepticism in evaluating responses. Also helps obtain/update understanding to identify areas of likely misstatement. Inquiries cover: estimates, related parties, significant/complex/unusual transactions, fraud, subsequent events, going concern basis, commitments/contingencies, non-monetary or no-consideration transactions; may include meetings of owners/TCWG, regulator communications, non-financial data.(1) Obtaining or updating understanding of entity and environment to identify areas of likely misstatement; (2) identifying inconsistencies or variances from expected trends, values or norms (congruence with key data incl. KPIs); (3) providing corroborative evidence for other inquiries/AP; (4) serving as additional procedures when FS may be materially misstated (e.g. comparing monthly revenue and cost across profit centres/branches). Consider whether accounting data is adequate for AP.

Examiner's twist: The Jan 2026 question spent the story on "unlike an audit, a review relies on inquiry and AP" and asked only how AP assist. Write the four AP points, not the inquiry list. If asked "why inquiry is important", lead with "principal source of evidence about management intent".

Specific circumstances (SRE 2400)

  • Related parties: stay alert for undisclosed RPs; significant transactions outside normal course → inquire nature, RP involvement, business rationale.
  • Fraud / NOCLAR: communicate to appropriate senior management or TCWG; request management's assessment of effects; consider effect on conclusion; determine any duty to report outside the entity.
  • Going concern: inquire plans and feasibility and whether they will improve the situation; evaluate responses (basis for GC presentation / FS misstated or misleading); consider in light of all information.
  • Work of others: take steps to be satisfied that work of other practitioner/expert is adequate. Group review → procedures directed at group FS.

Additional procedures when FS may be materially misstated

  • Design additional procedures to (a) conclude matter not likely to cause material misstatement, or (b) determine it does cause material misstatement.
  • May be more detailed inquiry/AP, or other types: substantive tests of details, external confirmations.
  • SM receivables example: past-due debts, no allowance → inquiry → (a) not misstated: stop; (b) misstated: stop, conclude FS materially misstated; (c) still doubtful → request analysis of after-date receipts. If neither (a) nor (b) reached → scope limitation → no unmodified conclusion.

Subsequent events (SRE 2400)

  • Events up to report date needing adjustment/disclosure → request management to correct.
  • No obligation to perform procedures after report date. Fact known after report date but before issue → discuss; determine if amendment needed; inquire how management will address. Report already given and no amendment → notify management and TCWG not to issue to third parties; issued anyway → act to prevent reliance.

Written representations — SRE 2400 vs SRE 2410 Asked: Sep 2025 Q6(b); May 2026 Q4(b)

SRE 2400 (non-auditor reviewer)SRE 2410 (auditor reviewing interim)
Mandatory: (a) management fulfilled responsibility for preparation incl. fair presentation and gave all information and access; (b) all transactions recorded. Also disclosed: related parties; frauds/suspected frauds; NOCLAR; going concern information; subsequent events adjusted/disclosed; material commitments/contingencies; material non-monetary/no-consideration transactions. Public statements by management required by law may cover some reps.Management: (a) responsible for design and implementation of IC to prevent and detect fraud and error; (b) interim FI prepared and presented per AFRF; (c) uncorrected misstatements immaterial (summary attached); (d) disclosed frauds/suspected frauds; (e) disclosed results of its fraud risk assessment; (f) disclosed actual/possible NOCLAR; (g) disclosed significant subsequent events up to date of review report.
  • Reps not provided (SRE 2400): (a) discuss with management/TCWG; (b) re-evaluate integrity and effect on reliability of oral/written reps and evidence; (c) take actions incl. effect on conclusion.
  • Disclaim or withdraw (if permitted by law) if: (a) doubt on integrity makes reps unreliable; or (b) the responsibility reps (preparation of FS; all transactions recorded) are not given.

Examiner's twist: Sep 2025 Q6(b) said management "merely stated" interim FI is prepared per AFRF — that is only rep (b). The ask is the full SRE 2410 list (a)–(g). Do not write the SRE 2400 list for an interim review by the auditor; the "uncorrected misstatements immaterial" and "fraud risk assessment results" points belong to 2410.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Review of Financial InformationSM Ch 10

Evidence, scope limitation and withdrawal (SRE 2400)

  • Evidence insufficient → extend work or other procedures; if impracticable → effect on report or on ability to complete (e.g. management member unavailable for inquiries on significant matters). Discuss limitation with management/TCWG.
  • Inability to perform a specific procedure ≠ scope limitation if evidence is obtained by other procedures.
  • Withdraw when all three: (a) scope limitation imposed by management after acceptance → no sufficient evidence; (b) possible effects material and pervasive; (c) withdrawal possible under law.
  • Forming conclusion: consider uncorrected misstatements (this and previous year's review), qualitative aspects incl. management bias; for fair presentation framework, overall presentation and whether FS achieve fair presentation.

SRE 2400 conclusions — the modification grid

SituationMaterial, not pervasiveMaterial and pervasiveKey words in the paragraph
FS materially misstatedQualified: "except for the effects of the matter(s)…"Adverse: "due to the significance… FS do not give a true and fair view / are not prepared…"Basis para: quantify effects (unless impracticable — say so); explain misstated narrative; describe omitted info and include it where practicable
Unable to obtain sufficient appropriate evidenceQualified: "except for the possible effects…"Disclaimer: unable to obtain evidence; "does not express a conclusion"Basis para: reasons for the inability
Unmodified (no modification needed)"Based on our review, nothing has come to our attention that causes us to believe that the FS do not give a true and fair view…" (fair presentation framework)"…are not prepared, in all material respects, in accordance with the AFRF" (compliance framework)Negative form of words
  • Headings: "Qualified Conclusion / Adverse Conclusion / Disclaimer of Conclusion"; basis paragraph immediately before the conclusion paragraph.
  • EOM (matter presented/disclosed, fundamental to understanding; only if evidence shows it is not likely materially misstated) — immediately after conclusion. OMP — matter not presented/disclosed, relevant to understanding of review/responsibilities/report.
  • Other reporting responsibilities → separate section "Report on Other Legal and Regulatory Requirements" after "Report on the Financial Statements".

SRE 2400 report elements (a)–(m)

  • Title (independent practitioner, review engagement) · addressee · introductory para (FS identified, policies referred, FS reviewed) · management responsibility (preparation, IC) · special purpose FS: purpose/intended users; choice of frameworks → responsibility to determine acceptability · practitioner's responsibility incl. reference to SRE · description of a review and its limitations · Conclusion para (with framework) · modified: conclusion + basis paragraphs · reference to ethical requirements · date · signature · place.
  • Three limitation statements: (1) limited assurance engagement; (2) procedures primarily inquiries of management and others and AP, and evaluation of evidence; (3) procedures substantially less than an audit → no audit opinion.
  • Date no earlier than sufficient evidence obtained, incl. all statements and notes prepared and those with authority have taken responsibility.
  • Documentation (sufficient for an experienced practitioner with no previous connection): NTE of procedures; results and conclusions; significant matters and judgments; who performed and date; who reviewed, period and extent; discussions with management/TCWG; how inconsistencies were addressed.

2.8 Audit vs Review

PointAuditReview
AssuranceReasonableLimited (lower than reasonable)
ProceduresElaborate: tests of controls + substantive proceduresFewer: primarily inquiry and AP
ConclusionsReasonable conclusions on sufficient appropriate evidenceLimited conclusions on sufficient appropriate evidence
OutputOpinion, positively wordedConclusion, negatively worded

Examiner's twist: "A review gathers less evidence, so sufficient appropriate evidence is not required" is false — SM: review gathers sufficient appropriate evidence; it is the level of assurance and volume of procedures that differ.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Review of Financial InformationSM Ch 10

3.1 SRE 2410 objective Asked: Sep 2025 Q3(c)

  • Enable the auditor to express a conclusion whether, on the basis of the review, anything has come to attention that interim FI is not prepared, in all material respects, per AFRF.
  • Inquiries + analytical and other review procedures to reduce to a moderate level the risk of an inappropriate conclusion.
  • Differs significantly from an audit: no basis for true and fair opinion; not designed for reasonable assurance; may bring significant matters to attention but does not provide all evidence required in an audit.

3.2–3.4 Terms, understanding, procedures (SRE 2410)

  • Terms ordinarily in an engagement letter: objective and scope, management's responsibilities, extent of auditor's responsibilities, assurance obtained, nature and form of report.
  • Update understanding (ordinarily): read prior year audit and prior interim review documentation; significant risks incl. management override; read latest annual and comparable interim FI; materiality for interim FI; corrected material and uncorrected immaterial misstatements of prior year; continuing matters like material weaknesses; current-year audit results; internal audit results; inquire management on fraud risk assessment, business changes, IC changes, preparation process and reliability of records. Recently appointed auditor (no annual audit yet) → obtain understanding for both annual and interim.
  • Ordinarily no tests of records through inspection, observation or confirmation. Procedures: read minutes; effect of prior modifications/adjustments; communicate with other auditors of significant components; inquiries (changes in policies, new transactions, uncorrected misstatements, business combinations, fair value assumptions, RPTs, commitments, contingent liabilities, debt covenants, transactions in last/first several days, fraud knowledge/allegations, NOCLAR); AP to identify unusual items; read interim FI.
  • Lawyer: ordinarily not necessary to send inquiry letter; direct communication appropriate if a matter raises doubt and lawyer may have information.
  • Agree/reconcile interim FI to general ledger or consolidating schedule and other data.
  • Subsequent events: inquire whether management identified all events up to the date of the review report; no other procedures needed for events after report date.
  • Going concern: inquire whether management changed its assessment; if doubt → inquire plans, feasibility, outcome; consider adequacy of disclosure. Ordinarily not necessary to corroborate feasibility of plans.
  • Matter questioning a material adjustment → additional inquiries/procedures (e.g. discuss sales terms with marketing/accounting; read the contract).
  • Evaluate uncorrected misstatements individually and in aggregate.

3.7 Accompanying (other) information

  • Read for material inconsistency: amendment needed in interim FI and refused → implications for report. Amendment needed in other information and refused → additional paragraph in report, or withhold report, or withdraw. Example: alternative earnings measures given excessive prominence.
  • Material misstatement of fact → discuss; consider whether to ask management to consult a qualified third party; refused → notify TCWG, obtain legal advice.

3.8 Communication ladder (SRE 2410)

StepMaterial adjustment neededFraud / NOCLAR
1Appropriate level of management, as soon as practicable (oral or written; oral → document)Appropriate level of management as soon as practicable; level depends on likelihood of collusion/management involvement
2No appropriate response in reasonable period → TCWGConsider reporting to TCWG; consider implication for review
3TCWG no appropriate response → consider (a) modify report; (b) withdraw from engagement; (c) resign from appointment to audit annual FS—

Examiner's twist: SM TYU 4 (CA Seerat): CFO and audit committee both silent. The scoring line is the three options including resigning as statutory auditor — that option exists only under SRE 2410 because the reviewer is the auditor. It does not exist in SRE 2400.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Review of Financial InformationSM Ch 10

3.9 SRE 2410 report contents

  • Title · addressee · identification of interim FI (each statement, date and period) · management responsibility ("preparation and fair presentation" if complete set of GPFS under fair presentation framework; otherwise "preparation and presentation") · auditor responsible for conclusion · review done per SRE 2410 + consists of inquiries and analytical and other review procedures · review substantially less in scope than an audit, no assurance of becoming aware of all significant matters, no audit opinion · conclusion (true and fair wording for complete GPFS under fair presentation; otherwise "not prepared, in all material respects") · date · place · signature with membership number · FRN. UDIN for both SRE 2400 and 2410.
  • SM integrated case (SEBI Reg 33, Ind AS 34): addressee Board of Directors; "Our responsibility is to express a conclusion"; SRE 2410; "substantially less in scope than an audit… might be identified in an audit"; negative-form conclusion.

3.10–3.12 Modified review report (SRE 2410)

SituationReportTrigger words
Departure from AFRF, not correctedQualified (explanatory para; nature and, if practicable, effects; include missing disclosure where practicable); adverse if so material and pervasive that qualification is inadequate"material adjustment should be made"
Scope limitation known before acceptanceDo not accept"preliminary knowledge"
Management imposes limitation after acceptanceRequest removal → refused → cannot complete; communicate reason in writing to management and TCWG; still communicate material-adjustment matters; if law requires a report → disclaim + reason (+ material adjustment matter)"management refuses access", "regulation requires a report"
Other limitation (not management)Ordinarily cannot complete; rare: confined, material not pervasive → "except for" in scope statement and qualified conclusion"records destroyed for one location"
Prior year audit qualified for scopeConsider whether limitation still exists and implications"last year's qualified opinion"
GC material uncertainty adequately disclosedAdd Emphasis of Matter paragraph (continued uncertainty also highlighted)"fire, uninsured, disclosed appropriately" (TYU 3)
GC material uncertainty not adequately disclosedQualified or adverse, with specific reference to the uncertaintyGAK Ltd (TYK 6)

Examiner's twist: "Can CA Reena give a clean report if disclosure is made?" — not "clean" in the sense of no paragraph: SRE 2410 says the auditor modifies the review report by adding an EOM paragraph. The conclusion itself stays unmodified; say both.

3.13 Other considerations · 3.14 Documentation

  • Terms include management's agreement that if a document says FI was reviewed by the auditor, the review report will be included. Not included → consider legal advice. Modified report omitted → legal advice + possibility of resigning as auditor.
  • Condensed interim FI presumes users have the latest audited FS (listed entities); otherwise discuss a statement "to be read with latest audited FS"; absent it → is it misleading?
  • Documentation sufficient to support conclusion and show compliance with SRE 2410 and law.

Beyond the SM (practical): the quarterly "limited review report" filed by listed companies under SEBI Reg 33 is an SRE 2410 report by the statutory auditor.

08Ch 8 — Specialised Areas (SA 800, 805, 810)

Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(a) SA 810 procedures (5) · Jan 2026 Q4(a) SA 810 report when audit opinion qualified / adverse (5) · May 2024 Q1(c) SA 810 applied criteria (4) · MTP Nov 2026 SA 805 receivables for drawing power (4)

SA 800 vs SA 805 vs SA 810 — which SA is asked

PointSA 800SA 805SA 810
Deals withAudit of FS under special purpose frameworkAudit of a single FS or a specific element, account or itemReport on summary FS derived from FS audited by that same auditor
EngagementAudit (reasonable assurance, SA 700 opinion)Audit; adapt all relevant SAsNot a fresh audit; procedures to compare/evaluate summary vs audited FS
Opinion wordingSA 700 (true and fair / present fairly / prepared in accordance)Form depends on framework; consider "presents fairly / true and fair" acceptability"consistent, in all material respects, with" or "a fair summary of" the audited FS, per applied criteria
Who may do itAny auditor (also the statutory auditor — TYU 1)Any auditor; harder if not auditing complete set (practicability)Ordinarily only the auditor of the underlying FS
Trigger words"cash basis", "regulator's provisions", "bond indenture / loan agreement / grant", "contract""cash flow statement only", "trade receivables schedule", "inventory", "net tangible assets", "bonus schedule""abridged", "summary", "derived from audited FS", "AGM brochure"
  • Element in a special purpose framework → apply both SA 800 and SA 805 (TYU 3). SA 805 does not apply to a component auditor's report for group audit. SA 800/805 do not override other SAs.

2.1 Special purpose framework

  • Framework designed to meet financial information needs of specific users (vs general purpose — common needs of a wide range of users). May be fair presentation or compliance framework.
  • Examples: cash basis and cash flow information for creditors; regulator's provisions; contract provisions (bond indenture, loan agreement, project grant).

2.2–2.3 SA 800 acceptance, planning, performance

  • Intended users' information needs = key factor in framework acceptability. Standards by an authorised/recognised organisation with transparent process → presumed acceptable; regulator-prescribed → presumed acceptable absent contrary indications; supplemented by law → SA 210 conflicts check. Contract framework → test the attributes of acceptable frameworks; relative importance is judgment (very prudent receivable allowances agreed by buyer and seller — not neutral, still acceptable for their needs).
  • SA 320: materiality based on intended users' needs. SA 315: understand significant interpretations of the contract (significant = another reasonable interpretation would give a material difference).
  • Management–users agreed threshold below which misstatements are not corrected → does not relieve the auditor from determining materiality per SA 320.
  • SA 260: persons overseeing special purpose FS may differ from TCWG for GPFS.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Specialised AreasSM Ch 8

2.4–2.6 SA 800 reporting

  • Apply SA 700. Contract basis → evaluate whether FS adequately describe significant interpretations of the contract.
  • Report also: (a) purpose and, if necessary, intended users (or refer to note); (b) management's choice of frameworks → responsibility for determining framework acceptable; (c) if KAMs communicated → SA 701 applies in its entirety; (d) SA 720 applies where a report to owners/similar stakeholders accompanies the FS ("similar stakeholders" includes the specific users); (e) OMP may refer to the report on GPFS (e.g. its Material Uncertainty Related to Going Concern section).
Alert vs restrictionRequired?Content
Alert on basis of accountingRequired — Emphasis of Matter paragraph in a separate section headed with "Emphasis of Matter" (SA 706)FS prepared under a special purpose framework; may not be suitable for another purpose
Restriction on distribution or useOptional — auditor "may consider it appropriate", subject to lawReport intended solely for specific users; not to be distributed to/used by others; alert para expanded and heading modified

Examiner's twist: A story about a loan-agreement audit, then "how will she ensure the report is not misused?" (TYU 2) → answer is restriction on distribution or use, with the draft para ending "Our opinion is not modified in respect of this matter". Students write only the alert; that is the half that was already in the draft.

3 SA 805 — single FS and elements Asked: MTP Nov 2026 S-II 1(c)

  • Single FS: e.g. cash flow statement. Element: element, account or item — e.g. trade receivables, cash and bank. Both include related notes.
  • SM examples of elements: receivables, allowance for doubtful debts, inventory, pension benefit liability, intangibles, IBNR claims; schedules of externally managed pension assets, net tangible assets, disbursements on lease property, profit participation or employee bonuses.
  • Acceptance: SA 200 compliance applies whether or not auditor audits the complete set. If not auditing the complete set → determine whether audit per SAs is practicable (less understanding of entity/IC; no evidence on general quality of records; may need further corroboration). Certain SAs may be disproportionate (SA 570 for a receivables schedule). Not practicable → discuss another type of engagement.
  • Framework acceptability includes whether presentation gives adequate disclosures for users to understand the information and effect of material transactions.
  • Planning/performing: adapt all relevant SAs. SA 240, SA 550, SA 570 relevant in principle even for one element (fraud, RPTs, going concern basis). Interrelated items → procedures beyond the element. Materiality may be lower than for complete set → affects NTE and uncorrected misstatements. May use evidence from the complete-set audit.
  • Form of opinion (agreed in terms per SA 210): factors for "presents fairly / true and fair" — whether framework is restricted to complete sets; whether the statement/element complies fully with relevant requirements with disclosures; whether extra disclosures or departures are needed; general acceptance in the jurisdiction.

Examiner's twist: MTP: bank wants a report on ₹18 crore receivables for drawing power; the auditor already audits the complete set. The ask is "planning and performing considerations" — not acceptance. Score: adapt all SAs; SA 240/550/570 relevant; interrelated items; lower materiality; may use evidence from the main audit but still plan for sufficient evidence on the element; separate opinion.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Specialised AreasSM Ch 8

3.6 SA 805 forming opinion and reporting

  • Apply SA 700 (and SA 800 when applicable), adapted. Going concern descriptions adapted. KAM: SA 700 requires KAMs per SA 701 for audits of complete sets of GPFS of listed entities. SA 720 applies where a report to owners accompanies the statement/element. Signed in personal name (and firm name), membership number, FRN.
  • With complete-set audit → separate opinion for each engagement. Published together → presentation must be differentiated; else ask management to rectify; do not issue until satisfied.
  • Report on complete set contains modified opinion, EOM/OMP, MURGC section, or uncorrected misstatement of OI → consider implications. Factors: nature of matter and relation to the element; pervasiveness; framework differences; period differences; time elapsed. (Qualification on receivables → likely implications for receivables audit; on long-term debt classification → less likely for P&L or receivables.) May refer in OMP even if no implications.
Adverse/disclaimer on complete set — can you give an unmodified opinion on…Answer
…an element, in the same reportNo — SA 705 does not permit (would contradict)
…an element, in a separate auditYes, only if (a) not prohibited by law; (b) report not published together with the adverse/disclaimer report; (c) element is not a major portion of the complete set
…a single FS (e.g. P&L), even separate and not published togetherNo — a single FS is deemed a major portion
Disclaimer on results of operations and cash flows + unmodified on state of affairs in the complete-set reportPermitted — disclaimer is not on FS as a whole

4 SA 810 — summary FS Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(a); Jan 2026 Q4(a); May 2024 Q1(c)

  • Summary FS: historical information derived from FS with less detail, still a structured representation consistent with the FS.
  • Accept ordinarily only if engaged to audit the underlying FS (else lacks knowledge).
  • Before accepting: (a) applied criteria acceptable; (b) management acknowledges responsibility to (i) prepare per applied criteria, (ii) make audited FS available without undue difficulty (or describe the law that says otherwise), (iii) include auditor's report in any document with summary FS that says auditor reported; (c) agree form of opinion.
  • Applied criteria factors: nature of entity; purpose of summary FS; information needs of intended users; whether criteria result in summary FS that are not misleading. Criteria set by recognised body or law → may presume acceptable; else developed by management (industry practice).
  • Criteria unacceptable or no agreement → do not accept unless law requires; then not "in accordance with SA 810"; mention in terms; consider effect on the main audit. Disclosure example title: "Summary financial statements prepared from the audited financial statements for the year ended March 31, 20XX".
  • "Without undue difficulty" factors: summary FS say from whom/where audited FS available; audited FS on public record; process for ready access.

4.3 SA 810 procedures (a)–(g) Asked: Sep 2025 Q5(a)

  • (a) Adequately disclose summarised nature and identify audited FS. (b) Not accompanied by audited FS → describe clearly from whom/where available, or the law. (c) Adequately disclose applied criteria. (d) Compare — agree with or can be re-calculated from audited FS. (e) Prepared per applied criteria. (f) Contain necessary information at appropriate aggregation so as not to be misleading. (g) Audited FS available without undue difficulty.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Specialised AreasSM Ch 8

4.4–4.5 SA 810 opinion and report

  • Unmodified: "consistent, in all material respects, with the audited FS, per applied criteria" or "a fair summary of the audited FS, per applied criteria". Law prescribes different wording → apply procedures; evaluate whether users might misunderstand; additional explanation cannot mitigate → do not accept (unless law requires; then no SA 810 reference).
  • Elements (a)–(m): title (independent auditor) · addressee (different from main report → evaluate: terms, nature of entity, purpose) · identification of summary FS (page numbers if in a larger document) · identification of audited FS · opinion · statement: summary FS do not contain all disclosures; reading them is not a substitute for reading audited FS and report · if required, statement that neither reflects events after the date of the audit report · reference to the audit report, its date and that an unmodified opinion was expressed · management responsibility (per applied criteria) · auditor responsibility (per SA 810) · signature, FRN, membership no., UDIN · date · place.
  • Date no earlier than (i) sufficient evidence incl. summary FS prepared and responsibility taken; and (ii) date of the audit report on audited FS. Dated later → state that summary FS and audited FS do not reflect subsequent events (TYU 5: 15 June / 15 July). No need for additional audit evidence on audited FS. Facts that existed at audit-report date found later → do not issue until SA 560 consideration completed.

4.6 SA 810 — what the summary report adds when the audit report is modified Asked: Jan 2026 Q4(a)

Audit report on audited FS containsSummary FS report — opinionSummary FS report — add
Qualified opinion, EOM, OMP, MURGC, KAM, or uncorrected misstatement of OI (and summary is consistent/fair summary)Unmodified opinion on summary FS possible(a) State that audit report includes that item; (b) describe: basis for qualification and effect on summary FS / the EOM-OMP-MURGC matter and effect / the OI misstatement and effect on information in the document
Adverse opinion or disclaimerNo opinion — inappropriate to express oneState audit report has adverse/disclaimer; describe its basis; state that as a result it is inappropriate to express an opinion on summary FS
(Summary FS itself) not consistent / not fair summary and management will not changeAdverse opinion on summary FS—

Examiner's twist: Three different "adverse" situations sit close together. Adverse on the audited FS → no opinion on summary FS. Summary FS itself inconsistent → adverse opinion on summary FS. SA 805: adverse on complete set → separate element opinion possible only with three conditions. Read whose report is adverse.

4.7–4.12 Other SA 810 rules

  • Restriction/alert in audit report → include similar restriction or alert in summary report.
  • Comparatives omitted → reasonable? effect if unreasonable. Comparatives reported by another auditor → include SA 710 matters.
  • Unaudited supplementary information not clearly differentiated → ask management to change; refused → explain in report that it is not covered.
  • Other information in document: read; material inconsistency → discuss; refused → appropriate action incl. implications for report.
  • Association: document says auditor reported but omits report → request inclusion; else prevent inappropriate association. Auditor not engaged on summary FS but referred to → reference only in context of audit report and no impression of reporting on summary FS; else request change/deletion or engagement; else advise disagreement and act.
  • SM case (mutual fund abridged FS under SEBI format): criteria may be presumed acceptable; responsibility para cites SA 810; opinion "fair summary of those FS, and in accordance with the accounting policies and standards specified in SEBI regulations and GAAP in India to the extent applicable"; KAM usually not an element of the summary report.

AAA · Twist notes · PFI and Other Assurance ServicesSM Ch 11

11Ch 11 — Prospective Financial Information and Other Assurance Services

Asked: Jan 2026 Q6(c) SAE 3400 duties (4) · May 2026 Q6(a) SAE 3400 matters for NTE of procedures (5) · MTP Nov 2026 SAE 3400 duties (4) · May 2025 Q1(c) SAE 3420 source and pro forma adjustments (4) · RTP May 2024 SAE 3402 type 2

1.1 Standards on Assurance Engagements

  • Subject matter other than historical FS: PFI, or non-financial matters like design and operation of internal control. SM: "level of assurance provided by these SAEs is moderate" (but see SAE 3402 and 3420 below — both stated as reasonable assurance).
  • SAE 3400 Examination of PFI · SAE 3402 Assurance Reports on Controls at a Service Organisation · SAE 3420 Assurance Engagements to Report on the Compilation of Pro Forma FI Included in a Prospectus.

2 SAE 3400 — PFI, forecast, projection

  • PFI: based on assumptions about events that may occur and possible actions by an entity; highly subjective; forecast, projection or combination (e.g. 1-year forecast + 5-year projection).
  • Scope: examination procedures for best-estimate and hypothetical assumptions. Not for PFI in general or narrative terms (e.g. MD&A), though procedures may suit. Examiner need not be the statutory auditor. Other SAs applied to the extent practicable.
  • Management responsible for preparation and presentation: identification and disclosure of PFI, basis of forecast, underlying assumptions.
  • Nature of assurance: evidence is future-oriented and speculative → no opinion on whether results will be achieved.
PointForecastProjectionTrigger words
AssumptionsBest-estimate: events management expects to take place and actions it expects to take as of the date preparedHypothetical assumptions (events/actions not necessarily expected), or a mixture of best-estimate and hypothetical"expects", "based on current trends" → forecast; "if", "assuming we win", "start-up", "major change in operations" → projection
Best-estimate definitionAssumption reflecting anticipated experience with no provision for risk of adverse deviationUses hypothetical ones alone or mixed with best-estimate"no provision for adverse deviation" → best-estimate
Auditor's test of assumptionsBest-estimate assumptions are not unreasonableHypothetical assumptions are consistent with the purpose; no reason to believe clearly unrealistic; all significant implications included—
SM exampleCFO expects 5% sales growth next quarter (market, supply, buying pattern, season)New product for expected market growth; new plant with 7-year profit/cash projections for term loan—
Report caveatActual results likely different; variation may be materialPlus: prepared for intended use using hypothetical assumptions; readers cautioned not to use for other purposes—

2.3 Can a CA be associated with PFI? (Clause 3, Second Schedule)

  • Misconduct if he permits his/firm's name to be used with an estimate of earnings contingent upon future transactions in a manner that may lead to the belief that he vouches for the accuracy of the forecast.
  • Allowed to prepare/review forecasts if report clearly states sources of information, basis of forecasts, major assumptions, and he does not vouch for accuracy. Same for projections.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · PFI and Other Assurance ServicesSM Ch 11

SAE 3400: duties vs acceptance factors vs procedure matters vs presentation checks Asked: Jan 2026 Q6(c); May 2026 Q6(a); MTP Nov 2026

Duties (2.4) — evidence whetherAcceptance (2.6) — considerNTE of examination procedures (2.7) — mattersPresentation and disclosure (2.8) — whether
(a) Best-estimate assumptions not unreasonable; hypothetical consistent with purpose. (b) PFI properly prepared on the assumptions. (c) Properly presented; all material assumptions disclosed, saying best-estimate or hypothetical. (d) Consistent basis with historical FS, appropriate accounting principles.Intended use · general or limited distribution · nature of assumptions · elements included · period covered. Do not accept / withdraw if assumptions clearly unrealistic or PFI inappropriate for intended use. Consider reliance on historical FI. Engagement letter.(a) knowledge from previous engagements (b) management's competence (c) likelihood of material misstatement (d) extent PFI affected by management judgment (e) sources, adequacy and reliability of data incl. third-party industry statistics (f) stability of business (g) team's experience with business, industry and PFI reporting(a) informative, not misleading (b) policies disclosed (c) assumptions disclosed, best-estimate vs hypothetical clear; uncertainty and sensitivity disclosed (d) date of preparation disclosed; management confirms assumptions as of that date (e) range basis not biased (f) change in policy from latest FS — reason and effect disclosed
Trigger: "duties / responsibilities while examining""before accepting", "should he decline""nature, timing and extent", "what will you consider in determining procedures""disclosure", "notes", "range", "change in depreciation policy"
  • Procedures: assess source and reliability of evidence for best-estimate assumptions (budgets, debt agreements, industry publications). Hypothetical: all significant implications considered — sales beyond plant capacity need investment in capacity or subcontracting cost.

Examiner's twist: MTP case: zero cost escalation against 6–8% industry inflation, and revenue from two contracts not yet bid. The story invites "give an adverse opinion". The ask was duties → write (a)–(d), then apply: zero escalation is a best-estimate assumption that appears unreasonable against industry data; unbid contracts are hypothetical and must be disclosed as such and be consistent with purpose. Put the opinion consequence in one closing line only.

2.9 SAE 3400 report and modifications

  • Elements (a)–(m): title · addressee · identification of PFI · reference to SAs applicable to examination of PFI · management responsible incl. assumptions · purpose/restricted distribution where applicable · examination on a test basis of evidence for assumptions, amounts, disclosures · negative assurance on whether assumptions provide a reasonable basis · opinion on whether properly prepared on the assumptions and presented per framework · caveats on achievability · date (= date procedures completed) · place · signature.
  • Range → no assurance actual results fall within range. Projection → intended use and caution against other use.
Problem foundReport
Presentation and disclosure not adequate (e.g. sensitive assumptions' consequences not disclosed)Qualified or adverse, or withdraw
One or more significant assumptions do not provide a reasonable basisAdverse with reasons, or withdraw
Conditions preclude necessary proceduresDisclaimer describing scope limitation, or withdraw
  • SM TYU 1: depreciation on proposed assets under Income-tax Act, not disclosed → not consistent with historical FS (Companies Act basis) → state it; disclosure inadequate → qualified/adverse or withdraw.
  • SM case answers: assumptions listed by management; projection report must state intended use and caution against other purposes; accuracy is not vouched; UDIN mandatory.
  • Documentation: matters important to support the report and evidence of compliance with SAE.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · PFI and Other Assurance ServicesSM Ch 11

3 SAE 3402 — controls at a service organisation Asked: RTP May 2024

  • Report for user entities and their auditors on controls at a service organisation relevant to user entities' IC over financial reporting. Complements SA 402 (such reports give evidence under SA 402).
  • Applies only when the SO is responsible for, or able to assert about, suitable design. Not for: (a) reporting only on whether controls operated as described; (b) controls not relevant to financial reporting (production, quality control).
  • Controls at SO include aspects of user entities' information systems maintained by SO, control environment, monitoring, control activities; exclude controls over the SO's own FS. Operations/compliance controls may be relevant (payroll: late remittance → interest and penalties).
  • User auditor audits the user entity. Service auditor reports at the SO's request.
  • Objectives: reasonable assurance that description fairly presents the system; controls suitably designed; where in scope, operated effectively — throughout the period (type 1: as at a date); report accordingly.
PointType 1Type 2Trigger words
CoversDescription + designDescription + design + operating effectiveness"operating effectiveness", "throughout the year" → Type 2
TimeAs at a specified dateThroughout a specified period"as on 31 March" → Type 1
Extra in reportStatement: no procedures on operating effectiveness, no opinion thereonSeparate section/attachment: tests of controls and results; sample size; number and nature of deviations — reported even if objective achieved; risk of projecting to future periods"deviations found but objective met" → still report
  • Steps: ethics incl. independence; identify persons to interact; acceptance (capability; criteria suitable and available; scope not so limited as to be useless; scope change needs reasonable justification); suitability of criteria; materiality; understand system; evidence on description, design, (type 2) operating effectiveness; internal audit function; written reps; subsequent events up to report date.
  • Written reps: reaffirm assertion; all information and access; disclosed NOCLAR, fraud, uncorrected deviations; design deficiencies; controls not operated as described; subsequent events.
  • Report includes: CUEC statement (not evaluated; objectives achieved only with user controls); subservice organisation activities; intended only for user entities and their auditors; SO responsibilities (description, services, control objectives, design and implementation); opinion in positive form; date no earlier than sufficient evidence; place ordinarily the city.
  • Modified opinion if: description not fairly presented; controls not suitably designed; (type 2) controls did not operate effectively; or unable to obtain sufficient evidence — with clear description of all reasons.

4 SAE 3420 — pro forma FI in a prospectus Asked: May 2025 Q1(c)

  • Reasonable assurance on the responsible party's compilation; applies where required by securities law/regulation or generally accepted practice.
  • Pro forma FI = FI with adjustments to illustrate impact of an event/transaction on unadjusted FI as if it occurred at an earlier date. Solely illustrative; not actual position/performance/cash flows.
  • Compilation steps: identify source and extract unadjusted FI; make pro forma adjustments; present with disclosures. Practitioner has no responsibility to compile.
  • Acceptance: capability; criteria suitable and PFI unlikely misleading; evaluate prescribed opinion wording; modified opinion/EOM on source → does law permit use/reference; entity or acquiree FI never audited/reviewed → can sufficient understanding be obtained; responsible party agrees responsibility (disclose criteria if not public; compile; provide access incl. acquiree and advisors).
  • Planning/performing: assess criteria; materiality; understanding; evidence on appropriateness of the source (no audit/review report → procedures to be satisfied); appropriate extraction; evidence on pro forma adjustments: (a) illustrating impact as if at earlier date; (b) making it consistent with the reporting entity's framework and policies; evaluate presentation; read other prospectus information.
  • Written reps: all appropriate adjustments identified; compiled per criteria. Opinion phrases (equivalent): "compiled, in all material respects, on the basis of the applicable criteria" / "properly compiled on the basis stated". Law precludes a prospectus with modified opinion → discuss; no change → withdraw or seek legal advice. EOM permitted.
  • Report: not responsible for updating reports on historical FI; no audit or review of FI used; no assurance that actual outcome would be as presented.

Examiner's twist: May 2025: unaudited subsidiary FS as source, fair value adjustments, IFRS with local modifications. Ask (i) was the source evaluation, (ii) was what pro forma adjustments include. Unaudited source → "perform procedures to be satisfied the source is appropriate"; IFRS modifications → adjustment type (b), aligning to the reporting entity's framework and policies.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Related ServicesSM Ch 9

09Ch 9 — Related Services (SRS 4400, SRS 4410)

Asked: May 2026 Q1(c) SRS 4410 documentation (4) · May 2025 Q3(c) compilation independence and management responsibilities (4) · Nov 2024 Q6(b) AUP matters to agree and engagement letter (5) · May 2024 Q4(b) compilation vs assurance, SQC 1 (5)

1 Related services

  • Assurance engagement: practitioner expresses a conclusion to enhance confidence of intended users other than the responsible party about the evaluation/measurement of a subject matter against criteria.
  • Not assurance: tax return preparation (no conclusion), consulting/advisory, and SRS engagements (AUP and compilation). Person doing related services need not be the auditor.

Audit vs review vs AUP vs compilation — the master look-alike

PointAudit (SAs)Review (SRE 2400/2410)AUP (SRS 4400)Compilation (SRS 4410)
AssuranceReasonableLimited (2410: risk reduced to moderate level)None — users draw own conclusionsNone
IndependenceRequired (SA 200 ethics incl. independence)Required (SRE 2400: ethics incl. independence)Not required, unless terms/objective require; if not independent → state in reportNot applicable; law may specify requirements or disclosure
WorkTests of controls, substantive proceduresPrimarily inquiry and APProcedures of an audit nature as agreed: inquiry and analysis, recomputation/comparison/clerical checks, observation, inspection, confirmationsApply accounting and reporting expertise to assist management; no verification of accuracy or completeness
OutputOpinion (positive)Conclusion (negative)Report of factual findingsCompilation report — not a vehicle for any opinion or conclusion
DistributionGeneralGeneralUsually restricted to parties who agreed proceduresAs agreed in terms (intended use and distribution)
Trigger words"true and fair""nothing has come to our attention""specified procedures", "factual findings", "confirm balances above ₹X""assist in preparing FS from trial balance"

2 SRS 4400 Agreed-upon procedures Asked: Nov 2024 Q6(b)

  • Objective: carry out procedures of an audit nature agreed by auditor, entity and any appropriate third parties, and report factual findings. No assurance; non-assurance engagement.
  • May cover individual items (payables, receivables, related-party purchases, segment sales/profits), a balance sheet or even a complete set of FS.
  • Ethics: Code of Ethics — integrity, objectivity, professional competence and due care, confidentiality, professional conduct, technical standards. Independence as in table.
  • Matters to agree (with entity and ordinarily other specified parties): (a) nature — not an audit or review, no assurance; (b) stated purpose; (c) financial information to which procedures apply; (d) nature, timing and extent of specific procedures; (e) limitations on distribution — if in conflict with law, do not accept.
  • Engagement letter includes: listing of procedures agreed; statement that distribution is restricted to specified parties who agreed the procedures.
  • Plan the work; carry out agreed procedures; use evidence as basis for report.

Examiner's twist: Nov 2024 had two asks in one question: "matters to be agreed" (a)–(e) and "matters in the engagement letter" (two bullets). Students merge them and lose half. Note the addressee: procedures are on RS Ltd's figures, but the report goes to the appointing authority (ATP Ltd), and distribution stays restricted to parties who agreed the procedures.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Related ServicesSM Ch 9

2.4 AUP report of factual findings (a)–(o)

  • Title · addressee (ordinarily appointing authority) · identification of specific financial or non-financial information · procedures were those agreed with the recipient · performed per SRS for AUP · purpose · listing of specific procedures · factual findings with sufficient detail of errors and exceptions · not an audit or review, no assurance · had additional procedures, an audit or review been performed, other matters might have come to light · restricted to parties who agreed · (when applicable) relates only to elements specified, not FS as a whole · date · place · signature.
  • The four bold "scope" statements are the answer to "precautions so that readers do not misunderstand scope" (TYK 7, CA Kamna).
  • Documentation: matters important to support the report of factual findings and evidence of compliance with SRS and terms.

3 SRS 4410 Compilation engagements Asked: May 2024 Q4(b); May 2025 Q3(c); May 2026 Q1(c)

  • Assist management with preparation and presentation of historical FI without obtaining assurance, and report. Non-historical or non-financial compilations → may use SRS 4410 with adaptation. SQC 1 applies (SRS 4410 is an Engagement Standard).
  • Not assurance; no need to verify accuracy or completeness, or gather evidence for an opinion/conclusion.
  • Purposes: mandatory periodic reporting under law; or others — internal use by management/TCWG; external parties under contract (e.g. funding body for a grant); transactional (merger, acquisition, change in ownership/financing).
  • Objectives: apply accounting and financial reporting expertise to assist management per AFRF based on management's information; report per SRS.
  • Management retains responsibility incl. judgments, policies and estimates (may have prepared a draft). Framework ranges from entity-specific basis to established standards.
  • Ethics: comply; identify and address threats. Independence not applicable; law may specify.
Agreed terms (record in engagement letter before performing)Management responsibilities to be agreed May 2025 Q3(c)
(a) intended use and distribution, restrictions; (b) applicable framework; (c) objective and scope; (d) practitioner's responsibilities incl. ethics; (e) management responsibilities; (f) expected form and content of report. Recurring → evaluate revision, remind.(i) the FI and its preparation and presentation per a framework acceptable for intended use and users; (ii) design, implementation and maintenance of IC; (iii) accuracy and completeness of records, documents, explanations and information provided; (iv) judgments needed, incl. those where practitioner assists
  • Performing: understand business, operations, accounting system and records, and framework incl. industry application; compile using management's information; discuss significant judgments assisted; read compiled FI before completion.
  • Information incomplete/inaccurate → bring to management's attention, request corrected information. Management fails to provide → withdraw and inform management and TCWG of reasons.
  • Compiled FI (a) does not adequately describe framework, (b) needs amendments to avoid material misstatement, or (c) otherwise misleading → propose amendments; declined → withdraw and inform; withdrawal not possible → determine professional and legal responsibilities.
  • Obtain acknowledgement that management/TCWG took responsibility for the final version. Communicate significant matters on a timely basis.

Examiner's twist: SM case: a team member wants to verify revenue completeness "so FS are not misleading". The answer is that verification is not required — but an omission you notice (Rourkela expenditure) cannot be ignored: disregarding it breaches integrity. "Not required to look" is different from "may ignore what you saw". Kings & Co (TYK 8): partners say inventory understatement is "outside scope" → propose amendment; refused → withdraw.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Related ServicesSM Ch 9

3.7 Compilation report (a)–(m)

  • Title · addressee per terms · practitioner compiled based on information provided by management · responsibilities of management/TCWG · framework (special purpose → description or reference) · identification of FI (each element title, date or period) · practitioner's responsibilities incl. performed per SRS and complied with ethical requirements · what a compilation entails · explanations: not assurance → not required to verify accuracy/completeness; accordingly no audit opinion or review conclusion · special purpose framework → purpose/intended users and alert that it may not be suitable for other purposes · date · signature · place.

3.8–3.9 Quality control and documentation Asked: May 2026 Q1(c)

  • Engagement partner responsible for (a) overall quality of each compilation engagement; (b) performance per firm's QC policies and procedures.
  • Documentation: (a) significant matters and how addressed; (b) record of how compiled FI reconciles with underlying records, documents, explanations and information from management; (c) copy of final version of compiled FI acknowledged by management/TCWG, and the practitioner's report. May also keep trial balance, summary of significant accounting records or other information used.

Documentation — look-alike across the group

StandardDistinctive documentation pointTrigger words
SRE 2400Experienced practitioner test; who performed and date; who reviewed, period and extent; discussions with management/TCWG; how inconsistencies were addressed"review", "who reviewed the work"
SRE 2410Sufficient and appropriate to support conclusion and show compliance with SRE and law"interim review"
SAE 3402Experienced service auditor test: NTE of procedures; results and evidence; significant matters, conclusions, judgments"service auditor"
SRS 4400Evidence supporting the report of factual findings and compliance with SRS and terms"factual findings"
