AAA · Twist notes · Committees, scope, elementsSM Ch 15

Audit Boards and Financial Committees (p.15.6–15.8) Asked: May 2026 MCQ Q15

  • Audit Boards: unique feature of IAAD for comprehensive appraisals of PSEs; associate experts; discuss findings with managements and controlling ministries before finalisation; results go into C&AG's reports; no separate legal entity; work under C&AG's supervision and control.
  • Audit Reports and accounts automatically stand referred to PAC, Estimates Committee and COPU.
CommitteeDuty (SM)Trigger words → this committee
PACMoneys disbursed legally on the purpose applied; expenditure authorised; re-appropriation per provisions; accounts of autonomous/semi-autonomous bodies audited by C&AG"authorised", "re-appropriation", "government departments"
Estimates CommitteeEconomies, improvements, efficiency, administrative reform consistent with policy; suggest alternative policies; money well laid out; form of estimates. Does not comment on a policy approved by Parliament, but where a policy is not leading to desired results or leads to waste, brings it to the House's notice"policy not giving results", "alternative policy", "estimates"
COPUSame financial control over PSUs as PAC over departments; examine reports and accounts of PSUs; examine C&AG reports on PSUs; autonomy and efficiency, sound business principles, prudent commercial practices; other PAC/Estimates functions allotted by Speaker. Takes the form of a comprehensive appraisal"public undertaking", "physical and financial performance", "action yet to be taken by PSU"
  • C&AG = "friend, philosopher and guide" of the committees: his reports form the basis (committees not precluded from other issues); scrutinises Ministry notes; committees report to Parliament/Legislature; Ministries report action taken → Action Taken Reports; reports not discussed → written answers obtained.

3. Objective and scope; elements; principles (p.15.9–15.13)

  • DPC Act specifies entities; scope and extent determined by the C&AG itself. Not confined to financial and compliance — extends to performance (efficiency, economy, effectiveness); propriety; comprehensive audit (does not re-cover the field already covered; appraisal/efficiency-cum-performance); decisions by competent authority after all aspects, information and alternatives; helping Government; physical with financial performance; fiscal accountability (funds, sanctions, compliance, propriety) and managerial accountability (efficiency, economy, effectiveness = efficiency-cum-performance audit).
  • Elements: three parties — auditor (SAI India = C&AG + IAAD; State heads called Accountants General), responsible party, intended users · subject matter, criteria, subject matter information · engagement type.
  • Principles: General — ethics and independence; professional judgement, due care, scepticism; quality control; audit team management and skill; audit risk; materiality; documentation; communication. Process — planning, conducting, reporting and follow-up.
Look-alikeAttestation engagementDirect reporting engagement
Who measures subject matter against criteriaResponsible party; auditor gathers evidence for a conclusionAuditor
Which auditsFinancial audits — alwaysPerformance and compliance audits — generally

4–5. Financial and compliance audit (p.15.13–15.15) Asked: Sep 2025 Q2(c)

  • Financial audit: opinion on FS per applicable framework (fair presentation → "true and fair"); enhance confidence of users.
  • Compliance audit: independent assessment of whether a subject matter complies with applicable criteria — activities, financial transactions and information comply in all material respects with rules governing the entity. Concerned with (a) Regularity — formal criteria from laws, regulations, agreements; (b) Propriety — general principles of sound financial management and ethical conduct of public officials. Regularity emphasised; propriety equally pertinent in public sector.
  • Perspective: conducted (i) with audit of FS, (ii) separately as individual compliance audits, or (iii) in combination with performance auditing. Within performance audit, compliance is one aspect of economy, efficiency, effectiveness; non-compliance may be a cause, explanation or consequence.
  • Process stages: general principles and annual plan → planning → performing and gathering evidence → evaluating and concluding → reporting (include entity's responses; follow-up previous reports); documentation, communication, QC throughout.

Examiner's twist: State law defines "flat" by dwelling units and building area; database lacks area column; physical verification shows misclassification → water charges under-collected (TYK 9). Ask: type and to whom reported → compliance audit; under Article 151, State report goes to the Governor, laid before State Legislature. Do not say "President".

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Performance, comprehensive, propriety auditSM Ch 15

6. Performance audit (p.15.16–15.26) Asked: May 2026 Q6(c) OR

  • Definition: objective and systematic examination of evidence for an independent assessment of the performance of a government organisation, programme, activity or function, to improve public accountability and facilitate decision-making. Conducted by C&AG through subordinate offices of IAAD, guided by C&AG's manual and standards. Examines whether (a) decisions by legislature/executive are efficiently and effectively prepared and implemented; (b) taxpayers/citizens got value for money.
  • Objectives (6.1): economy, efficiency, effectiveness of policy, programmes, organisation, management; accountability; transparency. Responsibility for measurable objectives, performance indicators and measurement systems rests with Government departments/Heads of entities (C&AG's Regulations on Audit and Accounts).
ESM meaningSM question examplesTrigger words
EconomyMinimising cost of resources, with appropriate quantity, quality, best price; acquired in due timeBest price for consultancy? cost of sickness absence? lowest transport cost of food aid? waste of resources?"best price", "acquired in due time", "cost of inputs"
EfficiencyInput-output ratio; output maximised at minimum input; focus on processes converting inputs to outputs. Aspects: sound procurement; resources protected and maintained; resources efficiently used; optimum resources for quantity/quality in time; programmes efficiently managed; efficient procedures; objectives met cost-effectivelyCost per job created vs elsewhere; could project X be done with better timeliness/quality; prioritising transport projects; schools using IT equipment"per unit cost", "timeliness", "process", "input-output"
EffectivenessExtent objectives achieved; intended vs actual impact. Includes: objectives and means proper; extent of results; whether impacts are due to policy or other causes; inhibiting factors; overlap with other programmes; alternatives considered; management control system for measuring effectiveness; compliance with laws; ways to work more effectivelyDid infrastructure raise traffic flow, safety, cut journey time? environmental measures? objectives for all sectors of community?"intended impact", "objectives achieved", "due to the policy or other causes"
  • Planning (A)–(I): understand entity/programme (sources: entity documents, legislative documents, policy documents, academic/special research, past audits, media, special focus groups) → objectives and scope → audit criteria (sources: procedure manuals; policies, standards, directives, guidelines; criteria of same/other entities; independent expert opinion; scientific knowledge; management literature and research papers) → audit approach (analysis of procedures, case studies, existing data, surveys, analysis of results, quantitative analysis with sampling) → audit questions (hierarchy) → team skills and outside expertise (Accountant General retains full responsibility even when an expert is used) → Audit Design Matrix (columns: objective, questions, criteria, evidence, data collection and analysis method; one per objective) → time table and resources (AG liable for timely completion; variations approved) → intimation to auditee (scope, team, schedule; acknowledgement on record; changes in objectives recorded and approved).
  • Audit criteria = standards to judge whether a programme meets or exceeds expectations; reasonable and attainable standards of performance.
  • Discom illustration → performance audit. Observations: defective meters much above permitted 1%, not replaced within 1 month → average billing; meter rent ₹30 (single phase)/₹40 (three phase) per month — short charged ₹60 lakh from 3 lakh consumers in 5 lakh bills.

7. Comprehensive audit (p.15.26–15.28)

  • C&AG assists legislature; efficiency-cum-performance audit other than the field already covered by internal audit or professional auditors; locates weakness and extravagance. Combines financial, compliance and performance aspects → holistic assessment. Areas: investment decisions, project formulation, organisational effectiveness, capacity utilisation, production, materials, labour productivity, idle capacity, costs and prices, sales and credit control, budgetary and internal control.
  • Issues (a)–(j): capital cost vs approved; planned outputs; planned rate of return; project formulation and gestation; cost control and wastage; purchase policies and inventory pile-up; R&D; repairs and maintenance; procedures effective and economical; poor project planning. Criteria: Bureau of Public Enterprises guidelines, industrial engineering studies, standards of financial propriety. Starting point: audit programme from study of decisions on setting up, objectives, annual reports, budgets, board deliberations, earlier inspection reports.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Propriety audit, C&AG report, look-alikesSM Ch 15

8. Propriety audit (p.15.28–15.31) Asked: Jan 2026 Q3(a)

  • Verification of transactions on tests of public interest, commonly accepted customs and standards of conduct. Kohler: "that which meets the tests of public interest, commonly accepted customs, and standards of conduct, and particularly as applied to professional performance, requirements of law, Government regulations and professional codes". Tests boil down to economy, efficiency and faithfulness; emphasis on substance, financial prudence, public interest, prevention of waste.
  • Brings out improper, avoidable or infructuous expenditure even though incurred in conformity with rules. Examples: building for a telephone exchange not used for it (infructuous); school building used five years after completion (avoidable). Every officer to show the vigilance of a person of ordinary prudence spending his own money.
  • Principles (Audit Code): (i) expenditure not prima facie more than the occasion demands; ordinary-prudence vigilance; (ii) sanctioning authority does not pass an order that directly or indirectly accrues to its own advantage; (iii) funds not used for benefit of a particular person or group; (iv) apart from agreed remuneration, no other avenue to indirectly benefit management personnel, employees and others.
  • Executive departments enforce economy; audit brings waste to notice. Auditor's functions: expenditure properly planned; size and channels rightful for maximum results; likely optimum result; any substitute plan would improve operations/return on capital; management actions conducive to public interest and standards of conduct.
  • Issues: "propriety" is a moral term — hard to frame verifiable propositions; subjectivity → C&AG developed norms of propriety for public expenditure; these do not ipso facto apply to private sector; management of each entity may frame its own norms. Air travel not wasteful unless rail was feasible with the same results. Judge decisions with reference to circumstances at the time, not hindsight; auditor must reconstruct circumstances; otherwise initiative is dampened.

9. C&AG audit report on PSUs (p.15.32)

Parts: (a) Introduction — general review of working results of government companies, deemed government companies, corporations; (b) results of comprehensive appraisals by the Audit Board; (c) resume of company auditors' reports under C&AG directions and comments on accounts; (d) significant results of audit of undertakings not taken up by the Audit Board. Certain States: separate commercial report; others: commercial chapter in main report.

BasisCompliancePerformanceProprietyComprehensive
Question it asksComplied with laws, rules, agreements (regularity) and sound financial management (propriety)?Economy, efficiency, effectiveness; value for moneyIs the expenditure wise, faithful, economical in public interest — even if within rules?Have objectives, targets, rate of return been achieved — holistic appraisal
Engagement typeGenerally direct reportingGenerally direct reportingPart of C&AG audits, combined with regularity auditCombines financial, compliance and performance
Who in SMC&AG offices; test audit exampleIAAD offices; AG responsibleC&AG norms of proprietyC&AG; Audit Boards
Trigger words"rules", "notification", "classification under law""cost per unit", "objectives achieved", "programme""public interest", "customs", "avoidable", "infructuous", "own advantage""cost overrun", "capacity", "project delay", "rate of return"

Examiner's twist: C&AG appoints a CA firm for a "performance audit" and the firm only checks that expenses conform to public interest and accepted customs; C&AG rejects the report (TYK 7). The trap is to defend the firm. Score: what the firm did is propriety, not performance audit; performance audit evaluates economy, efficiency, effectiveness → rejection is in order. Solar project with delay and cost overrun (TYK 3) → write the comprehensive audit issues list, not performance-audit planning.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Group auditsSM Ch 13

13Ch 13 — Group Audits

Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-I Q3 item NOT a current-period adjustment (goodwill/capital reserve) · Sep 2025 Q3(a) Other Matter paragraph for subsidiaries audited by other auditors (5m) · Sep 2025 Q1(c) CARO reporting in CFS (4m) · Jan 2026 MCQ CS-II Q11 notes not needed in CFS · May 2026 MCQ Q13 unrealised profit, deferred tax, subsequent event. Also MTP May 2026 OMP drafting; May 2024 5(b) component treatment under Ind AS.

1. CFS and the law (p.13.3–13.7)

  • CFS = parent and subsidiaries presented as a single economic entity. Components of CFS: consolidated balance sheet, statement of profit and loss, cash flow statement, statement of changes in equity (if applicable), explanatory notes. Same format as parent's separate FS as far as possible; Schedule III formats. ICAI Guidance Note on Audit of CFS gives audit procedures; does not deal with accounting matters. AS 21 / Ind AS 110 give accounting principles.
  • s.129(3): company with one or more subsidiaries, including associate company and joint venture, prepares CFS of company and all subsidiaries in the same form and manner as its own; attach salient features in Form AOC-1. s.129(4): provisions on preparation, adoption and audit of holding company's FS apply mutatis mutandis to CFS. CFS approved by Board along with standalone and laid before AGM with standalone. Companies (Accounts) Rules, 2014: per Schedule III and applicable AS; if not required to prepare CFS under AS, enough to comply with Schedule III CFS provisions.
  • Exemption (all three): (i) wholly-owned subsidiary, or partially-owned whose other members (even non-voting), intimated in writing with proof of delivery, do not object; (ii) securities not listed or in process of listing on any stock exchange, in India or outside India; (iii) ultimate or any intermediate holding company files CFS with Registrar complying with AS. s.129(6): Central Government may exempt any class of companies by notification in public interest.
  • Investment entity (Ind AS 110 para 31) need not present CFS if it must measure all subsidiaries at FVTPL. Definition: (a) obtains funds from investors to provide investment management services; (b) commits that its business purpose is investing solely for capital appreciation, investment income or both; (c) measures and evaluates substantially all investments on fair value. Para 33: parent of an investment entity consolidates all entities it controls, unless the parent itself is an investment entity.
  • Ind AS 110: no exemption for temporary control or severe long-term restrictions on fund transfer; para 20 — consolidation begins when control is obtained and ceases when lost (51% bought, 25% sold in same year → consolidate till date of disposal; TYK 8).

2–3. Responsibility of parent vs auditor (p.13.7–13.9)

Parent's management (a)–(g)Auditor of CFS — objectives (a)–(e)
Identify components and include their information · reportable segments · related parties and transactions · accurate and complete information from components · consolidation adjustments · harmonise policies and framework · GAAP conversion. Also issues instructions to components (policies, disclosures, segments, related parties, reporting timetable).Satisfy that CFS follow the framework · opinion on true and fair view · enquire into matters in s.143(1) · report on s.143(3)(a)–(i) and, under s.143(3)(j) with rule 11, sub-rules (a)–(g) as applicable · validate the requirement to prepare CFS. Component matters are generally reported on the basis of component auditors' reports.
  • SAs, Statements and Guidance Notes apply to CFS as to standalone: plan; understand accounting and internal control systems including IT such as consolidation tool; assess risk.
  • CARO 2020 (SM Ch 7 p.7.71–7.73): Order does not apply to report on CFS except clause 3(xxi) — qualifications/adverse remarks in CARO reports of companies included in CFS: give details of the companies and paragraph numbers of those CARO reports.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Auditing the consolidationSM Ch 13

4. Audit considerations; materiality; other auditors (p.13.9–13.12)

  • CFS built from separate FS plus "other financial information" (component disclosures, proportion of items under different policies, effects of significant events between component and parent FS dates) — must be additionally disclosed.
  • On acceptance, assess whether his work alone supports an opinion; else consider using other auditors (component statutory auditors or auditors appointed to assist). Work done by component statutory auditors for the principal auditor is a separate assignment. SA 600 "Using the Work of Another Auditor" (para 2) applies when principal auditor uses another auditor's work.
  • Principal auditor acceptance factors (TYK 10 answer): materiality of portion he audits; his knowledge of components' business; risk of material misstatement in components audited by others; additional procedures giving him significant participation.
  • Materiality in CFS: compute for the group as a whole — used to assess consolidation adjustments; group materiality used to decide whether a component is material and whether to scope in components; compute component materiality and communicate to component auditor if needed; obtain confirmations from component auditors (independence, ethics, consolidation and disclosure information). Standalone materiality stays component-wise.
  • ICAI announcement 25 May 2017 amended para 17 of the Guidance Note: in considering component auditors' modifications/EOM/OM (SA 705/706), comply with SA 600 → materiality is considered.

Not in the SM text for this chapter: the SM Ch 13 uses SA 600 "Using the Work of Another Auditor" (principal auditor / other auditor). It does not define "significant component" or its types. Answer with principal-auditor terms and the materiality bullets above.

5. Auditing the consolidation (p.13.12–13.16)

  • Plan for: group structure and group-wide controls incl. IT for consolidation; policies of parent and components, translation of foreign components; nature, timing, extent based on risk in consolidation; extent of other auditors' work; coordination.
  • Completeness of components (TYU 1): prior-year working papers; parent's identification procedures; inquiries for new or outgoing components; investments of parent and components; JVs and joint arrangements; other arrangements not consolidated; statutory registers under ss.186, 190; changes in shareholding. Document these procedures.
  • Control evidence: Board minutes, shareholder agreements, technology/know-how agreements, statute; post year-end minutes (liquidation or further investment → was control temporary?).
  • Exclusion: examine reasons and conformity. Under Companies (AS) Rules, 2006: control intended to be temporary (intention to dispose existed at acquisition), or severe long-term restrictions on transfer of funds. Reasons must be disclosed; other reasons → consider effect on report. Check changes in status (subsidiary to associate, JV to associate). Watch off-balance sheet entities and de facto control (less than majority voting rights but practical ability to direct relevant activities unilaterally).
Component (May 2024 5(b))Ind AS (Companies (Ind AS) Rules, 2015)AS (Companies (AS) Rules, 2006)
SubsidiaryLine-by-line (Ind AS 110); goodwill/capital reserve (gain on bargain purchase) and NCI per Ind AS 103Line-by-line; goodwill/capital reserve, minority interest per AS 21
AssociateEquity method (Ind AS 28)Equity method (AS 23)
Joint venture / jointly controlled entityEquity method (Ind AS 28)Proportionate consolidation (AS 27)
Joint operationInterest in assets, liabilities, revenues, expenses in separate FS of the entity (Ind AS 111)—
Entities under common control (business combination)Pooling of interest method (Ind AS 103)—
Step acquisitionRemeasure previously held interest at acquisition-date fair value; gain/loss in P&L or OCI (Ind AS 103)—

AAA · Twist notes · Adjustments, disclosures, reportingSM Ch 13

6. Permanent vs current period consolidation adjustments (p.13.16–13.21) Asked: Sep 2025 MCQ CS-I Q3; May 2026 MCQ Q13

BasisPermanentCurrent period
When madeOnly on the first occasion, or later when shareholding changesIn the period for which CFS are prepared
Items(a) Goodwill or capital reserve; (b) equity attributable to minority/NCI. Auditor: pre-acquisition reserves (dates of investment), allocation between parent and NCI, changes on later acquisition/disposal; netting goodwill and capital reserve → gross amounts in notes. Example: holding cut from 70% to 55%Intra-group interest, management fees; unrealised intra-group profits; deferred tax on unrealised profit elimination (Ind AS 12); intra-group indebtedness; harmonising policies; recognised subsequent events till date of auditor's report on CFS; significant events where component FS dates differ; GAAP conversion of foreign component; movement in NCI since acquisition (NCI may be negative under Ind AS); deferred tax on temporary differences from eliminations and undistributed profits
Trigger words"on acquisition", "change in holding", "pre-acquisition reserves""intra-group", "unrealised", "this year", "conversion adjustments"
  • Adjustments are in memorandum records of the parent — auditor reviews them and also verifies: eliminations; uniform policies (AS: if impracticable, disclose fact and proportion of items; Ind AS 110: adjust to group policies); GAAP conversion; NCI; deferred tax (Ind AS); income/expenses from date control gained till ceased, based on acquisition-date amounts (Ind AS).
  • Goodwill on consolidation: carried at acquisition value, tested for impairment at every balance sheet date; foreign-currency goodwill impairment → check currency translation reserve; intragroup losses may indicate impairment. NCI share of losses exceeding NCI equity → per relevant standard.
  • Reporting dates: same as parent; if impracticable, adjust for significant events; difference not more than six months (AS) and three months (Ind AS). Consistency: length of periods and year-end differences same period to period; disclose changes.
  • Subsequent events: adjusting vs non-adjusting; non-adjusting significant → disclose nature and estimate of financial effect (or that it cannot be made) — in report of approving authority (AS) / in FS (Ind AS).

Disclosures (p.13.21–13.23) Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-II Q11

  • MCA Circular 39/2014 dated 14 October 2014: CFS should not merely repeat separate-FS disclosures; give only disclosures relevant to CFS (applies under Ind AS too, via s.129(4)).
  • Schedule III extra: for parent and each component (incl. foreign) — net assets and % of consolidated net assets; share in profit or loss and %; share in OCI and % (Ind AS). Consolidation adjustments shown as a single line or adjusted in each figure.
  • Need not be in CFS: source of bonus shares; unutilised issue money; MSMED Act disclosure; statement of investments with names of bodies corporate; CIF value of imports; foreign currency expenditure (royalty, know-how, fees, interest); imported vs indigenous consumption %; dividends remitted in foreign currency; foreign exchange earnings.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · CFS reporting situationsSM Ch 13

7–8. Representations and reporting (p.13.24–13.28) Asked: Sep 2025 Q3(a); MTP May 2026

  • SA 580: parent management acknowledges responsibility for true and fair CFS and has approved them; reps on matters material to CFS, e.g. completeness of components; reportable segments; related parties; appropriateness and completeness of permanent and current period adjustments incl. eliminations.
  • Report considering SA 700, SA 705, SA 706; plus SA 600 when other auditors' work used.
SituationWhat the CFS auditor does (SM)
Parent's auditor audits all componentsReport whether consolidation principles and procedures in AS were followed; departure → SA 705; opinion on state of affairs, results, and cash flows (if CFS includes cash flow statement)
Not auditor of all componentsConsider SA 600. If reference to other auditors is necessary (SA 706): disclose magnitude — aggregate rupee amounts or percentages of total assets, revenues and cash flows not audited by him, before permanent and current period consolidation adjustments. Reference is not a qualification — it shows divided responsibility. Placed in an Other Matter paragraph (TYU 3)
Component reports under a different accounting frameworkParent's management converts component's audited FS; conversion adjustments audited by the principal auditor. Alternative: component prepares FS on group accounting policies (group accounting manual); principal auditor checks those policies comply with parent's GAAP, then decides whether to rely and refer
Component audited under a different auditing frameworkComponents should be audited under a framework that corresponds to Indian GAAS, to allow reliance and reference
Components not auditedConsider possible modification (no sufficient appropriate evidence); evaluate qualitative and quantitative effect using SA 705

OMP skeleton (TYU 3 answer): "We did not audit the financial statements and other financial information of [n] subsidiaries, whose financial statements reflect total assets of ₹__ as at 31 March 20__, total revenues of ₹__ and net cash outflows of ₹__ for the year then ended. These have been audited by other auditors whose reports have been furnished to us by the Management … our opinion … in so far as it relates to these subsidiaries, and our report in terms of s.143(3) … is based solely on the reports of the other auditors. [Foreign ones:] prepared under GAAP of their countries, audited under auditing standards of their countries; Holding Company's management converted them to Indian GAAP; we have audited these conversion adjustments … Our opinion … is not modified in respect of the above matters."

Examiner's twist: The story lists CARO qualifications in a subsidiary's, a JCE's and the parent's own standalone reports (Sep 2025 1(c)) and asks how to report in the consolidated report. Only clause 3(xxi) applies: give details of the companies and the paragraph numbers of the CARO reports with qualifications/adverse remarks — do not re-report each clause. In an MCQ, "determination of goodwill" is a permanent adjustment even if the acquisition happened this year (it is the first occasion).

AAA · Twist notes · Internal auditSM Ch 16

16Ch 16 — Internal Audit

Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-II Q10 audit trail IT controls (SM section 9) · May 2026 Q5(b) SIA 390 follow-up and Action Taken Report to Audit Committee (5m). Also Nov 2024 2(b) SA 610 documentation of direct assistance; MTP Jan 2026 s.138 applicability; RTP May 2024 prior audit issues.

1. Meaning, section 138, who can be appointed (p.16.3–16.7) Asked: MTP Jan 2026 S-I 6(b)

  • ICAI constituted the Internal Audit Standards Board (earlier Committee on Internal Audit) as a non-standing board on 5 February 2004. Definition (Framework): "Internal Audit provides independent assurance on the effectiveness of internal controls and risk management processes to enhance governance and achieve organisational objectives." May include an advisory (consulting) role if independence is not compromised. Scope per SA 610: monitoring internal controls (three-way match), review of compliance with laws, examination of financial and operating information, risk management, review of operating activities, governance.
  • Internal auditor: CA or cost accountant (in practice or not), or such other professional as decided by the Board; may or may not be an employee; regarded as part of management, not merely an assistant; authority to investigate every activity. A non-CA retired IA&AS officer with law degree can be Chief Internal Auditor if the Board decides (TYU 1).
Class (s.138, rule 13)Paid-up capitalTurnoverLoans/borrowings from banks or PFIsDeposits
Listed companyEvery listed company — no threshold
Unlisted public company₹50 crore or more (preceding FY)₹200 crore or more (preceding FY)Exceeding ₹100 crore at any time in preceding FY₹25 crore or more at any time in preceding FY
Private companyNot a criterion₹200 crore or moreExceeding ₹100 crore at any timeNot a criterion
  • Any one criterion is enough. Existing company newly covered → comply within six months of commencement of applicability. Figures are of the preceding financial year (JKT Pvt Ltd: turnover ₹260 crore in preceding year → required, even though capital is ₹40 lakh).
  • Rule 13(2): Audit Committee or Board, in consultation with the internal auditor, formulates scope, functioning, periodicity and methodology. Others: those who appoint (owners, promoters, trustees) define objectives.
  • SIA 210 critical activities of the IA function: overall plan, scope, methodology; oversee assignments to closure of observations; staff and resources; external experts; communicate with stakeholders; quality evaluation and improvement programme.
  • Responsibilities re accounting function: ascertain adequacy of internal control by continuous examination of accounting procedures, receipts, disbursements; safeguard against misappropriation · operate independently of accounting staff · not perform executive functions (vested interest) · bring facts to notice of authorities; appraise policies · keep informed of events and policy changes · enjoy independent status always.

Examiner's twist: Private company with paid-up capital ₹70 crore and public deposits in the story — both are irrelevant for a private company; only turnover and borrowings count. Unlisted public company with capital ₹60 crore but turnover ₹100 crore → still required (capital ≥ ₹50 crore). Write the provision, then test each company separately.

AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · Scope, independence, engagement, reportSM Ch 16

2. Scope of internal audit review (p.16.8–16.12)

  • Plan so all business processes (financial and operational) are reviewed within a defined time cycle, balancing: risk in the process, value IA can add, effort, risk appetite, coverage of all auditable areas.
  • Seven review areas: (i) internal control system and procedures (minimise residual risk; consider limitations — cost-benefit, human error, collusion, abuse; controls in use throughout reliance period); (ii) custodianship and safeguarding of assets (existence, segregation, intangibles like credit control, EDP controls); (iii) compliance with policies, plans, procedures, regulations; (iv) relevance and reliability of information (reporting by exception; system controls for data integrity); (v) organisation structure (span of control, unity of command, overlap HQ vs units, segregation); (vi) utilisation of resources (standards and norms; under-utilised facilities, idle manpower); (vii) accomplishment of goals and objectives (quantified, budgets, flexibility).

3–4. Independence, integrity, objectivity; qualities (p.16.13–16.15)

  • Free from undue influence; independence in mind and in appearance; resist pressure on scope, conduct, reporting. Position and reporting line of Chief Internal Auditor establish independence. Honest, no conflicts, no undue personal benefit. Objective in gathering and evaluating evidence.
  • Do not review an activity previously his responsibility; listed company → report to Audit Committee, not CEO/CFO; flag conflicts immediately. Opening case: position outside functions audited (e.g. Finance); accept only limited operational role for a short duration.
  • Compliance (TYU 2): IA audits the compliance framework, does not manage it or engage directly with regulators — management's responsibility.
  • Reporting structure (TYK 6 answer): functional reporting to Audit Committee/Board; administrative reporting may be to CEO/MD if approved by Board and independence is not affected.
  • Qualities: expertise in control systems; accounting and finance; operations and technology; commerce, laws, taxation, cost, economics, quantitative methods, EDP; ERP and IT controls; management principles and dealing with people; confidentiality.

5–6. Engagement and reporting (p.16.16–16.21) Asked: May 2026 Q5(b); RTP May 2024

  • Five steps in sequence: knowledge of business → planning (SIA 310; scope approved by Audit Committee and Board; opening meeting) → gather information (directly from source) → audit checks (analytics per SIA 6, sampling per SIA 5, evidence per SIA 320, interim reports after review per SIA 350, documentation per SIA 330) → reporting (draft report; management action plan with responsibility and timelines; status of prior actions; final report and presentation to Audit Committee; SIA 360, SIA 370).
  • Actual or suspected fraud → bring immediately to management's attention.
  • SIA 370: two stages — assignment-level Internal Audit Report (to auditee, copies to management) and periodic (normally quarterly) comprehensive report by Chief Internal Auditor to Audit Committee. SIA 370 deals only with the first type. Key elements: objectives, scope, approach; statement of conduct per SIAs; executive summary of key observations; summary of corrective actions; nature of assurance, if any. No final report unless a written draft was shared with the auditee; issue within reasonable time; conclusions on all findings, acknowledge effective controls; no mandated format; assurance → SIA 380. Typical contents: scope, period, executive summary, critical findings, detailed findings with business impact and root cause, rating (High/Medium/Low per Audit Committee criteria), recommendations, management response with action plan and timelines. Documentation: draft and final reports cross-referenced; action plans counter-signed if appropriate.
  • SIA 390: Chief Internal Auditor continuously monitors closure of prior issues through a formal process pre-agreed with management and TCWG; implementation remains management's responsibility; output = Action Taken Report (ATR). ATR contents (TYK 10 answer): reference to previous report; agreed action and target date; status (Implemented / Not implemented); residual risk and rating for unimplemented actions; findings not implemented for long; critical findings needing immediate action. No action within reasonable time → draw management's attention; ascertain reasons; where accepted, review extent of implementation and report partly/not implemented items.
AAA LDR — Ss
AAA · Twist notes · SA 610, audit trail, look-alikesSM Ch 16

7. Internal vs external auditor; SA 610 (p.16.21–16.25) Asked: Nov 2024 2(b)

  • Statutory auditor must examine scope and effectiveness of internal audit (department, staff strength, qualification, powers); independence and status are key; evaluate IA function as relevant to nature, timing, extent of his procedures → may adopt less extensive procedures. Review of IA function in specified companies is now a statutory responsibility.
  • Do not use the work of the IA function if: organisational status and policies do not adequately support objectivity; function lacks sufficient competence; or does not apply a systematic and disciplined approach, including quality control.
  • Direct assistance: check that, in aggregate, the external auditor is still sufficiently involved given his sole responsibility. Not for procedures that: involve significant judgements; relate to higher assessed risks where judgement is more than limited; relate to work the IA function was involved in and reported to management/TCWG; relate to the auditor's decisions under SA 610. Significant judgements: assessing RMM; sufficiency of tests; going concern; significant accounting estimates; adequacy of disclosures. Not appropriate: discussion of fraud risks, determining unannounced procedures (SA 240); control over external confirmation requests and evaluating results (SA 505) — IA may assemble information to resolve exceptions.
  • Before direct assistance, written agreements: (a) from entity's authorised representative — IAs will follow external auditor's instructions and entity will not intervene; (b) from IAs — keep matters confidential and inform of threats to objectivity.
  • Documentation when direct assistance used: (a) evaluation of threats to objectivity and competence of IAs used; (b) basis for nature and extent of their work; (c) who reviewed, date and extent (SA 230); (d) written agreements; (e) working papers prepared by the IAs who provided direct assistance.
BasisInternal auditExternal audit
Performed byInternal function or external bodyIndependent body outside the organisation
ExaminesAdequacy of operational controlsAccuracy and validity of FS
Appointed byAudit Committee or BoardMembers
UsersTop management; referred by statutory auditorStakeholders
StatusCould be an employeeMandatorily not an employee

8–9. Management function; audit trail (p.16.26–16.27) Asked: Jan 2026 MCQ CS-II Q10

  • Management functions: planning, organising, staffing, directing, controlling; internal audit is an element of the controlling function (preserves stakeholders' wealth), while staying independent of operations.
  • Audit trail (edit log): visible trail tracing reported information back to original input; chronological record of creation, update, deletion. Records: when (timestamp), who (user ID), what (reference; success/failure). Enabled at software level or captured in the database. Controls to show it was functional and not disabled: not disabled/deactivated; unique user IDs, not shared; changes to audit-trail configuration authorised and logged; access to audit trail and backups disabled/restricted, access logs kept; periodic backups archived for the statutory period.
  • SIAs are recommendatory; mandatory from date notified by Council. Series: 100 key concepts (110 Nature of Assurance, 120 Internal Controls, 130 Risk Management, 140 Governance, 150 Compliance with Laws), 200 management (210, 220, 230, 240 Expert, 250 TCWG), 300–400 conduct (310, 320, 330, 350, 360, 370, 390), 500 specialised (520 IT environment, 530 Third Party Service Provider), older SIAs 5, 6, 7, 11 (Fraud), 18 (Related Parties).
Look-alikeUsing work of IA functionDirect assistance
GateObjectivity + competence + systematic, disciplined approach incl. QCSame evaluation of threats and competence + external auditor still sufficiently involved
BarGreater judgement/risk → do more himselfNo significant judgements; no fraud-risk discussion, unannounced procedures, confirmation control
PaperworkDocument evaluation and conclusionsWritten agreements (entity and IAs) + documentation (a)–(e) incl. IAs' working papers

Examiner's twist: Partner tells staff not to file working papers of internal auditors who gave direct assistance "as they are not part of statutory audit" (Nov 2024). Answer: wrong — SA 610 requires them in audit documentation; list (a)–(e). Another favourite: an assertion tested for the first time to add unpredictability must not be given to the internal auditor (TYK 14).
